at the dawn of night, crying "Gora, Gora!", they wake, immersed in that rasa.
All the elders, men and women — none can be compared with them:
with what care someone must have fashioned [their] nature and charming conduct!
Narahari offers himself in nirmanchhana to those elder kinsfolk of the Lord:
overflowing with love for Gora, they speak [to one another] in very sweet words.[^05-2]

*Someone says — vibhasha*

**5.2.** "Listen, O virtuous one! O wise one, quite in private I shall tell [you] a secret:
it seems the son of the lord of Vraja, [who was] in Vraja, has appeared here.
In the town of Nadia there is no one who does not pine for His qualities;
Srivasa, Murari and the rest — none of them can live without seeing [Him].
The ascetic Advaita, a resident of Shantipura, stays here constantly;
how sweet [His] qualities are — with what eagerness He tells [of them] to everyone!
Ah, how wondrous! Will there ever be another such marvellous boy?
[Says] Narahari: make Gorachanda, [your] all, the necklace about your neck!"[^05-3]

*And someone else — vibhasha*

**5.3.** "Listen, O virtuous one! Not in vain have I lived in Nadia:[^05-4]
in this old age the Creator is merciful [to me] — there is no end to [my] happiness.
To my fill, with these two eyes, I have beheld Nimai the moon, [son] of Shachi;
not seeing Him for half a moment, [my] heart weeps bitterly all the time.
Let me take all His misfortunes upon myself! I have never seen or heard of another like [Him].
Worshipping the gods with various rites, let us make vows for His welfare;
with great care let us give away wealth, and through astrologers perform worship of the planets.
Go with Narahari, perform auspicious rites in Shachi's house!"[^05-5]

*Raga yatharaga*

**5.4.** "Listen to my words: I do not know what will happen — I have grown utterly old.
Nimai is the treasure of our life, our all, the comfort of the heart.
O, I always long to see [Him]!
I have no strength to walk — that is [my] grief: the Creator has become [my] enemy. *(Refrain)*
Worship the gods, give gifts to the twice-born, always think of [His] welfare(?);
with care send various presents — whatever pleases Him.
Go with Narahari and teach Shachi the auspicious rites:
let her, adjuring [Him], break Nimai of His terrible obstinacy."[^05-6]

*Raga yatharaga*

**5.5.** [Their] bodies overflow with love for Gaura; the elders are joyful at the dawn of night.
Each told his own kinsfolk [about Him] in detail; hearing all this, [they] rejoice without end.
Unable to remain without seeing Gaura, [they] left their beds, summoned all their strength,
took sticks in hand — [their] limbs trembling. Narahari beholds [this] matchless play.[^05-7]

*Vibhasha*

**5.6.** At the dawn of night, leaving each his own house, the venerable old men
all came to the lovely courtyard of Shachi; how many, many delights arise in [their] minds!
They stand leaning on the sticks in their hands and again and again gaze at Gaura's body —
as if after many long days, with great effort, they had found a jewel [and pressed it] to their breast.
Someone, wholly overcome by affection, says to the Creator: "Fulfil all [my] wishes:
may His life be longer than mine; may the Beautiful One always remain in this town of Nadia!"
Someone, with folded hands, says to Vishnu: "Cast Your [gracious] glance on the son of Mishra:
may He be a stranger to no one, may all love [Him], may [people] ceaselessly praise [His] virtues!"
Someone says to the Lord of Kailasa: "Increase the radiance of each of [His] limbs;
may [His] fame fill the world, may there be prosperity; drive away the clouds of invincible misfortune!"
Someone says to Sarasvati: "Make [Him] a pandita, so that He may never know defeat!"
Someone says to Bhagavati: "May [all] ever behold Nimai — the life of Narahari!"[^05-8]

*Rasika-chhandah*

**5.7.** Thus all the elders, absorbed [in prema], give [Him] blessings again and again.
Who could count [these] many delights? What can Narahari say of [that] beauty?[^05-9]

*Raga yatharaga*

**5.8.** The old men gaze again and again at Gaura's face — abode of happiness, full of nectar;
deeming their many, many births to have borne fruit, they offer their bodies in nirmanchhana times without number.
Tears tremble and brim in their eyes; their bodies are adorned with abundant horripilation;
leaning his hand on another's hand, someone softly speaks sweet words:
"Tell me, who fashioned the son of Mishra? He has stolen my ears, mind and eyes!
Not seeing [Him] for a moment seems a kalpa; [it] makes me restless — not a drop of composure remains.
To be with Him always, to wander [with Him], is great happiness; for this everyone mocks [me], calling me mad.
When all those words reach [my] ears, my mind finds still greater delight.
Eating, walking, sleeping, speaking, the order [of things] — I remember nothing; everything has turned upside down.
The household routine seems sheer deceit(?); I have no love [even] for my own son."
Having spoken such words he falls silent, absorbed, and again in his heart cherishes various wishes.
[He] overflows with supreme affection — what can Narahari Dasa, crest-jewel of fools, say [of it]?[^05-10]

*Rupamala-chhandah*

**5.9.** Having drunk the nectar of the face of the moon Gaura, their bodies thrilling,
the elders became as if sixteen years old:
the sticks stay far away, strength abounds, their gait is drunk with joy.
Dasa Narahari, beholding [this] play, is filled with great bliss.[^05-11]

*Thus [ends] the fifth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Love of the Elders and Other Matters", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^05-12]

[^05-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon at the end of the kirana: "…pratah-kaliya-charitra-varnane vriddhanam snehadi-varnanam nama panchamah kiranah" — "the fifth kirana, called 'The Description of the Love (Sneha) of the Elders and Other Matters', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the excellence of the prema of the elders" (*punaḥ prātaḥkāle vṛddhagaṇānāṁ premotkarṣaḥ*) stands in the edition at the top of. Kirana 5 comprises verses 5.1–5.9.

[^05-2]: Vibhasha is a morning raga (cf. note 9 of kirana 3). Nadia (*nadīyā*) — Navadvipa. *āhā mari mari* is an exclamation of wonder ("ah, how wondrous!"), not a literal "I am dying". "Cannot keep life" — *parāṇa dharite nāre*. "All the elders, men and women" — *vṛddha vṛddha yata puruṣa prakṛti*. Lines 7–8 are taken thus: *narahari pãhu nichani se saba vṛddha parijana pāśe* — "Narahari [offers himself in] nirmanchhana to those elder kinsfolk of the Lord" (*pãhu … parijana*); *pāśe* ("beside, near") may also belong to the next line: "beside those elders [someone] says…". Nirmanchhana — see note 3 of kirana 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^05-3]: "Someone says" — *kopyāha* (Skt. *ko 'pi āha*). The address "O virtuous one" (*sati*), "O wise one" (*sumati*) is to a woman; probably an old man is speaking in secret to his wife (cf. 5.5: "each told his own kinsfolk in detail"; in 5.4 he cannot walk and asks her to go to Shachi). The sex of the speakers in 5.2–5.4 is not entirely clear from the text. "The son of the lord of Vraja" — Krishna, son of Nanda: the elder guesses that Gaura is Krishna Himself. Srivasa (Pandita) and Murari (Gupta) are the Lord's associates in Navadvipa. Advaita lived in Shantipura but often stayed in Navadvipa. "Make [Him] the necklace about your neck" — i.e., keep Him always with you, in your heart. Signature: Narahari.

[^05-4]: "And someone else [says]" — *kopi ca*. The first line is printed thus: *śuna ohe sati-nadīyā! basati saphala haila mora*; taken as *sati! nadīyā-basati…* — "O virtuous one! My dwelling in Nadia has borne fruit" (the exclamation mark and the hyphen are transposed).

[^05-5]: "Let me take His misfortunes upon myself!" — *bālāi laiyā mari*, a common Bengali expression of tender love (lit. "taking [on myself] His misfortunes, may I die"). "Let us make vows" — *mānābe*, from *mānā* ("to vow, to promise an offering to a deity for another's welfare"). "Through astrologers perform worship of the planets" — *graha pūjiba daivajña-gaṇe*: rites to propitiate the planets (*graha-pūjā*) performed by astrologers (*daivajña*). In the edition the seventh line ends with "॥" instead of "।". "Go with Narahari" — the author's signature woven into the speech: Narahari as a participant in the scene (cf. 5.4). On the rites and prayers to the gods for Gaura's sake — see note 8.

[^05-6]: The third line stands centred, the fourth ends with the mark *dhru* (refrain). "The comfort of the heart" — *parāṇa-juṛā*, lit. "cooling the life". "The Creator has become [my] enemy" — *bidhātā karile bāda*. "Think of [His] welfare" — the last word of the line is unclear in print: probably *hita* ("welfare"); possibly *cita* ("in the heart"). "Obstinacy" — *ā̃khuṭi* (*ākhuṭi*): a child's wilful stubbornness, insistent demands (the same word occurs in kirana 6). "Adjuring [Him]" — *śapatha diyā*, lit. "giving an oath": a mother coaxes a stubborn child by adjuring him. Signature: Narahari (as a participant in the scene, cf. 5.3).

[^05-7]: The verse is in couplets with a caesura; the metre is not named in the edition (only "yatharaga"). "Joyful" — *mudita*. "Beds" — *śeja*. "Summoned all their strength" — *bāndhala bala bhāri*, lit. "bound up great strength". "Sticks" — *laguṛa*. Signature: Narahari.

[^05-8]: "The venerable old men" — *bṛddha bṛddha bara puruṣa-gaṇe*. "After many long days" — *cira dibasāntara*. "All [my] wishes" — *manaratha sagare* (*sagare* — "all"). "May His life be longer than mine" — *madadhika hau paramāyu*, lit. "may [His] span of life be greater than mine". "May [people] ceaselessly praise" — *nirata janu guṇa bhaṇaye* (*janu* taken as "people"; possibly "as if"). The son of Mishra — Gaura, son of Jagannatha Mishra. The Lord of Kailasa — Shiva; Bhagavati — the goddess Durga. "So that He may never know defeat" — *ajaya janu na hai kadā* (*ajaya* — "defeat"). The last line also admits another reading: "may Narahari ever behold Nimai — [his] life". The elders, who love Gaura with parental love (vatsalya), see Him as a child and therefore pray to the Creator, Vishnu, Shiva, Sarasvati and Durga for His long life and welfare — just as in Vraja Yashoda and the elder cowherds prayed to the gods for Krishna's welfare (*Srimad-Bhagavatam*, Canto 10). This is a manifestation of vatsalya-prema, in which yogamaya hides the Lord's majesty from those who love Him; such prayers seek only the beloved's good, not any fruit for oneself. Signature: Narahari.

[^05-9]: Rasika (in print *rasikā*) is the name of a metre. "Absorbed" — *magana*. Signature: Narahari.

[^05-10]: "Borne fruit" — *suphala*. A kalpa is a day of Brahma, an immense span: a moment without Gaura seems an eternity. "Not a drop remains" — *rahai na thora*. "Upside down" — *biparīta*. Reading and sense unclear: *gṛha-paripāṭī nipaṭa kuṭamaya* — taken as "the household routine is sheer deceit" (*kuṭa* < *kūṭa*, "deceit, falsehood"); possibly "sheer bitterness". "Crest-jewel of fools" — *mūrkha-śiromaṇi*: the author's humble self-designation. Signature: Narahari Dasa.

[^05-11]: Rupamala is the name of a metre. "Sixteen years old" — *ṣoṛaśa baraṣa sadṛśa*: sixteen is the bloom of youth. "The sticks stay far away" — *dūra rahu laguṛa*. Signature: Dasa Narahari.

[^05-12]: The colophon was read in the 1947 edition: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে বৃদ্ধানাং স্নেহাদি-বর্ণনং নাম পঞ্চমঃ কিরণঃ ॥৫॥".


## Kirana 6. The Deeds of the Morning: Description of the Excellence of the Prema of [the Women] Endowed with Parental Love[^06-1]

*Morning in Shachi's courtyard: the elderly women of Nadia, who love Gaura with a mother's love — Sita Thakurani, the wife of Srivasa and others — gaze at Him, bless Him and pray to the gods for His welfare; Shachi asks Sita to bless Nimai and Nitai*

*Again, in the morning period — the conduct and so on of [the women] endowed with supreme affection.*

*Raga vibhasha*

**6.1.** Today at an auspicious moment the night has passed; the people of Nadia are immersed in bliss.
To see Gaurangachandra with affection, all come running to Shachi's house.
In the midst of the courtyard shines Gora — the thief of the minds and eyes of the people of the world.
The associates adorn every side — the moon amid the stars: what [else] can compare?
No one has any memory [of anything] in mind; everyone's eyes are on that face.
What can Narahari tell with a single mouth? All rejoice, each in his own rasa.[^06-2]

*Raga yatharaga*

**6.2.** Advaita's wife, Sita Thakurani — a very heap of rasa —
with unblinking eyes beholds the beautiful smile on Gaura's face.
Thinking and thinking of those new deeds, she has become like a doll;
she cannot keep her composure — streams [of tears] flow from both eyes.
How many, many thoughts arise in her heart! Her mind is distraught with affection.
With care she gives Shachi instructions [on how to protect Him];
giving endless blessings, she praises [Him] — there is no limit to [her] happiness.
Says Narahari: who has the power to understand these deeds?[^06-3]

*Raga vibhasha*

**6.3.** The wife of Srivasa, most virtuous, is like the very embodiment of affection,
ever modest, chaste, devoted to her husband — there is none like her in the world.
Her body blossoming [with joy], matchless, covering half her face with the edge of her cloth,
standing beside Sita she gazes intently [at Gaura] in the joy of her heart.
In the midst of the courtyard, surrounded by dear associates, sits Gora;
the beautiful moon of [His] face sparkles, [His] body is like gold.
Ever new and new — what can one say? Malini(?) floats in that ocean of beauty.
Wondrous prema! Narahari Dasa would take all [her] misfortunes upon himself.[^06-4]

*Raga yatharaga*

**6.4.** At the dawn of night there is no end to the joy in Shachidevi's heart;
beholding that face she cannot restrain herself — tears flow from her eyes.
Taking hold of Sita's feet, with care she speaks sweet words:
"You alone are [my] support, O [lady]; I know neither good nor bad.
Regarding Nimaichanda as your own, be ever gracious [to Him];
give the blessing that He may live long and happily.
I have no one [else] — how much more shall I entreat? This child is the star of my eyes.
Make it so that He always stays at home, that [His] restless ways may cease.
And I say more: this Nitai is my Vishvarupa, [my] life and soul;
grant this boon — that [they] may not be parted even for half a moment."
Having said many such things, she quickly performs the customary auspicious rites.
What can Narahari tell with a single mouth of the incomparable love of the mother?[^06-5]

*Raga yatharaga*

**6.5.** Shachi's house has become radiant — what can one say of [that] joy?
The elderly women, glad at heart, stand there and watch.
Someone says: "O, the lamp of the family, Nimai, a heap of virtues,
has appeared [here], coming to make our eyes fruitful!"
Someone says: "O son of Shachi, ever remain in well-being!
All my merit I have given to Him: may He escape many thorns."
Someone says: "For His sake I will worship the King of Kailasa,
that He, long-lived, may thus remain in Nadia."
Someone says: "Worshipping Ganga daily, I will ask for a boon:
may Shachi's darling, with His own people, joyfully keep house."
Someone says: "Worshipping Chandi, I will ask for what is in my heart:
that He may never go to foreign lands to earn wealth."
Someone says: "I worship Lakshmi — and there is a reason for it:
that He may effortlessly become very rich and never know want."
Someone says: "Listen further — I do not understand the ways of my own mind:
a love a hundred times greater than for my own son arises for Him."
Someone says: "Leaving my house, I come for His sake;
I do not know how [my] heart [aches] when I leave Him even for a moment."
Someone says: "Shachi is fortunate — she has done many pious deeds;
that is why she obtained this moon of Nadia, the treasure of everyone's life."
Someone says: "Let them say what they will(?) — of the Creator I ask just this:
birth after birth may I see this boy in Nadia(?)."
Thus, in the rapture of prema, they speak without end.
Says Narahari: what power have I to tell of the affection of them all?[^06-6]

*Raga yatharaga*

**6.6.** What joy today in the house of Shri Shachi at dawn, when the night has ended!
Amid His dear associates Vishvambhara shines, beautiful in His bearing.
To each, in whatever mood he has, in that mood [He] appears, making everyone happy.
Enchanter of the world, jewel of virtues, so accomplished — never [have we] seen [such a One]!
All the old men and women are deeply stirred by overflowing tenderness:
gazing and gazing at that moon of a face, no one can keep composure.
Tears flow from the eyes unceasingly, in the heart is the highest joy;
and again [they] tell one another of the deeds of Gauranga, the life of Narahari.[^06-7]

*A certain [woman] says — yatharaga*

**6.7.** Listen, listen, dear friend, I tell you: blessed is this town of Nadiya!
In the Treta-yuga — Kaushalya-devi, in the Dvapara-yuga — Yashoda, in the Kali-yuga — fortunate Shachi.
Blessed is Jagannatha Mishra, renowned in the world — there is no limit to his piety;
and this wife of his, devoted to her husband, full of tenderness — she has such a son, Nimai.
She is like the mother of the universe, I tell you: who else has such a nature?
Who [else] could bear so much childish mischief? There is no one in the world to compare with her.
I do not know which god showed mercy — that is why He has now become good;
otherwise Nimai, the life of Narahari, was a terrible madcap.[^06-8]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.8.** I will tell you about Nimaichand: Nimai is the crown of all madcaps!
Such a stubborn child I have never seen anywhere; blessed indeed are [His] father and mother!
On the full-moon day of the month of Phalguna, at the time of the eclipse, having been born, He cried bitterly;
however hard they tried, the infant would not take the breast — seeing this, everyone was afraid.
The great ascetic Gosai who lives in Shantipura knows that boy's ways:
I do not know by what device He made [Him] take the breast — and everyone's heart grew calm.
Let anyone say what they like — there is no fear in my heart; I have reasoned it out in my mind:
the treasure of Narahari's life will be a great madcap — it began with His very birth![^06-9]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.9.** My Nimai, [my] life, is a great madcap indeed! One day I saw with my own eyes:
His body grey with dust — how wondrous! — He crawls on all fours about the courtyard;
on His lovely moon-face a smile; He calls "Mother!" — at once Shachi came running.
Climbing onto [her] lap, He weeps until He is exhausted — seeing this, the heart seems to break.
However much [she] tries, [He] will not be consoled; again and again He flings His body [to the ground].
"What has happened, what has happened?" weeps the pious [mother]; no one can stay calm.
At that very moment a woman, in great distress, clapping her hands, says "Hari!"
Hearing this, the restless infant stops crying — and laughs, clasping her round the neck.
Everyone in joy says "Hari, Hari!"; Nimai, getting down from the lap,
cannot yet stand — and still He dances merrily, holding His mother's hand.
Why He cried — no one could understand; everyone ponders it inwardly.
Nimai, the life of Narahari, knows well how to play the madcap like this![^06-10]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.10.** The restless madcap Nimai heeds nothing! Listen to [what happened] one day.
Holding the edge of His mother's sari, He walks about the courtyard and sees His shadow there;
letting go of the sari's edge, He plays with the shadow; and there was a snake —
on its fearsome hood He lay down — and in what bliss, I cannot say!
Crying "Alas, alas!", everyone comes running; the serpent tries to slip away — [He] catches it again.
Everyone trembles; Shachi, distraught, carefully takes [Him] up in her arms.
At that very moment a bird flew past; seeing how it flew,
"Catch it and give it to Me!" He says again and again, and weeps, clasping His mother's neck.
From both eyes — streams [of tears] like a sapphire necklace; the line of kajal was washed away;
that moon-face grew dim — can one bear to see it with one's eyes?
Whatever anyone says — no one's words can distract [Him]; at His crying the heart breaks.
The infant, the life of Narahari, forgot by Himself — only then did [our] hearts grow calm.[^06-11]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.11.** My golden Nimai is the doll of my heart: are there such games anywhere in the world?
Whatever He asks for — if it is not given, there is trouble: no one can console [Him].
One day Nimai, crying "Give Me butter!", weeps, clutching the edge of His mother's sari;
the more they console Him, the more He rolls in the dust — not for a moment does He restrain Himself.
I do not know where from, but the mother brought fresh butter and put it in Nimai's hand;
having eaten the butter, He says: "I am a cowherd's son! Nothing but this is to My taste."
Looking into [His] face, we laughed and asked: "Whose cowherd boy are You, then?"
Hearing this, the infant, the life of Narahari, ran away; embarrassed, Shachi says: "Well, well!"[^06-12]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.12.** Listen to [what happened] with Nimai. One day Mother Shachi, gladly taking various offerings,
was worshipping the gods with care — so that restless Nimai would become good.
At that very moment, coming from who knows where, He ate the prepared food (naivedya) unseen
and, laughing, says: "I am the God of gods! Why do you not worship Me?"
Crying "Alas, alas!", Shachi chases [Him], great fear in [her] heart;
distraught, she thinks: "May nothing happen to Nimai!"
Meanwhile the child, seated on Mishra's lap, says: "Mother does not give Me anything to eat!"
Hearing the words of Nimai, the life of Narahari, the father's heart is filled with great joy.[^06-13]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.13.** This Nimaichand of mine, if He wants to eat and [the food comes] even a moment late,
in great anger breaks the house and the doors, causes great damage.
If anyone says anything — He [makes mischief] twice as much; He fears neither father nor mother;
none of the neighbours can restrain Him — He fears Vishvarupa alone.
Such a boy I have never seen anywhere: He roams Nadiya on His own,
slips unnoticed into anyone's house and, in play, does all sorts of things;
everywhere He makes the children cry — what is there to say of their mothers!
Who in Nadiya is not afraid of the pranks of Vishvambhara, the life of Narahari?[^06-14]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.14.** How much shall I tell of these deeds of Nimaichand! At bathing time on the bank of the Suradhuni
no one — neither woman nor man — can stay at peace: such great mischief He makes there!
Whoever comes carefully carrying various offerings to worship the gods —
there is no counting His quarrels with them; what does He not do in Nadiya!
And if anyone ever complains to Shachi and Mishra — how well-behaved He is then!
Great wonder grows in everyone's heart: seeing [this], the mind is lost.
As were the pranks of Kanu in the house of Nanda — such we see in the house of Shachi;
Nimai, the life of Narahari, surely is that very One; otherwise who would act like this?[^06-15]

*And another — yatharaga*

**6.15.** The story of Nimaichand is wondrous indeed: now the family deity has become gracious —
where have all those restless ways [of His] gone? Who [now] is as steady [as He]?
The people of Nadiya and all the panditas — who does not regard [Him] with respect?
Srivasa, Murari and the others — all the Vaishnavas — none can bear to leave His company.
And my Nitai loves [Him] like His own life — as Balai [loved] Krishna;
surely that very [love] has appeared here: such I have never seen anywhere.
Blessed in the world is pious Shachi: surely she is that same Queen of Vraja;
Nimai and Nitai are the two pupils of [her] eyes. Narahari offers himself in nirmanchhana to this prema.[^06-16]

*Raga yatharaga*

**6.16.** All the elderly women of Nadiya speak thus with one another.
How wondrous is the way of them all! What can [they] be compared to? — they are so full of tenderness.
Gazing and gazing at the moon of Gaurangachandra's face, they consider themselves blessed.
How many blessings they give again and again! Narahari, hearing [this], swims in that happiness.[^06-17]

*Thus [ends] the sixth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Excellence of the Prema of [the Women] Endowed with Parental Love", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^06-18]

[^06-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon at the end of the kirana: "…pratah-kaliya-charitra-varnane vatsalyavatinam premotkarsha-varnanam nama shashthah kiranah" — "the sixth kirana, called 'The Description of the Excellence of the Prema of [the Women] Endowed with Vatsalya (Parental Love)', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the conduct and so on of [the women] endowed with supreme affection" Kirana 6 comprises verses 6.1–6.16. It speaks of elderly women who love Gaura as a mother loves her child; this is not the mood of the "nagaris" (which begins from kirana 7 on).

[^06-2]: Vibhasha is a morning raga (cf. kirana 5). "Thief of the minds and eyes" — *mana-nayana-corā*. "The moon amid the stars" — *uḍu-madhi bidhu*. "With a single mouth" — cf. 4.3. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-3]: Sita Thakurani is the wife of Advaita Acharya; according to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 13), she came with gifts to the newborn Nimai and blessed Him. "A very heap of rasa" — *kebala rasera rāśi*. "New deeds" — *nava carita*: Gaura's youthful pastimes. "Like a doll" — *putula pārā* (the letters run together in print; the reading *putula* is probable): she stood motionless. "Gives Shachi instructions" — *upadeśa se-rūpa śacīra prati*: probably on how to protect the child. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-4]: The wife of Srivasa Pandita is Malini (cf. 1.13, note 30 of kirana 1). "Covering half her face with the edge of her cloth" — *ādha basana jhā̃piyā mukhe*: a sign of a married woman's modesty. In the seventh line the edition prints *myālānī* with the editor's own mark "(?)"; probably a corruption of *mālinī* — the name Malini; the translation is conjectural. "Ever new and new — what can one say?" — *naba naba saba ki kaba*. "Would take all [her] misfortunes upon himself" — *bālāi laiyā maru*, a Bengali expression of tender love (cf. note 5 of kirana 5). Signature: Narahari Dasa.

[^06-5]: Shachidevi is Gaura's mother. "You alone are [my] support" — Shachi addresses Sita with the respectful plural (*tomādera*, lit. "your [pl.]"). "The star of my eyes" — *ā̃khira tārā*. "Restless ways" — *cañcala dhārā*. Vishvarupa is Nimai's elder brother, who left home early and took sannyasa; Shachi fears that Nimai too will leave, and in Nityananda (Nitai) she sees her Vishvarupa. "Performs the customary auspicious rites" — *karaye maṅgala nīta* (*nīta* < *nīti*, "custom"). Signature: Narahari.

[^06-6]: "The lamp of the family" — *kulera pradīpa*. "All my merit I have given to Him" — *mora puṇya yata dilāma ihāre* (in print *dilāna*). "Thorns" — *kaṇṭaka*: dangers and misfortunes. The King of Kailasa — Shiva; Chandi — the goddess Durga; Lakshmi — the goddess of wealth; Ganga — the sacred river, worshipped as a goddess. On the elders' prayers to the gods for Gaura's welfare as a manifestation of vatsalya-prema, see note 8 of kirana 5. "With His own people joyfully keep house" — *nija-jane laiyā … ānande karuka ghara*. "What is in my heart" — *manete ye āche mena*: the last word is printed *mena*, probably for *hena* ("such"). "Never go to foreign lands to earn wealth" — later Nimai did in fact travel to East Bengal (*Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Adi 14). "Never know want" — *kabhu nā ṭhekibe bhāra*, lit. "never run up against a burden". Reading and sense unclear: *se bala se bala* — taken as "let [people] say what they will"; *nadyāya(?)* (print unclear; possibly another word) — taken as "in Nadia". Verse 6.5 begins and ends; it is translated in full. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-7]: Yatharaga — "a raga of choice". Vishvambhara is a name of Gaura. "Each one, in whatever mood…" — *yāra yei bhāva se bhāve bhābita*: understood as "to each, in whatever mood he is, [the Lord] appears in that mood"; possibly also "each contemplates [Him] in his own mood". "Accomplished" — *sughaṛa*. "Old men and women" — *bṛddha naranārī*: the elderly people of Nadiya; further on (6.7–6.15) the elderly women speak, full of parental love (vatsalya) for Nimai. Signature: Narahari ("Gauranga, the life of Narahari").

[^06-8]: The headings of 6.7–6.15 are Sanskrit notes: *kācid āha* ("a certain [woman] says", 6.7) and *anyā ca* ("and another", 6.8–6.15; in 6.12 the print looks like *antāca*; cf. note 3 of kirana 5); the ragas — *yathārāga*. The particle *go* in the middle of each line is an address to a female friend; as a rule it is not rendered. Kaushalya is the mother of Rama (Treta-yuga); Yashoda is the mother of Krishna (Dvapara-yuga). The end of the second line is cut off by the edge of the 1947 edition: it reads *bhāgyaba…*, understood as *bhāgyabatī* ("fortunate"); the end of the seventh line — *bhāla* ("good") — is also at the edge. "Has become good" — i.e., Nimai, having grown, has calmed down. "Madcap" — *khepā* (lit. "wild, crazed", said fondly of a mischievous child). Signature: Narahari.

[^06-9]: "Stubborn child" — *ā̃khuṭi* (a capricious, wilful child). "Blessed indeed" — *dhanya yena*: the reading *yena* is uncertain (faint print). Gaura appeared on the full moon of the month of Phalguna during a lunar eclipse (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 13; *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Adi-khanda). The ascetic Gosai of Shantipura is Advaita Acharya (cf. 5.2). "Let anyone say what they like" — the speaker is not afraid to say that she sees something extraordinary in Nimai. *mu* — "I". Signature: Narahari.

[^06-10]: "Crawls on all fours" — *hāmāguṛi*. "Flings His body [to the ground]" — *aṅga āchāṛaye*. The pious one (*puṇyabatī*) is Shachi. The crying infant Nimai would quiet down only on hearing the name of Hari — a well-known episode of His infancy (*Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Adi-khanda; *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi-lila). "Play the madcap" — *khepāmi*. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-11]: The episode with the snake — cf. *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Adi-khanda (the infant Nimai plays with a snake in the courtyard; according to the CB, it was Ananta-Shesha, who had come to serve the Lord). "Tries to slip away — [He] catches it again" — *palāite nāga punaḥ dhare*. "How it flew" — *kibā se bhaṅgite*. "Like a sapphire necklace" — *nīlamaṇi hāra-pārā*: tears darkened by kajal (eye collyrium). "Can one bear to see it with one's eyes?" — *tāhā kiye ā̃khe yāya dekhā*, i.e., it is unbearable to see. "Forgot by Himself" — *āpani bhulila*: He was distracted, forgetting the bird. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-12]: "The doll of my heart" — *parāṇa putuli*. "Edge of the sari" — *ācara* (*ā̃cala*). "I am a cowherd's son" (*mu' gopa-tanaya*) — Nimai unwittingly reveals that He is Krishna, the son of the cowherd Nanda, who loved butter. "Nothing … is to My taste" — *kichu nāi bhāya*. "Cowherd boy" — *chāoyāla* (child, son). The last line is ambiguous: *lāje* ("from embarrassment") may refer either to Nimai ("ran away in embarrassment") or to Shachi; "Well, well!" — *bhāla bhāla* (lit. "good, good"). Signature: Narahari.

[^06-13]: "Listen" — the print has *śunaye* (probably for *śunaha*). Naivedya — food prepared for offering to the Deity. "Chases" — *dābāṛiyā yāya*. "May nothing happen" — Shachi fears the gods will be angry with the child for eating the offering. Mishra is Jagannatha Mishra, Nimai's father. Similar childhood episodes are in the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi-khanda). Signature: Narahari.

[^06-14]: The third line has no end mark in the edition; *go* occurs in it twice. Vishvarupa is Nimai's elder brother. "Anyone's" — the print has *yāba tāra*, understood as *yāra tāra*. "In play" — *helāte*. "What is there to say of their mothers" — *ki baliba tā sabāra māya*: understood thus (possibly "what can one say to [their] mothers?"). Signature: Narahari.

[^06-15]: The Suradhuni is the Ganga. Nimai's mischief at the bathing ghats of the Ganga — with the bathers and with the girls who came to worship the gods — and His innocent look when His parents came to punish Him are described in the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi-khanda). "The mind is lost" — *nā rahe buddhi gati*. Kanu is Krishna; the pranks (*dhāmāli*) in the house of Nanda are Krishna's childhood play in Vraja. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-16]: "The family deity has become gracious" — *ebe e prasanna kuladebā*: now, by the grace of the family deity, Nimai has grown up and become steady (cf. 6.7). "With respect" — *samiha* (*samīha*). Srivasa Pandita and Murari Gupta are associates of the Lord in Navadvipa. Nitai is Nityananda; Balai is Balarama, Krishna's elder brother (cf. note 11 of kirana 4). The Queen of Vraja (*brajeśvarī*) is Yashoda. Nirmanchhana — see note 3 of kirana 3. Signature: Narahari. The stanza begins and ends; next come stanza 6.16 and the colophon of kirana 6.

[^06-17]: The verse is in couplets with a caesura in the middle of each line (in the edition the halves of the lines are divided by "।"); the heading is *yathārāga*. "Speak thus with one another" sums up the speeches of the elderly women in 6.7–6.15. "So full of tenderness" — *ati snehavatī*. "Swims in that happiness" — *se sukhe sā̃tāre*. Signature: Narahari.

[^06-18]: The colophon was read in the 1947 edition: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে বাৎসল্যবতীনাং প্রেমোৎকর্ষ-বর্ণনং নাম ষষ্ঠঃ কিরণঃ ॥৬॥".


## Kirana 7. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Conduct of the Nagaris of Shri Navadvipa[^07-1]

*Morning in Navadvipa: the young women of Nadia (the nagaris), filled with prema for Gaura, gather in secret at dawn on the pretext of household tasks and tell one another how, the day before, having outwitted their mothers-in-law, sisters-in-law and aunts, they managed to see Gaura on the road, if only for a moment*

*Again, in the morning period — the conversations with one another and so on of the nagaris of Shri Navadvipa, absorbed in prema for Him.*

*Vibhasha*

**7.1.** The joy there is in Shachi's house at the dawn of the night —
even in hundreds upon hundreds of yugas, with a thousand mouths, not the smallest part of it could be described.
How many playful ways Gaurachandra knows to give happiness to His own!
Surely, in the middle of the courtyard He has spread a snare that enchants the worlds:
leaving their beds, running, running, all the people come, full of joy,
sink into that ocean of beauty — and cannot turn back home again.
A most wondrous love arises unceasingly in everyone's heart;
at those crimson feet they offer body and mind, says Dasa Narahari.[^07-2]

*Raga yatharaga*

**7.2.** Shachi's darling is love embodied; His fame fills the world.
There is no one who, having once seen the sweetness of that form, would not be enchanted.
Most wondrous is [His] rasikata — one cannot understand what quality is in Him:
no one in the world can live without loving Gaurahari.
And the tenderness these nagaris of Nadia have for Gauranga —
is there anyone who keeps composure while telling or hearing of it?
Gora is their japa, tapas, meditation and contemplation; their minds know nothing else.
Without the sight of Gorachandra for even half a moment, they regard everything as empty.
Gora is their life, wealth, being and [the honour of their] birth; that Gora is the pupil of their eyes.
Lying down and in dreams, saying "Gora, Gora!", they have become like madwomen.
To composure, dharma, modesty and fear for [the honour of] the family they have given tilanjali;
they always wish for happiness only in Gora's happiness, sings Dasa Narahari.[^07-3]

*Raga yatharaga*

**7.3.** Ah, ah! Let me take upon myself the misfortunes of such nagaris of Nadia:
this night they all spent in great longing.
Some stayed awake all night, singing the deeds of Gorachandra;
some, falling happily asleep, found in dreams the moon — Gaura.
And again, leaving their beds, they rose at the dawn of the night
and came out of this house and that house on the pretext of tasks.
The nagaris are supremely clever — I cannot understand their conduct at all:
whatever task pleases their elders, that task they do with care;
with their permission they come and go — what more can I say?
Such is their custom day after day — and there is no limit to [their] happiness.
Unnoticed, in a most secluded place, the lovely young [women], the allure of the world, sit down —
little by little they all gathered there; Narahari beholds [that] beauty.[^07-4]

*Raga yatharaga*

**7.4.** What can I say of the love the young women have for one another?
Their bodies are different — their mind is one; who has the power to understand this tenderness?
Some rasika, laughing and laughing, throws her arms around someone's neck;
someone teases someone else, using all kinds of tricks.
Someone says: "Leave off pretending, tell us about yesterday — let us hear:
who had what obstacle, and how did each one see Gaura the jewel?"
Someone says: "Come now, tell us how you spent this night."
Narahari says: it would be good to tell all these stories in detail.[^07-5]

*A certain [woman] says — yatharaga*

**7.5.** What are you asking, friend, about yesterday? When I start to tell it, I feel like laughing.
Casting off modesty, I shall tell how I saw the moon of Nadia.
At the close of day my mother-in-law, my sister-in-law and some others [were at home];
I was sitting beside them, making a show of my good behaviour.
At that very moment the Lord of my life came along our road.
Hearing [of it], I started, looked all around — and became utterly restless.
I fell into a terrible predicament: not a single thought came into my mind;
my heart was in turmoil — what to do? — tears flowed secretly from my eyes.
Seeing me so distracted, my mother-in-law speaks sweet words:
"Why are you like this, child? I don't know — do you have some pain?"
As soon as she said this, I told her: "I feel somehow unwell."
Hearing that, she gave permission: "Go and lie down in the house."
On the pretext of lying down, I quickly went and stood outside the house;
seeing the sweetness of that face just once, I cooled my eyes and heart.
No one could notice anything, and my whole body was filled with bliss.
Narahari says: who will understand a rasika's cleverness?[^07-6]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.6.** What can I say about yesterday, friend? When I start to tell it, my heart weeps.
I saw — and yet could not see enough of the moon of Nadia, my life and soul.
Listen to that story: I was sitting alone, quite in seclusion;
suddenly I hear from people's mouths that Gaura is passing by.
Going out quickly, I saw Him together with His dear associates;
flashing like lightning, He went on His way.
Casting off modesty, having seen that face only once, I lost myself completely;
for fear of the elders I hurried home, my heart all but breaking.
I don't know who told it to my mother-in-law;
hearing it, with a fierce face she rushed at me in anger.
How many harsh words she spoke! Hearing them, I trembled with fear.
"I did not see [Him]!" I said, and, swearing an oath, touched her feet.
Telling so many untruths, I came out the good girl — she believed it.
Narahari says: there is a fault in this, and you do not admit it — that is not good.[^07-7]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.7.** Shamelessly I'll tell it, friend — listen to my words:
pitiless Providence yesterday gave me cruel pain.
After many days my aunt came to my house;
with joy in my heart I went and sat beside her, leaving everything.
At that very moment I hear that Gaura-nagara is passing by —
and I became most eager to go out of the door.
But whenever I think of getting up — oh! — she looks at me again,
takes hold of the edge of my sari and carefully seats me, conjuring me by her head.
Her guileless heart understands nothing of all this:
in every way, with concern, she asks about the household affairs.
But my heart is in turmoil — I hear it and do not hear with my ears;
what I mean to say, what I do say — neither good nor bad stays in my mind.
She shows me love, as is fitting, but to me it is like poison;
I cannot show it outwardly — from constraint my insides burn.
Seeing such a plight, I thought: better I should leave this body.
Narahari says: don't you know a trick to get round your aunt?[^07-8]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.8.** Listen, friend: as I was coming alone from the ghat of the Suradhuni,
I met the moon of Nadia on the road.
What wondrous sweetness! His sweet gait surpasses an elephant's;
I don't know who fashioned this form, love embodied, or how.
What comparison can I give? — I thought: Gora in a new attire…
I would keep Him in the depth of my heart, or make Him the pupil of my eyes!
At the sight of that face, composure, dharma and my inmost [feeling] were left far behind;
the pot on my hip fell to the ground and broke into a hundred pieces.
What could I do? Having joyfully given my life and youth to the Beloved of my heart,
for fear of the elders I came home and sat with sorrow in my heart.
I don't know who told my sister-in-law about the broken pot;
in terrible anger, like a forest fire, she came rushing at me.
Seeing her fearsome look, [I knew] no excuse would work;
clasping my head with both hands, I wept so much that my eyes swelled.
Seeing my extraordinary weeping, her anger left her;
growing calm, she asks again and again — but I gave her no answer.
After a while, having thought it over, she took me by the hand
and says softly: "Why won't you tell me what is in your heart?"
With great effort, in a choking voice, I spoke to her:
"I am weeping from grief of heart — why ask about all this in vain?
Those whom you hold dear as life — they do dreadful things:
at the ghats, in the fields, on the roads they slander you; hearing it, I am ashamed.
I thought of tying the pot to my neck and entering the waters of the Ganga —
but I could not do it, lest a stain fall on the family.
What am I to do? I cannot quarrel with all of them!
Hearing all the disgrace that has come upon you, I was utterly bewildered.
Whom could I tell? Not one friend was with me there —
in anger at them all I broke the pot on the road."
Hearing this, she was greatly delighted at heart and, showing me love,
consoling me in every way, wiped my face with her own hand.
Thus yesterday, by being bold, I escaped a terrible plight.
Narahari says: let me take upon myself the misfortunes of your cleverness![^07-9]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.9.** What shall I tell you, friend, about my sister-in-law! When I start to tell it, I cannot help laughing:
she is a faithful wife, and by her reckoning all the women of Nadia are unfaithful.
And, worse still, she will not let me exchange a word with anyone;
she scolds me all the time; I dare not go anywhere alone for fear.
In heartache I languish day and night, hearing her reproaches.
I prayed to the Creator: may her pride be brought low!
I do not know by what merit the kind Creator heard my plea:
what I had in mind came true at once, as it were.
Listen, then, to what happened: yesterday she set out, taking a water pot,
and I, having got her permission, followed close behind her.
On the road by which the two of us go to the ghat of the Suradhuni —
on that very road Gora was standing with His dear associates.
Seeing that sweetness of His form, my sister-in-law could not keep her composure:
a violent trembling seized her, her body shook under the onslaught of Madana.
The pot fell from her hip to the ground, the hair on her head came loose;
her garment slipped from her body of itself — and not a trace of self-awareness remained.
With great effort she regained her composure and, deeply ashamed,
took me by both hands and said very softly, looking into my face:
"Know for certain, virtuous sister-in-law: you are dearer to me than life.
Whatever I have said to you — bear me no grudge: I am in your power.
Whatever you do at any time, tell me about it without hesitation:
I will help you, giving my life and my wealth — I say this on oath.
Do not tell others about all this — keep it secret.
I am caught in this rasa — what can I say? — Gaurahari has driven me mad!"
Much like this she said; hearing it, I was filled with boundless joy,
but when I thought back on what had gone before, great bitterness welled up.
I thought to myself: what is the use of disclosing all this?
Narahari says: one whose ways are those of the virtuous guards the honour of others.[^07-10]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.10.** Listen, listen, friend of my heart: knowing that you feel for me, I tell you.
The ways of my household are unheard of anywhere in the land: when I begin to tell of them, my heart weeps.
If I so much as sigh, saying "Gora!", the old woman blazes with anger on hearing it;
my sister-in-law is like a deadly poison: through her "virtues" my very life is scorched;
the neighbours are nothing but thorns to the family: day and night they taunt me with this.
Whom, friend, can I call as witness to this? I live in the house like a bird in a cage.
What more can I say of all that? In the telling, my sorrow has no shore.
Yesterday the Creator was kind to me: He placed the moon of the sky in my hand.
At the close of day Gaura-raya was walking along our road;
hurrying to the window, I gazed at Him unseen.
How sweet is that moon of His face — a snare for the hearts of young women!
The pair of brows, curved in an exquisite arch: what woman could keep her composure?
The eyes dance, surpassing the khanjana birds — I understood: from them no one escapes.
On His neck a garland of flowers sways — at the sight of it how many Kamadevas swoon!
What more can I say of that wondrous beauty? His gait enchants the worlds.
I was able to see Him there only for a moment — and the ache of my heart was doubled.
Narahari says: the sorrow will not remain — all will be as your heart wishes.[^07-11]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.11.** What shall I tell you, friend, about our household? If you hear it all, it will pain you.
Yesterday my night turned into a happy dawn: in solitude I saw the moon Gaura.
To blazes with it — what is shame to me now? I will tell you something of that story.
Leaving me with my sister-in-law, my mother-in-law went off to that [far] quarter of the village.
Here my sister-in-law picked a quarrel and said many hurtful things to me;
to have her own way she dropped everything and went off angrily to someone else's house.
I stayed at home alone; I went and sat by the window;
thinking in my mind of Gaura's beauty and virtues, I kept gazing at the road.
At that very moment Gauranga appeared alone on our road.
Unseen, beholding that moon-face, I forgot a little of my heart's pain;
quickly I dropped a withered kumuda bud through the window.
Seeing it, Gora — so quick-witted! — took it in His hand and said to the kumuda:
"Do not grieve: the moon will rise, and the sun's heat will go far away."
Having said this, He smiled and glanced at me once from the corner of His eyes.
At once my body went limp, as if a deadly snake had bitten me;
try as I might, I could not keep my composure: I longed to go and touch Him.
Again and again I trembled, my body broke out in sweat; I stood up, but my feet would not walk.
What can I say? Consoling my heart, I stayed there in great distress.
Just then my mother-in-law came home and asked me: "Why are you like this?"
Very piteously I said to her: "My sister-in-law will not let me live in the house.
When you yourself are at home she says nothing, but inside she burns like fire.
You left the house and went there — and here she started a quarrel with me.
There is no end to the telling of it; in this I was not at all to blame.
If you have any doubt in your mind, ask these neighbours.
What can I say? Leaving me alone, my sister-in-law went off to someone else's house —
from this you can see how much sense she has. And why I am like this — listen:
first, being alone, there was fear in my heart; and on top of that I was worrying about the housework;
what could I do — I had to work very hard, and that is why I became giddy."
Hearing my faltering words, she lovingly laid her own hand on my head;
fanning me with the end of her own cloth and holding my hand, she comforted me at length;
and she called my sister-in-law and scolded her — hearing that, I rejoiced.
Narahari says: blessed are you! Does anyone else know such a clever trick?[^07-12]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.12.** Listen, friend, I will tell you: I do not know what has happened to me.
Hastily putting on a new sari, I set out alone for my brother's house.
On the way I met Gora — what can I say? His beauty is beyond description.
Once He glanced at me — I do not know what He did with the corner of His eyes:
composure, dharma, modesty — all of it was struck down at that very moment, as it were.
My heart is all astir; I console it, yet cannot calm it.
Restless, I want to walk, but my feet will not move; my body trembles and trembles;
again and again my garment slips from my body; seeing all this, He smiles once more.
What could I do? For fear of my elders I somehow made my way home.
And again my body is restless without the darshan of that Gaurasundara.
At that very moment my mother-in-law came, sat down beside me and asked:
"Why do I see you like this today? Your eyes are brimming with tears;
you speak so sorrowfully — I do not know what pain has come to you."
Hearing this, I said to her: "I had gone out to the outer gate,
and there I saw a terrible snake: my heart trembled, my courage left me.
It came darting at me again — in panic I ran away;
then it slid off into the ditch — I was saved by the power of your feet."
Hearing this, she was greatly alarmed and herself splashed water on my eyes and face;
laying her own hand on my head, in her heart's fear she made many vows to Shiva.
Narahari says: nothing daunts you — you certainly know how to fool a mother-in-law![^07-13]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.13.** Listen, listen, friend: at my mother-in-law's bidding I went to do the cooking.
In great haste, with much care, I lit the stove.
On my wrist was my golden bangle — suddenly I caught sight of it,
and at once golden-hued Gaura-raya came to my mind.
No awareness remained: falling to the ground, I closed my eyes.
"What has happened?" crying, my mother-in-law, distraught at the sight of me, caught hold of me.
At that very moment my aunt arrived, and [my mother-in-law] spoke with her:
"The daughter-in-law I longed for so much — and seeing this, I suffer terrible pain.
Now and then she becomes like this — oh, it frightens me.
How much money has been spent — how many more times must I go to the exorcist's house?"
Hearing all this, my aunt says: "Do not worry.
No expense will be needed — I will tell you a simple remedy.
The other day I saw Nimai, the son of Shachi, to my eyes' content.
What power is in His feet I cannot understand at all:
many people, afflicted by many ailments, came to Him,
and with the touch of His feet He took away their suffering — what more can I say!
If somehow you can have Him touch her with His feet,
she will recover without trouble — be sure to do it."
My mother-in-law says: "Good, good that you reminded me — I too have heard of it.
But how can we go there now? Tell me a way."
So the two of them, seeing no way out, ponder, clutching their heads.
At that very moment the moon Gaura appeared on the road.
Hearing of it from people, my mother-in-law ran, took Gauranga by the hand,
and pleaded: "Come to my house, just for a moment!"
Hearing this, He came quickly; my mother-in-law entreated Him again:
"Be merciful — touch these crimson feet to my daughter-in-law's body.
Do not hesitate in the least, Nimai: good fame will be Yours in the world.
By the touch of Your feet all her troubles will go."
Hearing this, Gora, the ocean of virtues, covering His face with His cloth,
very slowly placed His lovely foot upon my breast.
At once I regained consciousness and opened both my eyes;
beholding the sweetness of that beautiful form, I floated on an ocean of happiness.
Rising quickly and setting my clothes in order, I went and sat down to the cooking.
My mother-in-law and my aunt, looking at me, were filled with joy;
how many blessings they gave Gorachanda — their hearts could not contain their delight!
And He, joining His associates, went on to the house of Nandanacharya.
Again and again the two of them praised [Him] and made Gauranga their all.
Hearing all that talk — what was in my heart, how can I say more?
Narahari says: you are very clever, and besides, your mother-in-law is fond of you;
had your sister-in-law been there, there would have been trouble — none of this would have happened.[^07-14]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.14.** However much I tell of yesterday, I cannot tell it all; listen, friend of my heart.
My sister-in-law is dreadful; I will tell you about her ways.
Having prayed to the Creator, at daybreak, gathering her own [friends],
she holds long counsel on how to pick a quarrel with me.
Finding no fault in me, she speaks to my mother-in-law in private:
"Know for certain: thanks to the 'exploits' of your daughter-in-law we shall not be able to stay in this land.
Whenever I send her on some errand, she sits there and stays on;
she is forever speaking ill-omened words about her own husband to everyone;
she plots your ruin and mine — she wants to be [mistress] alone.
The quarrels she picks with me are beyond counting.
Dressing herself up, she goes strutting about wherever she pleases;
so many people say so much about her — hearing it, my whole body burns as if on fire.
And there is one more thing I must tell — I cannot keep it to myself:
your daughter-in-law will surely bring disgrace [on the family] with a brahmana's son."
Hearing all this, my mother-in-law says: "I understand; [but] how shall I know [the truth] without looking [myself]?
When I see it with my own eyes, then I will believe."
My sister-in-law fell silent; my mother-in-law went off about her own work.
And [my sister-in-law], put to shame, again gathered her own and laid a plan:
"Good — now her mother-in-law is not at home.
Let her stay alone; I will leave her and go for water.
On the road I shall find some young man
and send him quickly to the house in mother's name.
He will sit waiting in the house, and I, having fetched water, will hurry back;
seeing him, I will angrily call the neighbours together,
show him to them all, and quarrel with the daughter-in-law;
I will throw the man out, again saying many bad things [about her].
To the old woman I will say: 'When you went over there, I went for water —
and here your daughter-in-law entertained a strange man in the house.'
If she will not believe it, then I will say in anger:
'Oh, she is very virtuous indeed, your daughter-in-law — just ask the neighbours!'
Hearing so much from the neighbours, she will question them and believe;
she will make the daughter-in-law's life unbearable — what joy that will be for me!"
Having reasoned thus, haughtily putting on a new sari,
she went to fetch water, leaving me alone at home.
Glad at heart, in great haste — by the very road my sister-in-law takes,
by that road comes Gaura-raya with His associates.
Seeing the sweetness of that form, my sister-in-law lost her senses again and again;
every scheme in her heart — she forgot it all.
Along that road came my mother-in-law and, looking, sees her close by:
with the water pot on her hip, [my sister-in-law] is wandering along behind the moon Gauranga.
"Fine, fine!" saying, in great anger [my mother-in-law] snatched the pot away.
What could my sister-in-law say, and to whom? At once she was mortified to the core.
Meanwhile I, finding Gauranga-sundara, my very life, on our own road,
soothed the ache of my heart gazing at that moon-face.
After a while my mother-in-law came and praises me at length;
and my sister-in-law's shame — what can I say? She is in the house as if she were not there.
Narahari says: when one is a fool, one can see nothing at all:
tying one's own fault in the end of one's sari, one wants to blame another.[^07-15]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.15.** What can I say, friend? Never has the wish of my heart been fulfilled:
to keep me from tasting happiness, the merciless Creator has raised many obstacles.
Yesterday the Beloved of my heart passed along our road.
Hearing of it from people, I took courage, went upstairs, and stood on the gallery.
Seeing the sweetness of that form, both my eyes were drowned [in it];
I longed to become a bird and fly close to Him.
The fragrance of His graceful body came and reached my senses;
I lost all composure — what can I say? — my happiness knew no bounds.
Gora turned and looked my way — and at that very moment the old woman
began calling [me] again and again; my heart trembled, and I came [down], leaving that happiness.
She gave me leave to go for water — and I floated on waters of bliss.
Narahari says: such a mother-in-law is found only by great good fortune.[^07-16]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.16.** Friend! However much I tell the tale of my sorrows, I cannot tell it all.
But yesterday the kind Creator eased my pain a little.
Leaving me at the cooking, my mother-in-law was out of the house;
hearing that Gaura was coming, she hurried back to me.
Looking at me again, she bolted the doors of the house
and, sitting in the middle of the courtyard, kept watching warily in every direction.
And here my heart was in turmoil — I could see no way out.
There was a small window — I pressed both my eyes to it.
Among His associates — the crown of rasikas: who can understand His ways?
Quite unnoticed, glancing in all four directions, He walks with an elephant's gait.
Once I beheld the sweetness of that form — eyes met eyes;
what state I was in — I made it known to Him, as it were, catching a sign.
Coming my way, with a slight smile, the Jewel of the clever spoke [a word].
And I sat down again to the cooking; my one-eyed mother-in-law unbolted the doors;
looking at me askance with her left eye, she was reassured.
Narahari says: had that eye gone too, it would have been just as well![^07-17]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.29.** My husband is a very good man, friend; listen to what his ways are like.
Yesterday, sitting alone [with his father], he said to his father:
"In the town of Nadia [lives] Nimai Pandita — in Him is the power of Ishvara.
Who created [Him] I do not know; there is no end to such virtues.
Such a master of the senses, so righteous a [man], I have never seen with my own eyes.
He is very merciful to people of evil mind; He bathes in the joy of kirtana.
Therefore I say: never, not even by oversight, forbid your daughters-in-law [to see Him];
I know for certain: by His darshana all that is inauspicious is destroyed.
Fortunate are all [our] daughters-in-law — what more can I say? I cannot even express it.
Therefore blessed is this woman — I would take upon myself the misfortunes of her whole life!
Drunk with false pride, day and night we live like the blind:
such a wonder in the midst of Nadia — and we cannot recognize it.
In Vraja the brahmanas did not know the Lord of Vraja — the brahmanas' wives attained [Him].
So it is here too, there is no doubt of it — I have understood this on reflection."
Thus father and son both spoke in many ways;
standing in hiding, I listened and listened — and my heart rejoiced.
I thought: if at this moment I could see Gorachanda,
with the corner of my eyes I would tell Him this whole story.
At that very moment joyful cries rose again and again in our quarter;
I hurried out and stood by the road, knowing that Gaura was coming.
Standing at a distance, I gazed, filling my eyes: what wondrous beauty!
The radiance of His body, surpassing gold, lights up everything around.
On His left — Gadadhara, on His right — Nityananda, the abode of bliss;
all around — other associates too: they gaze at that moon of a face.
With His own [companions] comes the Crown of rasikas, full of rasa.
Is there anyone who, seeing that lovely glance, could stand firm?
Smiling, smiling, under the guise of conversation the moon of Nadia showers streams of nectar;
the grace of His movements has utterly enchanted the world — as if it were the snare of Madana.
Knowing that the Lord of life was coming, the young women of the quarter came running;
their mothers-in-law and sisters-in-law sternly held them back, saying many [things].
But no one held me back — I fulfilled my heart's wish.
Narahari says: for one whose husband is so gracious, what obstacles can there be?[^07-18]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**7.30.** Listen, listen, friend: the Creator has no taste for rasa — I have understood how things stand.
Otherwise why would He give [me] such sorrow day and night?
If He had settled one of Gaura's associates here,
then the moon of Nadia would come to this quarter and dispel [my] pain.
So let me tell you, oh, about yesterday: leaving all [the work] in the house,
I went with my aunt to that quarter, to the house of Murari Gupta.
There the women of the house were overjoyed, their happiness had no bounds:
for the Beloved of their life they were grinding sandal paste and stringing garlands of flowers.
From their lips I heard that Gauranga would come there.
Having finished her business, my aunt came [for me] — I could not stay there.
Thinking and thinking [of Him], having gone a little way, I looked at the road:
the young crown prince of Nadia, adorned, was coming with His own [companions].
What wondrous beauty of the lips! The teeth — a radiance of stars;
the smile showers streams of nectar; the face — the splendour of the autumn moon.
What a curve of the brows! Long curving eyes, glances in so many ways;
on the forehead lovely sandal paste — at the sight of it all young women are immersed [in that beauty].
At His neck sways a necklace of gold and jewels — it lights up the world;
His gait is of a captivating cast: who in the world, seeing it, would not be enchanted?
In that ocean of beauty I bathed happily, staying beside my aunt.
Looking back, I saw: my husband's elder brother was coming behind with some students.
By good fortune he did not see me — he was looking at Gora.
Covering my face with my veil, lowering my eyes, I carefully hurried away.
Narahari says: what shame there is before a husband's elder brother — do I not know it?
If all this were disclosed, you could not live in this land![^07-19]

*Surata-surati-chhandah*

**7.39.** Thus, remembering the happiness of the past day, the young women
each tell their own charming story, as the heart prompts.
A thrill of the utmost sweetness — as if from cold, their bodies broke into gooseflesh.
Narahari cannot fathom that joy — that supreme delight.[^07-20]

*Thus [ends] the seventh kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Conduct of the Nagaris of Shri Navadvipa", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^07-21]

[^07-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon at the end of the kirana: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্রচিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে শ্রীনবদ্বীপ-নাগরীনাং চরিত্র-বর্ণনং নাম সপ্তমঃ কিরণঃ ॥" — "the seventh kirana, called 'The Description of the Conduct (*caritra*) of the Nagaris of Shri Navadvipa', in the description of the deeds of the morning period" (the print has *nāgarīnāṁ* for *nāgarīṇāṁ*; there is no number after "॥"). The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the conversations with one another and so on of the nagaris of Shri Navadvipa, absorbed in prema for Him" Kirana 7 comprises verses 7.1–7.39. The nagaris (*nāgarī*, lit. "town woman"; in poetry also "a refined heroine skilled in love") are the young married women of Navadvipa; this and the following kiranas are about them.

[^07-2]: Vibhasha is a morning raga (cf. kiranas 5–6). "With a thousand mouths" — *sahasra badane*: like the thousand-mouthed Ananta-Shesha, who eternally sings the glories of the Lord. "Spread a snare that enchants the worlds" — *phā̃dila bhubana-mohana phā̃da*. "Leaving their beds" — *śeja teji*. "Ocean of beauty" — *śobhā-sāyara*. "Crimson feet" — *rāṅgā caraṇa*. Signature: Dasa Narahari.

[^07-3]: "The nagaris of Nadia" (*nadīyā-nāgarī*) — see note 1. Here Narahari for the first time directly describes the mood of the nagaris — the young women of Navadvipa who, seeing Gaura's beauty, are filled with prema and longing for Him; so in this and the following kiranas. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati and the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women or even look at them), reject *gaura-nagari-vada*, the worship of Gaura as an enjoying lover (*nāgara*) and of oneself as His nagari: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer. The descriptions of the nagaris in the poem are understood as a glorification of Gaura's irresistible beauty and of the prema it awakens in hearts; cf. the last line of the verse: the nagaris wish for happiness only in Gaura's happiness. Further descriptions of the nagaris refer back to this note. "Love embodied" — *pīriti murati*. "Rasikata" — *rasikatā*: expertise in tasting rasa. "Meditation and contemplation" — *dhiyāna bhābanā*. "[The honour of their] birth" — *jāti* (lit. "caste, birth"): all that a person values in society. "Like madwomen" — *pāgalī pārā*. Tilanjali is the offering of water with sesame seeds by which one takes final leave of the departed; "to give tilanjali" is an idiom of complete renunciation. "Fear for [the honour of] the family" — *kula-bhaya*. Signature: Dasa Narahari.

[^07-4]: "Let me take upon myself the misfortunes" — *bālāi laiyā*, a Bengali expression of tender love (cf. note 5 of kirana 5, note 4 of kirana 6). "Spent [the night]" — *gõyāilā*. "Found in dreams" — *svapane pāilā*. "On the pretext of tasks" — *kājera chale*. "Pleases their elders" — *gurujana-sukha*. "With their permission they come and go" — *tā sabāra anumati mate gatāgati*: the nagaris do not break outward propriety, leaving the house only with their elders' permission. "Unnoticed" — *alakhita*. "The lovely young [women], the allure of the world" — *suyubatī jagata-lobhā*; the reading *suyubatī* is uncertain (blurred print). The first word of the last line (*krame*, "little by little") is damaged in the print. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-5]: "Their bodies are different — their mind is one" — *tanu bhina mana eka*. "Rasika" — *rasikinī*: one skilled in tasting rasa. "Using all kinds of tricks" — *kariyā kateka chalā*. "Gaura the jewel" — *gauramaṇi*. "Spent [the night]" — *bañcile*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-6]: The heading of 7.5 is Sanskrit *kācid āha* ("a certain [woman] says"); the headings of 7.6–7.8 are *kācic ca* ("and a certain [other]"; cf. note 8 of kirana 6). Mother-in-law — *śāśuṛi*; sister-in-law — *nanada* (the husband's sister): the speaker is a young married woman in her husband's house, under the eye of her elders. "Making a show of my good behaviour" — *jānā'yā sujanapanā*. "The Lord of my life" — *parāṇa-pati*, an address of the padas to the beloved Lord; see note 3. "The moon of Nadia" — *nadyāra śaśī*. "My heart was in turmoil" — *ān cān kare prāṇa*. "Distracted" — *bimanā*. The ninth line has no end mark. "I feel somehow unwell" — *gā mora kemana kare*, lit. "my body is somehow [strange]". "Cooled my eyes and heart" — *juṛānu nayāna hiyā*. "My whole body was filled" — *bharila de* (*de* = *deha*, "body"). "A rasika's cleverness" — *rasika janāra cāturi*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-7]: "I saw — and yet could not see enough" — *dekhiyā dekhite nā pāinu*: she saw Him only for a moment. "Flashing like lightning" — *bidyutera mata camaki*. "I lost myself completely" — *bikala hainu*. "My heart all but breaking" — *parāṇe mari*, lit. "perishing at heart". "With a fierce face" — *bikaṭa badane*. "Swearing an oath, touched her feet" — an oath made by touching an elder's feet. "I came out the good girl" — *sujana hanu*; "she believed it" — *se pratyaya gela*. The last line is unclear: the last word in the print looks like *bhāga*, but by sense and rhyme (*gela*) it is probably *bhāla* ("good"); *ithe doṣa ihā nā māna* is understood as "there is a fault in this, and you do not admit [it]" — Narahari's playful reproach; another understanding is possible. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-8]: "Aunt" — *māsaisa*: probably the mother's sister (*māsī*) or the mother-in-law's sister (*māsī-śāśuṛī*); later in kirana 7 *pisaisa* (paternal aunt) also occurs. "Pitiless Providence" — *nikaruṇa bidhi*. "Gaura-nagara" — *gaura nāgara*: *nāgara* ("townsman; a refined hero skilled in love") is a common epithet of Krishna in the padas; on the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "Takes hold of the edge of my sari" — *ā̃care dhariyā*. "Conjuring me by her head" — *māthāra śapatha diyā*: a common Bengali oath-entreaty ("by my head, I beg you — don't go"). "Guileless heart" — *kapaṭa-rahita cita*. "Asks about the household affairs" — *puchaye gharera rīta*. "In turmoil" — *ānachāna*. "As is fitting" — *yathocita*. "Better I should leave this body" — *śarīra chāṛiyā di*: a hyperbole of despair. "To get round" — *bhulā'te*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-9]: The verse begins and ends with "॥৮॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a). The Suradhuni is the Ganga. "Surpasses an elephant's" — *kuñjara jini*: a stately gait like an elephant's, a classical comparison. "Love embodied" — *pīriti murutikhāni*. "Inmost [feeling]" — *marama* (*marma*). "The pot on my hip" — *kā̃khera kalasī*: water is carried in a pot on the hip. "Having joyfully given my life and youth to the Beloved of my heart" — *prāṇa-piyāre jībana yaubana sā̃piyā sukhe*, a common formula of the padas for complete self-surrender; see note 3. "Like a forest fire" — *dābānala sama*. "No excuse would work" — *kichu chala nāhi calaye*. "Extraordinary weeping" — *biparīta … kāndana*. "I thought of … entering the waters of the Ganga" — part of the feigned speech, the speaker's ruse. "A stain on the family" — *kalaṅka … kule*. "Utterly bewildered" — the print has *bandya*; probably *dhanda* ("confusion"), which would rhyme with *dvandva*; the reading is unclear. "Not one friend" — *sakhī*: possibly also the address "friend" to the listener. "The misfortunes of your cleverness" — *tuyā cāturīra bālāi* (cf. note 4). Signature: Narahari. Verse 7.9 begins next.

[^07-10]: The stanza begins at the bottom of and ends. The speaker is a young married woman of Nadia (*badhū*, "daughter-in-law, bride"); the sister-in-law (*nanadī*, *nanadinī*) is her husband's sister, living in the same house. "Faithful wife" — *pativratā*; "by her reckoning" — *tāra lekhe*. In the edition the first line has no end mark. "Pride" — printed *darūpa*, taken as *darpa*. "Came true" — *sidhi* (*siddhi*). The Suradhuni is the Ganga. "Under the onslaught of Madana" — *madana-bhare*; Madana is Kamadeva; "her garment slipped", "her body shook" are conventional signs of being overwhelmed in padas of purvaraga; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas on the mood of the nagaris see note 3 of this kirana. "Bear me no grudge" — *doṣa nā la-ibe*. "Caught in this rasa" — *ṭhekinu e rase*. "What is the use of disclosing all this?" — *e sakala kathā bekata karile kāja*: literally "disclosing all this — [what] business", taken as a rhetorical question; the speaker resolves not to betray her sister-in-law's secret, and Narahari praises her for it. "Honour" — *lāja* (literally "shame"). Signature: Narahari.

[^07-11]: "Feel for me" — *bethita* (= *byathita*, "sympathetic"). "Unheard of anywhere in the land" — *deśera bāhira* (literally "outside [the customs of] the land"). The old woman (*buṛī*) is the mother-in-law (cf. 7.11, 7.12). "Through her 'virtues'" — *tāra guṇe*, ironically. "Thorns to the family" — *kulera kā̃ṭā*; possibly a pun: *kul* is both "family" and the thorny jujube tree. "Taunt me with this" — *dey e khõṭā*, i.e. with the speaker's sighs for Gora. "Placed the moon of the sky in my hand" — a proverb for unexpected good fortune. Gaura-raya is Gaura. "Snare" — the print looks like *phānda* (possibly *kānda*). The khanjana is the wagtail, a usual comparison for restless eyes. "No one escapes" — *keha nā bā̃che*. "How many Kamadevas swoon" — *murucha katek kāma*: Kama is Kamadeva, the god of love. The last line is Narahari's consolation; on the speaker's mood see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-12]: "My night turned into a happy dawn" — *kāli suprabhāta haila niśi*. "To blazes with it" — *maruka*, an exclamation of vexation (literally "let it perish"). "To that [far] quarter of the village" — *se pāṛā pāne*. "To have her own way" — *nija jita lāgi*: the reading *jita* ("victory") follows the print; possibly *hita* ("for her own advantage"). The kumuda is the white water lily that opens under the moon and closes by day; the withered bud is the speaker's hint at her longing; Gora's words are addressed to the flower: the moon will rise, the heat of the day will pass. "From the corner of His eyes" — *nayāna-koṇe*. "As if" — *janu*. "I longed to go and touch Him" — the townswoman's fancy; in His lila the Lord did not touch women or look at them (CB, Adi); see note 3. Then comes the ruse before the mother-in-law: the daughter-in-law puts her state down to the quarrel with her sister-in-law and to fatigue. "Giddy" — *bhrami*. "Fanning me with the end of her own cloth" — *āpana basane pabana kari*. "Clever trick" — *cāturī*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-13]: "My brother's house" — i.e. her parental home. "Struck down" — *hata*. "Dharma" — *dharama*, here the duty of a married woman; "modesty" — *sarama*. "All astir" — *kemana kemana karaye hiyā*. "Seeing all this, He smiles once more" — the speaker's perception; on the Lord not looking at the women of Navadvipa and on the position of the tradition see note 3. "Somehow" — *dharame dharame* (literally "by dharma, by dharma" — "by God's grace, safely"). "Restless" — *ānacāna karaye*. Darshan — beholding. The "snake" is a ruse before the mother-in-law (cf. "as if a deadly snake had bitten me", 7.11). "My heart trembled, my courage left me" — in the print a slash stands between *kā̃pila* and *miṭila* (probably a broken comma); *dāpa* — "courage, boldness". "Slid off" — printed *sā̃dhālya*, taken as *sẽdhāla* ("crept in, hid"). "By the power of your feet" — a polite formula: "thanks to your blessings". "Made many vows to Shiva" — *māne śibe*: she promised Shiva offerings if her daughter-in-law stayed well. "Nothing daunts you" — *kichu nā māno* (literally "you heed nothing"). Signature: Narahari.

[^07-14]: The speaker is a young married woman. "Stove" — *unāna* (*unun*), a clay hearth. "Golden-hued" — *kanaka-baraṇa*, lit. "of the colour of gold": the golden bangle reminded her of golden Gaura; Gaura-raya is Gaura (cf. note 11). "Caught hold of me" — *sāmāle*. "Aunt" — *pisaisa*: probably a paternal aunt (*pisī*); cf. *māsaisa* in 7.7 (note 8). Lines 8–10 are the mother-in-law's words. "The daughter-in-law I longed for so much" — *aneka sādhera badhū*. "Exorcist" — printed *rojhā*, i.e. *ojhā*, a charm-healer who treats "possession" and snakebite; the mother-in-law takes the young woman's fainting fits for an illness. Line 10 reads in print *kata dhana byaya karila* ("how much money has been spent"). "To my eyes' content" — *nayāna bhari*, lit. "filling the eyes". "Appeared" — *udaya hailā*, lit. "rose", as of the moon. "Just for a moment" — *bāreka*, "just once". "Crimson feet" — *rāṅgā caraṇa*. "Good fame" — *suyaśa*. "Covering His face with His cloth" — *basana jhā̃piyā mukhe*: granting the elders' request for healing, the Lord does not look at the young woman; cf. Chaitanya-bhagavata (Adi 15): in His lila the Lord did not look at women. On the speaker's perception and the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 3. "Placed His foot upon my breast" — *dharila āmāra buke*, lit. "placed on my breast": the healing touch of the foot that the elders had asked for. "Setting my clothes in order" — *sambari basana*. Nandanacharya is a resident of Navadvipa and an associate of the Lord; Nityananda stayed at his house on arriving in Navadvipa (CB, Madhya-khanda). "Made Gauranga their all" — *gaurāṅge karila sāra*. "Your mother-in-law is fond of you" — *śāśuṛīra prīta*. "None of this would have happened" — *nahita e saba rīta*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-15]: "Dreadful" — *biṣama*. "Having prayed to the Creator" — *bidhi ārādhiyā*: probably ironic — the sister-in-law prays each morning for trouble to befall the young wife. "Her own [friends]" — *āpana gaṇe*. "Counsel" — *mantraṇā*. "Quarrel" — *kandala*. "'Exploits' of your daughter-in-law" — *badhūra pratāpe* (lit. "by the might of"), ironic. "We shall not be able to stay in this land" — *rahite nāribe deśe*: the family would have to leave the village for shame; the end of the line is damaged in print. "Stays on" — *basiyā raya*. "Ill-omened words" — *aśubha bacana*: words that invite misfortune. "Plots your ruin and mine" — *maraṇa cintaye*. "Beyond counting" — *nāhika tāhāra lekhā*. "Dressing herself up" — *beśa banāiyā*. "My whole body burns as if on fire" — *anala jalaye gāya*. "A brahmana's son" — *brāhmaṇa-tanaya*: i.e. Gaura, the son of Jagannatha Mishra — so in the sister-in-law's slander. Line 15 — *bujhinu ihā nā cāhile pāba*: taken as "I understand; [but] how shall I know without looking [myself]?" in view of the next line ("when I see it with my own eyes, then I will believe"); another understanding is possible. "Put to shame" — *paṛiyā lāje*: the mother-in-law did not take her word for it. Line 21: *nabīna aneka(?) cināba(?) puruṣe*; the reading and sense of *aneka cināba* are unclear (perhaps "some [unknown]" or "well-acquainted"); translated by the general sense. "In mother's name" — *māyera nāme*: as if the mother-in-law had sent for him. "Neighbours" — *paṛasabāsī*, *paṛasi*. "The old woman" — *buṛī*, the mother-in-law (cf. note 11). "Entertained a strange man in the house" — *parapuruṣa laiyā bilāsa karilā ghare*; printed *bigāsa*, taken as *bilāsa*. "Oh, she is very virtuous indeed" — *tora badhū baṛa satī baṭe*, a spiteful taunt; *satī* looks like *gatī* in print. "She will make the daughter-in-law's life unbearable" — *badhūra parāṇa badhibe*, lit. "she will slay the daughter-in-law's life": hyperbole. "Wandering along behind" — *pāchete phire*. "Fine, fine!" — *bhāla bhāla*, in angry irony. "Mortified to the core" — *marame mariyā gela*, an idiom of extreme shame. "Gauranga-sundara, my very life" — *prāṇa-gaurāṅga sundare*. "As if she were not there" — *yena thāki nā thākaye ghare*: she hides for shame. "Tying one's own fault in the end of one's sari" — *āpanāra doṣa ā̃cale bā̃dhiyā*: a proverb — keeping one's own guilt knotted in the end of one's sari, one blames others. On the mood of the nagaris, see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-16]: "The wish of my heart" — *manera sādhā*. "Tasting happiness" — *sukha bhuñjāite*. Line 3 is printed *gata dina mena*; *mena* is taken as a misprint (probably *yena* or *mora*) and left untranslated; the reading is unclear.
