"Beloved of my heart" — *parāṇa-piyā*, the address of the padas; see note 3. "Gallery" — *upara-dālāna*, an upper storey with an open gallery. "Drowned" — *majila*. "Fly close to Him" — *nikaṭe uṛiyā yāiye haiyā pākhī*: the speaker's daydream. "Reached my senses" — *nāsāya paśila*, lit. "entered my nostrils". "Lost all composure" — *athira*. "My happiness knew no bounds" — *sukhera nāhika ora*. "The old woman" — the mother-in-law. "Gora turned and looked my way" — the speaker's perception; that the Lord did not look at women — see note 3. "Waters of bliss" — *ānanda-jale*: leave to go for water (to the Ganga, along Gaura's road) is a fresh hope of seeing Him. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-17]: "Friend" — *sajani*. "The tale of my sorrows" — *duḥkhera kāhinī kathā*. "Eased" — *ghucāila*. "Bolted the doors" — *kapāṭa diyā*. "Warily" — *cakita*. "My heart was in turmoil" — *ānacāna kare* (cf. note 13). "A small window" — *alapa gabākṣa*. "The crown of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara*: the most expert in relishing rasa, a usual epithet of Krishna in the padas. "Quite unnoticed" — *ati alakhita*: possibly referring to the speaker ("I, quite unnoticed…"). "Elephant's gait" — *kuñjara-gati* (cf. note 9). "Eyes met eyes" — *nayāne nayāna diyā*; "catching a sign" — *iṅgita pāyā*; "I made it known" — *jānā'nu*: the reading and sense of line 12 are uncertain. Line 13 — *mora pāśe āsi īṣat hāsiyā balilā catura-maṇi*: "the Jewel of the clever" (*catura-maṇi*) is probably Gaura (cf. *rasika-śekhara* above and 7.11, where He speaks to the flower by the window); what He said is not told. All this is the speaker's perception and daydream; for the Gaudiya Vaishnava position (in His lila the Lord did not look at women), see note 3. "One-eyed mother-in-law" — *śāśuṛī kānī*. "Askance" — *terachha haiyā*. The last line is Narahari's joke: had the mother-in-law lost her other eye as well, the young wife would have been better off. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-18]: Verses 7.17–7.28 are not translated. Verse 7.29 begins with the heading "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" and ends with "॥২৯॥". The speaker is a young married woman; her husband is talking with his father, the head of the family. "A good man" — *sujana*. "The power of Ishvara" — *īśvara-śakati*: the father-in-law and the husband see divine power in Nimai Pandita. "Master of the senses, righteous" — *jitendriya dhārmika*: cf. the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15) on Nimai's strict conduct towards women; see note 3. "He bathes in the joy of kirtana" — *bhāsaye kīrtana-sukhe*. "Do not forbid your daughters-in-law" — *nija badhūgaṇe … niṣedha*: the wives of the sons in the father-in-law's house. "I would take upon myself the misfortunes of her whole life" — *janamera bālāi laiyā mari* (cf. note 4); "this woman" (*ei nārī*) is apparently the speaker's wife, i.e. the narrator herself. "We" (*morā*) — the men of the family. Lines 13–14 refer to the yajna-patnis, the wives of the brahmanas: the brahmanas performing a sacrifice did not recognize Krishna and refused Him food, while their wives came to Him and received His mercy (*Bhagavata Purana* 10.23). "Standing in hiding" — *āṛe thāki*. "Could see", "would tell" — *pātu*, *ditu* (conditional forms: *pāitu*, *ditu*). "With the corner of my eyes I would tell Him" — the speaker's longing; see note 3. Gadadhara is Gadadhara Pandita, the Lord's inseparable companion; Nityananda — "the abode of bliss" (*ānandarāśi*). "The Crown of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara* (cf. note 17). "Stand firm" — *parāṇa dhare*, lit. "hold on to one's life". "The moon of Nadia" — printed *nadyāra* (?) *cānda*; the reading of the first word is uncertain. "The snare of Madana" — *madana-phānda*: Gaura's beauty, which captivates the worlds, is compared to the snare of the god of love (cf. 7.10, note 11). "The Lord of life" — *prāṇanātha*, a form of address in the padas; see note 3. "Saying many [things]" — *aneka kaiyā*. "My heart's wish" — *manera sādhā*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-19]: The verse begins and ends with "॥৩০॥". "The Creator has no taste for rasa" — *bidhi arasika*. Lines 3–4 are understood thus: if the Creator had settled one of Gaura's associates in our quarter, Gaura would come here. Murari Gupta is a physician from Shrihatta, a close associate of the Lord in Navadvipa (*Chaitanya-bhagavata*, *Chaitanya-charitamrita*). "Aunt" — *māsaisa* (cf. note 8). "That quarter" — *sepāṛā*. "The women of the house" — *badhūgaṇa*, lit. "daughters-in-law" (in Murari's house). "For the Beloved of their life" — the print seems to read *prāṇapriyā lāgi*; understood as *prāṇa-priya* (Gaura); see note 3. "The young crown prince of Nadia" — *nadīyāra naba-yubarāja*. "A radiance of stars" — *tāraka-chaṭā*. "The splendour of the autumn moon" — *śarada śaśīra ghaṭā*. "All young women are immersed [in that beauty]" — *majaye yubatī-jāti*; on the mood see note 3. "Of a captivating cast" — *manohara chānde*. "I bathed" — *sināinu* (*snāna*). "Husband's elder brother" — *bhāsura*: by Bengali custom a young woman does not appear before him with her face uncovered. "With some students" — *paṛuyāra saha*. "Veil" — *ghũghaṭa*. "Lowering my eyes" — *ā̃khi sambari*. The last lines are Narahari's jest: the shame before the brother-in-law is great, and disclosure of the secret would bring disgrace on her throughout the land. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-20]: The verse number in the edition is "॥৩৯॥", printed directly after "॥৩০॥"; probably a misprint for "৩১"; the edition's numbering is kept (there are no 7.31–7.38). The metre is "সুরত সুরতী ছন্দঃ"; the reading of the second word is uncertain: *suratī* (or *sumatī*). The language of the verse is Brajabuli (*bhaṇata*, *samujhata*, *tahi*). The verse sums up the nagaris' stories of this kirana. "As the heart prompts" — *mana māne*. "Charming" — *rucikārī*. Line 3 — *ati madhuratara tarala hima pulaka bharu aṅga*: understood as a thrill of the utmost sweetness and a "chill" that made their bodies break into gooseflesh (*pulaka*, one of the sattvika-bhavas); the understanding is uncertain. "Supreme delight" — *parama raṅga*. Signature: Narahari.

[^07-21]: The colophon is read in the 1947 edition: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্রচিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে শ্রীনবদ্বীপ-নাগরীনাং চরিত্র-বর্ণনং নাম সপ্তমঃ কিরণঃ ॥". Cf. note 1.


## Kirana 8. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Conversations about Dreams[^08-1]

*Morning in Navadvipa: the nagaris tell one another the dreams of Gaura they saw during the past night: one in a dream anointed Him with sandal paste and adorned Him with garlands in the house of Shrivasa; to another, in a forest bower, the youthful Shyama appeared and before her eyes became Gaura*

*Again, in the morning period — the conversations about dreams and so on of the nagaris of Shri Navadvipa.*

*Raga vibhasha*

**8.1.** Today, at the close of night, at dawn — how much merriment in the circle of friends!
All float in the rasa of Gauranga, laughing and frolicking in many ways.
Some playful one says: "Come now, tell how you spent the night."
Hearing this, they cast off shyness and, drunk with happiness, tell one another in many ways.[^08-2]

*A certain [woman] says — yatha[raga]*

**8.2.** Listen, listen, friend: I shall carefully tell you about the past night.
In the evening, having finished my work, I was sitting at home.
Thinking and thinking of Gora's beauty and virtues — I do not know what came over my heart —
I went with my mother-in-law to the house of Shrivasa Pandita Thakura.
There, among His incomparable associates, Gora sits —
the thief of the treasure of composure of young wives from worthy families, faithful to their husbands.
A radiant golden body, and on it lovely sandal paste, richly applied:
as if Mount Sumeru had been anointed with the ground-up autumn moon.
A garland of malati sways on His neck — like a snare that enchants the worlds;
how many, many hundreds of Madanas swoon at the sight of the contours of His face!
Smiling, smiling, He holds sweet conversation with Gadadhara —
He showers nectar in streams and dispels the longing of the ears.
Ah, ah! When I see that beauty, my heart is not my own.
What can I say? Not for a moment could I gaze my fill at Him with both eyes.
Unfortunate as I am — what can I do? The Creator made me a dependent woman:
for fear of my mother-in-law I could not stay — I came back, barely alive.
With sorrow in my heart I lay down; when my sister-in-law asked, I said to her:
"I am not hungry, do not wake me — I feel somehow unwell."
She, utterly guileless, went away, and I lay there with a troubled heart;
my body languished, oh — sleep came [only] late in the night.
In a dream I went to the house of Shrivasa Thakura and saw Him;
with what heartfelt joy I anointed Gaura's body with fragrant sandal paste!
Fresh, fresh garlands of many flowers I carefully placed around His neck.
The life of Narahari, the Crown of rasikas, on some pretext embraced [me].[^08-3]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.3.** Listen, listen, O friend of my heart — I shall tell you:
such a dream as today's I have never seen.
I was sitting in a forest bower, adorned in many ways;
thinking and thinking [of Him], I had wasted away in body — there was not a trace of joy.
With restless eyes I look all around — I do not know what has happened to me.
And suddenly I see: someone has come to the outer door.
How wondrous — youthful, of kishora age, like rasa embodied;
what can I say of His grandeur as a nagara? His body is the colour of a cloud.
His palms and soles surpass the dawn [in redness], His nails are a host of moons;
the locks and the tilaka on His forehead shine — like a snare that enchants the worlds.
His crown is beautifully tilted, the peacock feather sways incomparably;
around it, drawn by the fragrance of lovely flowers, bees circle — there is no counting them.
Holding the flute to His lower lip, again and again He plays on it —
who in the world, hearing it, could keep composure?
On His neck is a double string of pearls, like the stream of the Suradhuni;
as He walks, the little bells at His waist and the lovely anklets on His feet jingle.
With a graceful play of His brows He came close to me
and jested on so many pretexts — who could understand it?
Smiling, smiling, He looks at me and signals with the corner of His eyes;
by a trick He draws aside my veil — what is He doing! — He draws my very life after Him.
And another wonder: as I watched, that Shyama became Gora —
what can I compare Him to? — the Thief of the hearts of so many faithful young wives.
Slowly, slowly coming near, He sat down beside me
and with sweet, sweet words tries to please [me], hoping for a touch.
In feigned anger I turned my face away — yet my happiness had no bounds.
"Forgive the offence!" He said, and again took hold of the edge of my sari.
At the touch I lost all strength and was immersed together with Him [in bliss].
Narahari's Lord of life, the most expert rasika, fulfilled what was in my heart.[^08-4]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.4.** Tonight I saw a dream, all full of happiness, friend.
You are the most blessed in the world: listen, I shall tell you about it.
Each of you adorned herself, and, restless, you sat in seclusion;
singing and singing Gora's virtues, you passed [a whole] watch of the night.
Knowing the time had come, you sent a messenger to where Govinda abides;
she went very quickly and told Gauranga everything.
Then she hurried back to you, agitated;
hearing from her lips the news of the Beloved of your life, you all set out running.
From afar you saw the sweetness of Gora's form and were enchanted;
entering the forest bower, you came close to the Lord of your life.
With great affection He took each of you by the hand and seated you;
smiling, smiling, how many jests you exchanged with one another!
The most expert Gora loosed a fierce arrow from the corner of His eyes;
struck by it, you could not hold on to the pride of youth.
The serpents of the brows of all of you bit [Him] again and again —
and all the composure the moon of Nadia had seemed to leave at once.
Opening both arms, He embraces — incomparable is His tenderness.
In great joy I gave [Him] a sign; understanding it, He was enraptured all the more.
What was in your hearts the King of rasikas, as it were, fulfilled.
Narahari says: why are you shy to tell your own story?[^08-5]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.5.** Listen, listen, friend: in a dream I saw Gora in the grove of bowers.
Gazing at the path by which you [were to come], in His distress He sheds tears from His eyes.
Hearing from my lips that you were coming, how He entreated me!
Seeing His state, in great haste I came and told you.
Hearing this, you joyfully adorned yourself and met [Him] in the bower;
welcoming [you] from afar, the King of rasikas took [you] by the hand and clasped [you] in His arms.
How much merriment arose! — and on some pretext you became a manini.
Narahari's Lord entreats [you] — and then I woke, distraught.[^08-6]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.6.** Listen, listen, O [friend], I shall tell you of the night's dream.
I went to the house of Shrivasa Pandita Thakura, where Gaura [was].
How lovely is the courtyard of Shrivasa! — seeing it, my eyes were refreshed.
With joy in my heart I stood in a secluded spot, keeping my dharma with composure.
There the associates [of the Lord], happy at heart, taking khol and karatalas,
sing with sweet, nectarean voices, utterly enraptured.
In the midst of that circle the son of Shachi shines — what wondrous attire!
His glossy curling hair is beautifully arranged with flowers of many kinds.
At His ears graceful earrings sway — incomparable is the radiance of His cheeks.
On His forehead are dots of fragrant sandal, like a host of autumn moons.
On His very broad, ample chest necklaces of many kinds swing;
the new ornaments He has put on shine — what can I compare Him to?
With graceful movements of His arms the Most Expert One dances — lovely is the motion of His feet.
He calls out "Hari, Hari!" — hearing it, no one keeps composure.
I do not know what feeling arose in Him: His body trembles again and again,
from both eyes streams flow ceaselessly, like the current of a river.
Heaving deep sighs, distraught, He fell to the ground
and rolls like a golden lotus — no one can hold Him.
Seeing that, my heart trembled and I gave tilanjali to shame;
crying "What has happened? What has happened?", I wept aloud, beside myself.
At that moment my sleep broke; waking, I sat up where I lay.
"What is it, what is it?" — all came running and ask why I am weeping.
Whom could I tell, and what? With grief in my heart I fell asleep again, like a chataki bird.
And again I dreamt: Gora came into my courtyard.
"Come in, come in, friend!" I said, and quickly seated [Him] on a couch.
Knowing He was weary, I carefully fanned [Him] with the edge of my sari.
Preparing fresh tambula, smiling, I offered it to that moon-face —
and with Narahari's Lord of life I lay down [to rest] with joy in my heart.[^08-7]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.7.** Listen, listen, O [friend]: I shall tell the night's dream that is in my mind.
The people of the world went mad over the virtues of Gaurangachandra.
The evil-minded, the crooked, the deceitful, the slanderers — as many as there were —
all gave up their perverse nature and became followers of Gaura.
Seeing many such things, I woke once, friend;
falling asleep again, I saw another wonder — I shall tell you all of it.
As Shyama on the bank of the Yamuna performed the rasa-vilasa and so on,
so Gora on the bank of the Suradhuni created an abode of rasa.
Casting off shame, [the honour of] family and fear, all the nagaris of Nadia,
mounted on the chariot of their longings, go in throngs, evading hundreds of thorns.
I, leaving the housework, was very restless to go there,
but suddenly my husband came and quickly shut the door of the house.
I fell into a plight — whom could I tell, and what? — and my distress grew.
In my heart it was thus: life will not remain… shall I see the Beloved again?
At that moment my clever [breeze], as if by chance, threw open the door,
and, by good fortune, softly, softly I stepped outside the house.
Overjoyed, I ran without turning to look at anyone —
as a young bird flies away from an iron cage.
Arriving quickly, I filled my eyes gazing at Gaura-raya:
He shines splendidly amid a circle of young women — what can I compare Him to?
Instruments of many kinds play on all sides — there is no limit to the happiness;
the women sing with sweet voices, showering streams of nectar.
Drunk with the honey of that lotus-face, I too joyfully began to dance.
Narahari's Lord, seeing that, smiled and held me to His breast.[^08-8]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.8.** Listen, friend, to the night's dream — I tell it, having cast off shyness.
Softly, softly Gora enters the chamber, warily glancing on all sides.
Smiling, smiling, He came to the head of the bed and sat down;
with His own hand He touched my lips — and floats in an ocean of happiness.
Enraptured, He speaks the sweetest words in many ways, under the guise of jest;
twining His arms [about me] and holding [me] to His heart, He is drenched with tears from His eyes.
In His heart He fears to part from His treasure even for a moment.
The Beloved of Narahari's life is love embodied: what more can I say?[^08-9]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.9.** Listen, listen to the night's dream, friend: casting off shyness, I tell you.
At daybreak, in lovely attire, Gora came to me.
Looking at that moon-face, I said: "Why have You come running?
Where You happily spent the night — go there quickly and meet [her] there.
The plain signs cannot be hidden: with her You passed the night awake."
Hearing this, He swears hundreds of oaths; hoping for a touch, He entreats me.
At that moment my sleep broke — Narahari knows what state I was in.[^08-10]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.10.** Listen, listen, O friend! I shall tell you the night's dream.
With great effort the moon of Nadia came and slipped into the house.
At that moment my cruel sister-in-law, standing at the door, says:
"You dally with another man — and are you not afraid?
Very well, very well: when brother comes in the morning, I shall tell him all this.
I shall leave, taking my honour with me — I will not stay in this sinful house!"
Hearing this, I thought in fear: "When the night is over,
I do not know what harsh thing my husband will do when he comes home.
Let everyone revile me as much as they like — that will not pain me;
[but] lest wicked people speak ill of the Beloved of my life!
If the Creator makes this known, it will be terrible:
for the rest of my life I shall never again see the moon of Nadia.
He will never come towards our quarter, will not remember me.
I am unfortunate — now I know it for certain; otherwise why would it be so?"
Saying this, I wept, distraught, repeating that name again and again.
Narahari knows: I escaped with my life only by quickly coming to my senses.[^08-11]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.11.** Friend, listen to my dream of the night — it is a very funny story!
To guard me, my sister-in-law lay down where my bed is.
He, the moon of Nadiya, came and entered, wholly in the sway of Madana,
and, mistaking her for me, sat down on my sister-in-law's couch.
Gently, rejoicing, He touched her chin with His hand, tender as a sprout.
My sister-in-law woke, started up with a shudder, and looks all around.
She says to me: "Wake up, wake up quickly — a thief has got into the house!"
Hearing this, Gora ran off in fright and stood at a distance.
Hard on His heels the fierce sister-in-law ran after Him, scolding;
running some way, she caught Him — the Beloved of my heart could not escape.
Drunk with the pride of youth, she seized Him firmly by both hands
and, again and again speaking bitter words, leads [Him] into the house.
Gora, young, of kishora age, full of rasa, looked at my sister-in-law,
clasped her in His arms, overcame her — and did what was in His mind.
Not finding me, the Jewel of virtues grew sad.
And my sister-in-law, not herself, came back to me.
Looking at her, I asked: "Why is there such joy in your heart?"
She, with downcast face, says: "I have fallen into the hands of a terrible thief.
Keep it secret — or in the morning the clamour of scandal will spread through the world."
Narahari is witness: while I was consoling her, sleep left my eyes.[^08-12]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.12.** Listen, friend, to my dream: the Lord of my heart, the king of rasikas,
yesterday entered [my] house unnoticed, a blanket wrapped about His body.
Seeing this, I asked with a soft smile: "Why have You dressed like this?"
The Beloved says: "Lest your sister-in-law recognize Me tomorrow."
Much He said in this way; hearing it, I covered my mouth with the end of my cloth
and, taking the Lord of my life by His lovely hand, joyfully seated Him on the couch.
What can I say of the sweetness of His face at that moment? It seemed to me
as if a mass of golden lotuses had blossomed in the waters of the Kalindi.
Seeing this, I took heart and pulled that blanket off Him —
as if the autumn moon had come out from behind dense clouds.
At that very moment my mother-in-law asks: "What is that light in the room?"
Hearing this, my whole body trembled — my heart all but flew away.
Hurrying out, I stood in the doorway, looking about with fear in my heart,
and, plucking up courage, began to speak clever words to her:
"You know I keep the vow of Chandra-vrata — I observe it with care.
Today he, pleased with my worship, has graciously appeared to me.
He wishes to grant a boon — and what boon to ask I do not know at all.
Whatever you yourself say, that I will take; only do not come in here."
Hearing this, she, delighted, says softly and with great care:
"Ask for wealth, long life and well-being for everyone — take that much."
Hearing this, [I] quickly went back into the room in great joy,
sat beside the Beloved and wiped His body with the end of my cloth.
Narahari's Lord of life — with what tenderness He embraced me!
And at that moment sleep broke — in separation I was flooded with tears.[^08-13]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.13.** Listen, listen, I shall tell you: in a dream He, the moon of Nadiya,
came smiling and sat beside me — like a mass of golden lotuses.
He says to me: "Today, with your own hands, adorn Me."
What happiness, hearing those clever words! What can I say?
The merriment grew. That hair by which the young women of Nadiya are enchanted
I anointed with many fragrant oils, tied in a graceful slanting knot and adorned with flowers.
On His forehead I drew a lovely dot of sandal paste, like a beautiful moon;
in His ears I placed earrings — they flash [like] the sun; with what can they be compared?
Wiping His lotus face, steeped in smiles, and seeing His brows — a row of bees,
gazing eye into eye, rapt in joy, I put a pearl in His nose.
His graceful arms, surpassing an elephant's trunk, steal away composure and dharma;
putting many ornaments on them, I lovingly placed bangles on His wrists as well.
On His broad chest I arranged a necklace — matchless is the beauty of His waist!
Around His hips I put a belt of little bells — the radiance of His knees shines.
On the feet of Narahari's Beloved I carefully put anklets —
and at that moment sleep broke: I could not gaze my fill [at Him].[^08-14]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.14.** Listen, listen, friend of my heart!
Casting off shyness, I shall tell you all my dream of the night.
Smiling, smiling, floating in happiness, that playful One — with what tenderness —
opened both arms and with many graceful gestures quickly embraces me!
I cannot keep still: trembling, my body quivers like lightning;
like a greedy bee, rapt with Madana, He kisses my face.
That moon-face became beautifully adorned with kohl, kunkuma and sindura —
seeing this, I teased Him; hearing it, the Beloved grew shy.
To my heart's content I again wipe that moon-face, smiling a little…
[…]
My heart flew away — what to do? — the Beloved hid in fear.
At that moment sleep broke; awake — my heart is pounding.
When I fell asleep again, that young Nagara adorns me:
He decked my parting with sindura and with such care bound up my hair.
When He went to put a bodice on my breast, I said: "Why are You putting a bodice [on me]?"
The Beloved, laughing, says: "Have you no man's attire in mind?"
What more can I say? Putting a ring in my nose, He, quite unable to restrain Himself,
at once laid me upon His broad chest.
In many ways He tells stories of rasa — as if pouring out nectar.
Narahari's Lord — love embodied, like the Enchanter of young women.[^08-15]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.15.** What can I say? How He jests with me in my dream — Gora, my crown of rasikas!
What eyes! His sidelong glances are irresistible — the Thief of the treasure of life and youth.
Smiling a sweet, sweet smile, floating in happiness, He puts His face to my face and embraces [me];
His body thrills in the waves of Madana — what talk of rasa He does not start!
Again and again swinging the ring in my nose — I do not know in what rasa He is lost —
covering [my] breast with His palm, He says again and again: "These two golden lotuses are Mine."
He wants to remove all the coverings from [my] body; when I restrain Him, His play only grows.
The Beloved of Narahari's heart is so shameless — He breaks the vows of young women.[^08-16]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.16.** In my dream the Beloved sat on my couch — once I glanced [at Him] from the corner of my eye.
Gora, love embodied — with what tenderness He regards Himself as subject [to me]!
With that moon-face He tells me again and again: "You are dearer to Me than life."
Saying this, He embraces [me] and floats in happiness — and I am overcome with shyness.
Preparing tambula, He places it in my mouth and, lost in Madana, gazes [at me];
again with His tender hand He touches my lips — and gives His very life for them.
Smiling a sweet, sweet smile, He showers nectar — how He prizes the fine art of rasa!
The Beloved of Narahari's heart — the darling of the heart, the one who enchants young women.[^08-17]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**8.17.** Hear my dream: gazing and gazing at me, Gora, the thief of young women's hearts,
plays with His eyes that surpass a pair of wagtails — I do not know in what rasa He is lost.
Smiling, smiling, He comes, sits down near me and with His own hand draws back [my] veil;
saying "Ah, how lovely!" He touches [my] chin and looks at [my] face again and again.
What love is His! In His mind it is this — to make [me] a necklace and wear [me] round His neck.
At the touch of body to body, how the merriment grows! Young Madana is His companion.
And how much art of rasa in His kiss — what can I say? Never have I heard of such.
Where did the Beloved of Narahari's heart learn it? How many words of rasa He knows![^08-18]

*And another — yatharaga*

**8.18.** O friend! My Gora is a bee of rasa,
the thief of the honey of the lotus faces of ever new young women — who knows His secret?
Coming in a dream, He sat down near me and, smiling, smiling, speaks.
I do not know how He pours out that nectarean rasa — it dispels the longing of ears and heart.
With what tenderness He touches my chin — and on some pretext, what graceful gestures He makes!
Lips pressed to lips, never moving His eyes away, He covers [my] breast with His palm.
He holds heart to heart — what more can I say? — His golden body trembles again and again.
The Beloved of Narahari's heart — what does He not know! His love is an ocean of happiness.[^08-19]

*Yatharaga*

**8.19.** As they were telling their dreams, a wave of prema rose:
such is that matchless love — no one could keep her composure.
One says: "Ah, the Creator made [me] a woman only to make me suffer:
never could I gaze my fill at such a moon — Gora."
Another says: "Ah, being a woman, the wish of my heart is not fulfilled.
I shall use all kinds of cunning and be rid of this fear of the elders."
Another says: "Ah, a plague on these elders — what is there to fear?
For Gaurasundara, the treasure of my life, I shall surely leave my home."
Another says: "Ah, the people of Nadiya are so spiteful:
I never even see the Lord of my life, and still they say so many evil things."
Another says: "Ah, in the town of Nadiya there will be talk of scandal.
I shall pay no heed to it: I shall keep the Beloved in my heart and be rid of my pain."
Another says: "Ah, day and night there is one wish in my heart:
let me surely be slandered — [together] with Shri Shachinandana."
Another says: "Ah, say what you will — I can stay no longer:
without Him my heart is restless; tell me, what am I to do?"
Another says: "Ah, consigning this shame before the family to the fire,
come, come — let us go now and quickly meet the Lord of our life!"
Another says: "Look, what is this? Ah, my left eye is twitching!"
Narahari says: why worry? All these are companions of good fortune.[^08-20]

*Yatharaga*

**8.20.** At dawn, having seen many auspicious signs, the young women
partly forgot the pain of their hearts, and joy grew in their minds.
One says: "Ah, I have understood: today the Creator has become gracious;
whatever desire each of us has — all of it will be fulfilled."
Another says: "Ah, day after day I worship this Jahnavi;
by her blessing I shall surely attain the Lord of my life — know this."
Another says: "Ah, with great care I worship Gauri every day;
she will free [me] from sorrow — I shall meet Gauranga, the Lord of my life."
Another says: "Ah, I worship Bhanu in many ways;
by the power of his mercy I shall soothe my heart — do not be anxious in your mind."
Another says: "If today, without hindrance, I see the Beloved of my life,
then I shall carefully worship Budo-Shiva, offering various gifts."
Another says: "I feel as if I shall meet Him right now."
Thus, in the rapture of prema, they say so much to one another;
for the darshan of Shri Gaurasundara the hearts of all are restless.
Narahari says: ah, ah! I would take upon myself the misfortunes of such prema![^08-21]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**8.21.** The ways of the nagaris of Nadia — who has the power to describe them?
Without Gora their minds know nothing else; Gora is their all, their life and soul.
The tales of the moon Gaura are a well of nectarean rasa; His incomparable qualities are the water in it.
Narahari always hopes for this one thing: may the deeds of the nagaris shine forth in his heart.[^08-22]

*Thus [ends] the eighth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Conversations about Dreams", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^08-23]

[^08-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon at the end of the kirana: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে স্বপ্নপ্রসঙ্গ-বর্ণনং নামাষ্টমঃ কিরণঃ ॥৮॥" — "the eighth kirana, called 'The Description of the Conversations about Dreams' (*svapna-prasaṅga*), in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the conversations about dreams and so on of the nagaris of Shri Navadvipa" Kirana 8 comprises verses 8.1–8.21. The nagaris are the young married women of Navadvipa (see notes 1 and 3 of kirana 7).

[^08-2]: Vibhasha is a morning raga (cf. 7.1). Each line of the verse consists of two halves, separated in print by "।". "Merriment" — *kautuka*. "In the circle of friends" — *sakhīra mele*. "Float in the rasa of Gauranga" — *gaurāṅgera rase bhāse*. "Playful one" — *raṅginī*. "Come now" — the print has *bhāga*; by sense and rhyme (*bala*) probably *bhāla* ("well then, come now"; cf. note 7 of kirana 7, the same case); the reading is unclear. "Spent the night" — *bañcile* (cf. note 5 of kirana 7). "Drunk with happiness" — *sukhe māti*. No signature.

[^08-3]: The heading in print is "কাচিদাহ—যথা" (*kācid āha — yathā*), without the word "raga"; understood as an abbreviation of *yathārāga*. Here a new kind of the nagaris' mood begins — dreams in which they serve Gaura and He responds to them with intimacy. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati and the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women or even look at them), reject *gaura-nagari-vada*, the worship of Gaura as an enjoying lover (*nāgara*) and of oneself as His nagari: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer. The nagaris' dreams and longings in the poem are understood as a glorification of Gaura's irresistible beauty and of the prema it awakens in hearts; in waking life Gaura here sits among His associates and converses with Gadadhara (cf. the main note of kirana 7, note 3). Further descriptions of the nagaris' dreams refer back to this note. Shrivasa Pandita (Shrivasa Thakura) is an associate of the Lord in whose house the nightly kirtanas took place (*Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Madhya-khanda). "The thief of the treasure of composure" — *dhairaja-ratana-corā*. "From worthy families, faithful to their husbands" — *kulabatī satī*. "As if Mount Sumeru had been anointed with the ground-up autumn moon" — *sumeru parbbata lepiyāche janu bā̃ṭiyā śarada śaśī*: white sandal paste on the golden body. Malati is jasmine. Line 9 ends in print with "॥" instead of "।". "How many hundreds of Madanas swoon" — *kata kata śata madana murache* (cf. 7.10, note 11). "The contours of His face" — *badana-chānda*. "The longing of the ears" — *śrabaṇa-byathā*: the ears pine when they do not hear Him. "My heart is not my own" — *parāṇa kemana kare*. "Could not" — *pālũ*. "A dependent woman" — *parabaśa nārī*. "Barely alive" — *parāṇe mari*, lit. "perishing at heart" (cf. note 7 of kirana 7). Sister-in-law — *nanada*. "I feel somehow unwell" — *gā mora kemana kare* (cf. note 6 of kirana 7). "Languished" — *ānachāna kare* (cf. note 8 of kirana 7). "On some pretext embraced" — *āliṅgana kaila chale*: all this is in the speaker's dream. "The life of Narahari" — *narahari prāṇa*: a signature formula, Gaura is Narahari's life. "The Crown of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara*.

[^08-4]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" (*anyā ca*, "and another"). The verse begins and ends with "॥৩॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a). "Friend of my heart" — *parāṇa sajani*. "Forest bower" — *nikuñja-bhavana*, the usual meeting place of Radha and Krishna in the padas. "Wasted away in body" — *deha kṣīṇa*: from separation. "Someone" — *janeka*. The description of the visitor (kishora age, a body the colour of a cloud, a peacock feather on the crown, the flute, bells at the waist, anklets) is the form of Shyama, Krishna of Vraja; then "that Shyama became Gora" (*se śyāma haila gorā*): in the speaker's dream Krishna turns into Gaura — Narahari shows that Gaura is that same Krishna (cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 1.5: Krishna who has assumed the mood and complexion of Radha). "His grandeur as a nagara" — *nāgara garimā*: *nāgara* is an epithet of Krishna in the padas (see note 3). "His nails are a host of moons" — *nakhara-nicaya cānda*. "Locks" — *alakā*. "His crown is tilted" — *cūṛāra ṭālani*: the crown (*cūṛā*) set aslant. "A double string of pearls" — *dothari mukutāra mālā*. The Suradhuni is the Ganga. "Signals with the corner of His eyes" — *ṭhāraye ā̃khira koṇe*. "By a trick He draws aside my veil" — *ghucaye ghũghaṭa kapaṭe*. "He draws my very life after Him" — *parāṇa sahite ṭāne*. "Thief of the hearts" — *parāṇa-corā*. "Hoping for a touch" — *aṅgera paraśa āśe*. "Feigned anger" — *michā krodhe*: mana, feigned offence, common in the padas. "Edge of my sari" — *ā̃care*. "Was immersed together with Him [in bliss]" — *majila uhāra sane*. "Fulfilled what was in my heart" — *kaila ye āchila mane*. All this is the speaker's dream and longing, modelled on the padas of Krishna-lila; on the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "Narahari's Lord of life" — *narahari prāṇapati*; "the most expert rasika" — *surasika*. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-5]: The heading, as for 8.3, is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" ("and another — yatharaga"). The verse is wholly. The speaker tells her friends their own dream: in her dream she saw their meeting with Gaura. "Tonight" — *ājuka rajanī*. "Each of you adorned herself" — *nija nija beśa biraci*. "Watch of the night" — *prahara*, about three hours. "Messenger" — *dūtī*, the go-between of the padas. "To where Govinda abides" — *gobinda āchena yathā*: understood as Gaura (Govinda is a name of Krishna; the messenger goes to Govinda and speaks to Gauranga); less probably Gaura's servant Govinda; noted for checking. "Beloved of your life" — *prāṇa-priya*; "Lord of your life" — *prāṇanātha* (see note 3). "Forest bower" — *nikuñja-bhavana* (cf. 8.3). "A fierce arrow" — *biṣama bāṇa*: the arrow of a glance, like Madana's. "The pride of youth" — *yauvana māna*. "The serpents of the brows" — *bhuru-bhujaṅgame*: curved brows like serpents, a figure for piercing glances. "The moon of Nadia" — *nadīyā-cānda*. "Tenderness" — *leha* (= *sneha*). "Gave a sign" — *ṭhārinu*. "King of rasikas" — *rasika-rāja*. The end of the last line is rubbed in print: "বাসিহ লাজ" (*bāsiha lāja*, "you are shy") — by rhyme with *rasikarāja*; the reading is unclear. All this is the speaker's dream; on the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-6]: The verse is. In the dream the speaker is a go-between (*dūtī*, cf. note 5): she brings her friend to Gora — modelled on the padas of Radha's abhisara. "Grove of bowers" — *nikuñja-kānana*. "Sheds tears" — *jharaye locana lorā*. "Entreated" — *sādhila*. "Met" — *bheṭilā*. "Welcoming from afar" — *dūrete ādari*. "Clasped in His arms" — *kore karila*, lit. "took onto His lap, into His arms". A manini is a heroine full of mana, feigned offence (cf. note 4). "Narahari's Lord" — *narahari pahũ*. "I woke, distraught" — *jāgi biyākula āmi*. On the mood see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-7]: The verse begins and ends with "॥৬॥". In the first dream the speaker sees the nightly kirtana in Shrivasa's courtyard (cf. 8.2 and note 3) and Gaura's ecstasy. "My eyes were refreshed" — *juṛāla ā̃khi*, lit. "my eyes were cooled". "Keeping my dharma with composure" — *dhairaje dharama rākhi*: standing apart, observing propriety. Khol (mridanga) and karatalas (hand cymbals) are the instruments of kirtana. "Enraptured" — *unamata* (= *unmatta*). "Glossy curling hair" — *cikaṇa cā̃cara keśa*. "A host of autumn moons" — *śarada śaśīra ghaṭā*. "Very broad, ample" — in print the first word looks like *mṛhatara*; probably *bṛhatara* ("very broad"); the reading is unclear. "The Most Expert One" — *sucatura*. Trembling, streams of tears and falling to the ground are signs of Gaura's ecstasy of prema while singing the holy names (cf. *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Madhya-khanda). "Rolls" — *gaṛi yāya*. "I gave tilanjali to shame" — *lāje tilāñjali dinu*: I gave up shame entirely (cf. note 3 of kirana 7). "Beside myself" — *bikala*. "Where I lay" — *śayana yathā*. "Like a chataki bird" — *cātakī-pārā*: the chataka, by tradition, drinks only rainwater and pines for the cloud — a figure for longing for Gaura. In the second dream Gora comes to her courtyard. "Friend" — *bandhu*, an address to the beloved in the padas. "Couch" — *pālaṅka*. "Edge of my sari" — *ā̃care*. Tambula is betel (a leaf with nut and spices) offered to a guest. "Lay down [to rest]" — *śutinu*: all this is the speaker's dream; on the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "Narahari's Lord of life" — *narahari-prāṇanātha*. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-8]: The verse begins and continues: in the 1947 edition repeat (see note 1); the join was — the last line of, "মনোরথে চড়ি চলে যূথে যূথে এড়ায়া কণ্টক শত॥", is continued by the first line of, "গৃহ কাজ ত্যজি মু বড় চঞ্চল তথা যাইবার তরে।", with nothing missing; the verse ends with "॥৭॥". The first dream is of how all, even the hostile, become followers of Gaura (cf. the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* on the transformation of the slanderers and of Jagai and Madhai). "Gaurangachandra" — *gaurāṅga-cānda*. "Perverse nature" — *biparīta svabhāva*. The second dream: "rasa-vilasa" — *rāsa-bilāsa*, Krishna's rasa-lila with the gopis on the bank of the Yamuna; in the speaker's dream Gora on the bank of the Suradhuni (the Ganga) creates an "abode of rasa" (*rasera dhāma*). This is a dream and longing of the nagaris modelled on the rasa-lila; the Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept such a lila of Gaura with women — see note 3. "Mounted on the chariot of their longings" — *manorathe caṛi*: *manoratha* means both "desire" and "chariot of the mind". "Evading hundreds of thorns" — *eṛāyā kaṇṭaka śata*: obstacles raised by the household. "Shall I see the Beloved again?" — the print has *piyāre punahũ dekhite pāinu*, lit. "[if only] I could see the Beloved again"; understood as a wish-question. "My clever [breeze]" — *catura āmāra bā*: *bā* is understood as "wind" (*bāya*, *bāyu*); the reading is unclear. "By good fortune" — *dharame dharame* (an idiom). Gaura-raya is Gaura (cf. note 11 of kirana 7). "Held me to His breast" — *karila buke*. "Narahari's Lord" — *narahari-nātha*. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-9]: The verse is. "Having cast off shyness" — *nilajī haiyā*. "Chamber" — *mandira*, an inner room. "Warily" — *cakite*. "Head of the bed" — *sithāna-pāśe*. "Floats in an ocean of happiness" — *sukhera sāyare bhāse*. "Twining His arms" — *bhuje bhuja diyā*. "Holding to His heart" — *hiyā mājhe rākhi*. "Drenched with tears" — *bhijaye ā̃khira jale*: tears of prema. The seventh line is partly rubbed in print ("আপনার মনে মানে পাছু নিধি তিলেক ছাড়াতে তার", *pāchu nidhi*): understood as "He fears (*māne*) lest (*pāche*) He be parted from His treasure (*nidhi*) even for a moment (*tileka*)"; the reading is unclear. "Love embodied" — *pīritera murati*. "The Beloved of Narahari's life" — *narahari-prāṇapiyā*. All this is the speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-10]: The verse is; each printed line consists of two short halves ("।" in the middle, "॥" at the end). A new kind of the nagaris' mood — a dream in the spirit of the khandita: the heroine reproaches the beloved who comes at dawn with the marks of a night spent with another (a heroine type of the padas, cf. Radha and Krishna). On the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "The plain signs" — *bekata rīti*. "With her" — *tā saha*. "Hoping for a touch" — *paraśera āśe* (cf. 8.3). "What state I was in" — *ye daśā haila*. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-11]: "Slipped into" — *sāndhāila* (in print it looks like *sāṅkāila*). Sister-in-law — *nanadī* (cf. note 3). "You dally with another man" — *para puruṣera sane bilasaha*: the sister-in-law's accusation. "Brother" — the speaker's husband. "Harsh thing" — *biparīta kriyā*. "Revile" — *gañjanā*. "Wicked people" — *pāpa loka*. "Speak ill" — *kalaṅka kathā*, lit. "words of disgrace": the speaker fears not for herself but for Gaura's good name. "The Creator" — the print has *rihi*, probably a misprint for *bihi* (= *bidhi*, Brajabuli); the reading is unclear. "For the rest of my life" — *janamera mata*. "Towards our quarter" — *e pāṛāra pāne*. "That name" — *se nāma*: Gaura's name. "I escaped with my life only by quickly coming to my senses" — *prāṇe bā̃cāinu turite cetana pāyā*: waking, she realised it was only a dream. On the mood see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-12]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" (*anyā ca*, "and another"). The verse begins with its heading: ends with verse 8.10, "॥১০॥". "To guard me" — *āgulite*. "Wholly in the sway of Madana" — *adhika madana-bhare*; Madana is the god of love, Kamadeva (cf. 8.2). "Mistaking her for me" — *āmāra bharame*. "With His hand, tender as a sprout" — *kara-pallabe*. "Started up with a shudder" — *ghana camaki*. "Has got into" — *sāmāila*. "Beloved of my heart" — *parāṇa-piyā*. "Drunk with the pride of youth" — *yaubana-garabe māti*. "Bitter words" — *kaṭu bāṇī*. "Clasped her in His arms" — *bā̃dhi bhuja-pāśe*, lit. "binding [her] in the noose of His arms"; "overcame" — *kari parājaya*. The humour of the verse: the strict sister-in-law who was guarding the young wife is herself conquered by Gaura — hence her joy and her words about the "terrible thief" (*biṣama cora*), the stealer of hearts. All this is the speaker's dream; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 3. "Jewel of virtues" — *guṇamaṇi*. "Grew sad" — *bimana*. "Not herself" — *abaśa*. "The clamour of scandal" — the last word of line 19 is damaged in print (*dhum…*); from the rhyme with *ghuma* it is read *kalaṅka-dhuma*, "the uproar, talk of scandal"; reading unclear. "Narahari is witness" — in print *narahari sāthī* ("companion"); taken as *sākhī* (*sākṣī*, "witness"); reading uncertain (cf. 8.19). Signature: Narahari.

[^08-13]: "King of rasikas" — *rasika rāya*. "Unnoticed" — *alakhita*. "A blanket wrapped about His body" — *kambala uṛiyā gāya*: Gaura comes wrapped up so that His golden radiance will not betray Him. "Tomorrow" — *kāli*: the word means both "yesterday" (as in line 2) and "tomorrow"; perhaps an allusion to the dream of 8.11. "Covered my mouth with the end of my cloth" — *basana jhā̃piyā mukhe*: from laughter. "It seemed to me" — *manete bāsi*. The Kalindi is the Yamuna with its dark waters: Gaura's golden face in the dark blanket. "Took heart" — *dhari dhṛti*. At the end of line 9 the print has *mena*; taken as *yena* (as at the end of line 10) or *hena*; reading unclear. Chandra-vrata is a vow of worship of the moon-god (Chandra); the speaker passes off Gaura's radiance as the appearance of the moon-god, pleased with her vow. "Whatever you … I will take" — *āpane ye kaha tāhā lei*: the respectful *āpane* is addressed to the mother-in-law. "Take that much" — *eteka leyaha cāyā*. "[I] quickly went back into the room" — *ghare sāmāila*: the verb is in the third person; taken as referring to the speaker (another understanding is possible: the mother-in-law went off to her own room). "Embraced me" — *karila kole*, lit. "took [me] on His lap". "In separation" — *bicchede*. The speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: "Narahari's Lord of life".

[^08-14]: "Moon of Nadiya" — *nadyāra śaśī* (print indistinct). "A mass of golden lotuses" — *hemāmbuja rāśi*. "Adorn Me" — *mora beśa banāoha*: the service of adorning (*beśa*) is a frequent motif of the padas. "Clever words" — *cāturī bacana*. "Merriment" — *kautuka*. "That hair by which the young women of Nadiya are enchanted" — *nadīyāra naba yubatī bhule ye cule*. "In a graceful slanting knot" — *bā̃kā chā̃nde*: the hair tied to one side. "Like a beautiful moon" — *sucandrera prāya*. "Flash [like] the sun" — *jhalake bhānu*. "A row of bees" — *bhṛṅga-pā̃ti*. "Gazing eye into eye" — *ā̃khe ā̃khi diyā*; understanding uncertain. "Steal away composure and dharma" — *dhairaja dharama hare*. "Bangles" — *balayā*. "Matchless is the beauty of His waist" — *atula udara śobhā*, lit. "of the belly". "Belt of little bells" — *kiṅkiṇī*; "the radiance of His knees shines" — *lasaye jānura ābhā*. "I could not gaze my fill" — *dekhite nā pānu nayana bhari*. The speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: "Narahari's Beloved" (*narahari priya*).

[^08-15]: The first line is a refrain. "Casting off shyness" — *nilajī haiyā*. "Playful One" — *raṅgiyā*. "Graceful gestures" — *bhaṅgī*. "Trembling" — *tharahari*. "Rapt with Madana" — *madane māti*; "my face" — *majhu mukha* (print unclear; possibly *madhu mukha*, "honeyed face"). "With kohl, kunkuma and sindura" — *kājara kuṅkuma sindure*: marks from the speaker's face have remained on Gaura's face — hence her teasing. Line 9 stands alone, without its paired line, and after it the print has a row of asterisks: apparently a gap in the manuscript (rendered "[…]"); hence it is unclear what frightened the Beloved in the next line. "My heart is pounding" — *hiyā dhakadhaka kare*. "Young Nagara" — *naba nāgara*; see note 3. "Adorns me" — *karaye āmāra beśa*. "Parting" — in print *sitira*, = *sĩthira*. "Bodice" — *kā̃culi* (*kā̃cali*). Lines 14–15 are unclear: "Have you no man's attire in mind?" (*puruṣera beśa nāhi ki tomāra mane*) — perhaps a jest of Gaura's or an allusion to the exchange of attire (a motif of the padas on Radha and Krishna); understanding uncertain. "Nose-ring" — *besara*. "Quite unable to restrain Himself" — *sucañcala haiyā*. "Stories of rasa" — *rasa-kāhinī*. "Like" — at the end of the last line the print has *mena*, as in 8.12; taken as *yena*. "Enchanter of young women" — *yubatī-mohana*. All this is the speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: "Narahari's Lord".

[^08-16]: In each line of 8.15–8.18 there is the vocative particle *go* ("O!") in the middle, not rendered in the translation. "Irresistible" — *biṣama*. "The Thief of the treasure of life and youth" — *jībana-yaubana-dhana-corā*. "Puts His face to my face" — *mukhe mukha diyā*. "His body thrills in the waves of Madana" — *pulakita aṅga ati madana-taraṅge*. "Covering [my] breast with His palm" — *uraja jhā̃piyā kare*; "two golden lotuses" — *hema kamala duṭi*. "Coverings" — *basana*, "garments". "Play" — *raṅga*. "Shameless" — *nilaja*. "He breaks the vows of young women" — *yubatī barata kare bhaṅga*: *barata* (*vrata*) is a vow, here the fidelity and modesty of married women. The speaker's dream and longings, in the manner of the padas on Krishna; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 3 (according to the *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, Adi 15, in waking life the Lord did not jest with women). Signature: "the Beloved of Narahari's heart".

[^08-17]: "From the corner of my eye" — *ā̃khi-koṇe*. "Regards Himself as subject [to me]" — *āpanā adhīna kari māne*. "I am overcome with shyness" — *lājete mariyā yāi* (an idiom). Tambula is a chew of betel leaf, areca nut and spices; offering tambula is a sign of intimacy. "Lost in Madana" — *madane bibhora*. "Gives His very life for them" — *parāṇa nichiyā deya tāya*: *nichani* is the custom of waving something precious around a beloved person and casting it away, taking on oneself his misfortunes; cf. *bālāi laiyā* (note 4 of Kirana 7). "How He prizes the fine art of rasa" — *kibā bāse surasikapanā*; understanding uncertain. "Darling of the heart" — *hiyāra putali*, lit. "doll of the heart". "The Beloved of Narahari's heart" — in print *narahari-prāṇa-priyā* (feminine form; cf. *prāṇa-priya* in 8.15). "The one who enchants" — *eka janā*. The speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-18]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" (*kācic ca*, "and a certain [other]"). "Thief of young women's hearts" — *yubatī-parāṇa-corā*. "A pair of wagtails" — *khañjana yuga*: in poetry restless eyes are compared to wagtails. "Plays with His eyes" — *nayana nācāya*, lit. "makes His eyes dance". "Lost" — *bhorā*. "Veil" — *ghũghaṭa*. "Ah, how lovely!" — *āhā mari mari*, an exclamation of delight. "To make [me] a necklace and wear [me] round His neck" — *galāya paraye kari hāra*: a figure of inseparability. "Young Madana is His companion" — *nabīna madana sāthī tāra*. "In His kiss" — *adhare adhara dite*, lit. "when He puts lips to lips". "Never have I heard of such" — *nā śuni kabhu kāṇe*. "The Beloved of Narahari's heart" — *narahari-prāṇa-piyā*. The speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-19]: The first line is a refrain (centred); the second ends with the mark "ধ্রু" (*dhruva*, "refrain"). "Bee of rasa" — *rasera bhramara*; "thief of the honey of the lotus faces" — *badana-kamala-madhu-corā*: cf. the *Bhramara-gita* (*Bhagavata Purana* 10.47), where the gopis compare Krishna to a bee flitting from flower to flower. "Secret" — *marama*. "The longing of ears and heart" — *śrabaṇa-mana-byathā* (cf. 8.2). "Graceful gestures" — *bhaṅgimā*; "on some pretext" — *chale*. "Covers [my] breast with His palm" — *uroja jhā̃piye karatale*. "Trembles again and again" — *saghane kā̃paye*; "golden body" — *hema dehā*. "What does He not know!" — *kibā jāne*. "His love is an ocean of happiness" — *sukhera pāthāre tāra lehā* (*lehā* = *sneha*, "love"); understanding uncertain. The speaker's dream; see note 3. Signature: "the Beloved of Narahari's heart".

[^08-20]: The heading is only "যথারাগ" (without *anyā ca*): the verse is the author's own words about the gathering of nagaris. The verse begins and ends with "॥১৯॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a). The first word is damaged in print: *svapanera* ("of dreams") or *svajanera* ("of one's own, of dear ones"); taken as *svapanera*. "A wave of prema" — *premera ḍheu*. "Ah" — *ogo*, an address to the friends. "Only to make me suffer" — *dukha bhuñjāite*. "Fear of the elders" — *gurujanera trāsa*. "A plague on" — *maruka*, a curse, lit. "may they perish". "I shall leave my home" — *tejiba ghara*: cf. the gopis who left their homes at the sound of Krishna's flute (*Bhagavata Purana* 10.29). "Spiteful" — *biṣama*. "Let me surely be slandered — [together] with Shri Shachinandana" — *mora paribāda hauka niścaya śrī-śacīnandana-sane*: she is ready for any gossip, so long as her name is linked with His. Shachinandana, "the joy of Shachi", is Gaura. "Restless" — *ānachāna kare* (cf. 8.2). "Consigning this shame before the family to the fire" — *e kula-lājera kapāle āguni diyā*, lit. "putting fire on the forehead" — an idiom of complete renunciation (cf. tilanjali, note 3 of Kirana 7). In print line 16 ends with ";" and line 17 with "॥" instead of "।". "My left eye is twitching" — *nācaye e bāma ā̃khi*: for women a good omen, foretelling a meeting with the beloved. "Companions of good fortune" — *śubhera sāthī* (or *sākhī*, "witnesses of good fortune", cf. 8.11): good omens. On the mood of the nagaris, see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-21]: The verse begins at the bottom of and ends with "॥২০॥". The heading is only "যথারাগ": the author's own words. "Many auspicious signs" — *aneka maṅgala*: cf. the twitching left eye in 8.19. "Partly forgot the pain of their hearts" — *bisarila kichu hiyāra bedanā*. "The Creator has become gracious" — *prasanna haila bidhi*. "Will be fulfilled" — *siddhi*. Jahnavi is the Ganga, "the daughter of Jahnu". Gauri is Parvati, the consort of Shiva; cf. the gopis who, for Krishna's sake, kept the vow of Katyayani (Durga) (*Bhagavata Purana* 10.22). "She will free [me] from sorrow" — *te̐ha dukha dūra kariba* (lit. "she… [I] shall be freed from sorrow"). Bhanu is the Sun god (Surya); in Krishna-lila Radha worships the Sun in order to meet Krishna. "Soothe my heart" — *juṛāiba hiyā*, lit. "cool". "Without hindrance" — *abirodhe*: without obstacles from the household. Budo-Shiva (*buṛā-śiba*, "old Shiva") — probably Shiva as worshipped of old in Navadvipa under this name. "I feel" — *mora mane laya*. "In the rapture of prema" — *premera ābeśe*. "Ah, ah!" — *mari mari*. "I would take upon myself the misfortunes" — *bālāi laiyā* (cf. note 4 of Kirana 7). The nagaris turn to gods and goddesses only for the sake of meeting Gaura; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position regarding the mood of the nagaris, see note 3. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-22]: The heading in print is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" ("and a certain [other]"), although the verse is the author's own words; the heading is kept as in the edition. Each printed line consists of two short halves ("।" in the middle, "॥" at the end). "Ways" — *rīti*. "Their all" — *sarabasa* (*sarvasva*). "A well of nectarean rasa" — *sudhārasa-kūpa*. "The water in it" — *anupa* (Skt. *anūpa*, "a watery place, a pool"); so understood, rhyming with *kūpa*; the reading and sense are uncertain (possibly *anurūpa*, "worthy of it"). "Shine forth in his heart" — *sphuruka antare*. Signature: Narahari.

[^08-23]: The colophon is: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র বর্ণনে স্বপ্নপ্রসঙ্গ-বর্ণনং নামাষ্টমঃ কিরণঃ ॥৮॥"; the 1947 edition has *svapna-prasaṅga*. Kirana 8 comprises verses 8.1–8.21.


## Kirana 9. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Longings and So On of the Nagaris of Shri Navadvipa[^09-1]

*Morning in Navadvipa: a mother-in-law, delighted by an astrologer's words about the greatness of Shachi, herself sends her daughters-in-law to Shachi's house; the nagaris joyfully set out to see Gaura and one by one tell their longings about how they will meet Him*

*Again, in the morning period — the going [to Shachi's house], the longings, the darshan and so on of the nagaris of Shri Navadvipa.*

**9.1.** Today at dawn all the young, young nagaris,
hoping for the darshan of Gaura, dear to them as life, devise so many schemes.
All of them are most clever rasikas, floating in that ocean of rasa:
one contrives various pretexts, another now weeps, now laughs.
Another, driving away various fears from her mind, reflects on her mother-in-law's disposition:
and she, at the auspicious words of an astrologer, has now grown more kindly —
with a light heart the old woman has gone to sleep with the doors of the house open;
starting up again and again, she jumps up: "The night is over!"
Waking, she sees that in the east the dawn has risen;
leaving her bed, she hurried to her daughters-in-law
and sweetly asks: "Children, what are you doing sitting here?"
One says: "Ah, for the Lakshmi-puja we are learning the tale of Lakshmi."
Hearing this, she praises them many times, saying, "Good, good."
Narahari says: who in the world does not desire wealth?[^09-2]

*The elderly [mother-in-law] says — yatharaga*

**9.2.** Listen, listen, daughters-in-law: at last the Creator has become gracious to me.
Yesterday, during a prahara of the day, an astrologer came to our house.
What can I say of his qualities? I have never seen anyone like him here:
whatever anyone asks, he answers everything; he knows what is in one's heart.
"How shall good fortune come?" I said, and clasped his feet.
Seeing my distress, he said: "Do not worry about anything, mother.
In your village lives Shachi-devi — do you not know her greatness?
She is highly revered in the world; her deeds are known to all.
The dust of her feet is the rarest of things; whoever places it on his head
will gain wealth and kin — and is that so great a thing? — at once the threefold miseries are removed.
Whoever, rising at dawn, sees her face
floats in happiness birth after birth and never knows sorrow.
But whoever reviles mother Shachi burns and perishes in the fire of misery:
know for certain — the goddess Ugrachanda destroys him.
Therefore I advise: abandoning the guile of your mind,
at the close of night, at dawn, carefully send your daughters-in-law to the house of Shri Shachi-devi.
She will mercifully bless [them] and fulfil the desire of your heart:
prosperity will increase, there will always be happiness, many troubles will vanish.
Know that Nimaichanda's mother truly suffers at the suffering of others."
Having said much in this vein, the venerable astrologer went to another house.
Hearing all this, I was filled with great joy:
I felt in my heart as if all misfortune had gone.
Therefore go quickly: she is like my own family,
she affectionately calls me "didi" and never regards me as a stranger.
Going there, bow to her and speak humble words —
by her mercy every happiness will come; of this I am certain.
She will say to you: "Stay here this morning" —
so on what pretext would you come back early? I myself will go there too.
Hearing their mother-in-law's very pressing words, in great joy,
[but] for propriety's sake, they say softly, softly, covering their faces with their cloth:
"How can we leave the housework in the morning and go?"
Narahari says: you will come back and do it; now there must be no delay.[^09-3]

*Yatharaga*

**9.3.** Again she affectionately said many things to the daughters-in-law in various ways —
what can I say of it! All rose, intoxicated with prema for Gaura.
Keeping their faces in their veils, smiling softly, softly, full of rasa,
with sidelong glances of their eyes they say so much to one another.
In all of them the ocean of happiness surged up — how much can I tell of it?
Their bodies thrill with rapture, their hearts are restless, their feet do not touch the ground.
Quickly they put on fine new clothes and ornaments
and, bowing at their mothers-in-law's feet, went out of the house.
Thus, each having received the permission of her own mother-in-law,
at dawn they set out, jubilant, to see Gaurangachandra.
One says to another: "Friend, it will be hard [to keep composure] there:
having bowed at Shachi-devi's feet, stay calm."
Another says: "Ah, Gaurahari is the treasure of our life;
casting off shyness, I shall gaze at Him to my heart's content, with eyes full."
Another says: "Ah, when we get there, let no one say anything to me:
I will not leave Narahari's Lord of life and come home again."[^09-4]

*Raga lalita*

**9.4.** All the young women, enchanting everyone, have set out on the way, stepping with great sweetness;
on the road they do not tell path from pathlessness; their hearts are stirred, their creeper-like bodies thrill with rapture.
Their hair has come all loose, their attire glitters endlessly — how can poets describe it?
The edges of their new garments trail on the ground; not a trace of memory [of themselves] remains in their minds.
Thinking of the wondrous radiance of the beautiful Gaura, they are in boundless happiness;
holding hands, they laugh with one another and endlessly speak of His deeds.
Rasa blossoms; they jest and frolic in boundless enthusiasm.
How many, many longings they have! — Narahari will joyfully tell a little of them.[^09-5]

*Their longings [the author] relates in order.*

*A certain [woman] says — yatharaga*

**9.5.** Going to Shachi's house and bowing at Shachi's feet,
all bashful, I shall stand there, carefully keeping my composure.
Drawing my veil aside just a little, I shall glance but once at Gauranga —
and effortlessly steal His heart with the curved corner of my eye.
He will not be able to keep His composure — the King of nagaras will lose His calm.
Seeing that, I shall smile slyly and lean against my friend.
Looking at me again, the Beloved of my life will smile and be abashed.
Narahari, understanding this, will swim in the ocean of happiness.[^09-6]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**9.6.** With my friend, joyful, I shall go and stand in the courtyard of Shri Shachi-devi;
unnoticed, I shall look at Him just once — and soothe my eyes and heart.
And then He will look at me, giving an irresistible sign with His eyes —
neither composure nor restraint will remain: I shall tremble under the onrush of Madana.
My body will be drenched with sweat, again and again the hair on my head will come loose,
my garment will slip again and again — and not a trace of shame will remain.
With all my strength I shall rush to embrace the moon Gauranga —
my friend will hold me back by the end of my sari, and I shall heed her words.
Seeing me like this, the other [women] will laugh among themselves so much!
Understanding all this, I shall grow calm at heart, a little abashed,
and stand again, turned away, covering my face with my garment.
The Lord of Narahari's life, seeing this, will laugh with joy of heart.[^09-7]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**9.7.** Again I shall stand beside my friend, at a sign from my friend,
and shall not look at Gaura-nagara — I shall stay turned away.
Unable to see my face, He will grow still more restless
and, glancing unnoticed at my friend, will say to her with the corner of His eye:
"I understand nothing — why has she become so terribly angry?
Persuade her to forgive: no one holds a grievance against one's own.
Entreat her in My name to draw back her veil just once:
gazing on the sweetness of that face, let My eyes and heart be soothed."
Understanding this, my friend, pleading, will say to me again:
"Take the covering from your face and stand [openly] — let Gaurahari look."
Hearing this, I shall pretend not to hear — then she herself will draw [the veil] back with her own hand.
Stopping her, in still greater anger I shall go and stand far off.
Seeing this, Gauranga-raya will be awash in tears;
seeing that, my friend in distress will clasp my feet.
Then, smiling, I shall draw back my veil and look sidelong —
I shall give the highest joy to the Friend, the Lord of Narahari's life.[^09-8]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**9.8.** Gazing at Gaura-nagara, the ocean of rasa,
with a faint smile I shall begin talk of rasa with my friend.
Seeing my wondrous manner, He again will float in happiness
and, smiling ever so slightly, smiling, will give a sign with curving eyes.
Understanding this, I shall bite my lip and draw down my veil
and, unnoticed, taking aim with my brows, I shall steal the Friend's composure.
The King of rasikas will grow eager to embrace me —
Narahari will carefully hold [Him] back, reminding [Him] of shame before people.[^09-9]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**9.9.** Ai will show me much kindness — [but] I shall not stay near Ai:
quite unnoticed I shall stand behind my friend.
In the highest joy I shall satisfy the hunger of many days —
I shall let the chakoras of my eyes drink the nectar of that moon, His face.
I shall look my fill, but He will not be able to see me;
growing restless, He will say to my friend with a sign of His eyes:
"You have come alone — where has your companion stayed?
Not seeing the two of you together, I suffer at heart."
Understanding this, my friend will take me by the hand and bring me before her;
I, feigning anger and smiling slightly, will not go forward.
Yet my friend, holding [me], will keep [me] in front of her.
[…]
With a sign of her eyes she will say to the Friend: "Look — here is Your [own dear one]!"
Looking at me, the Crest of rasikas will say with the corner of His eye:
"Well, well! Where did you learn all these tricks?
Tell Me, tell Me: how shall I live without seeing you?"
Much like this He will convey; understanding, I shall admit my fault —
yet Gora, the Crest of rasikas, will still be angry with me.
Then Narahari will win [Him] over; relenting, [He] will show the necklace on the neck
and, smiling slightly, says: "Never do so again."[^09-10]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**9.10.** Gazing at the moon Gauranga, I shall fall into a heavy daze,
I shall grow faint — my earrings will slip and roll on the ground.
Quickly I shall cover myself with the end of my sari — at that movement of my hands
the bangles on my wrists will jingle; hearing that sound, He will be delighted.
Gauranga, who enchants the worlds, will convey with the slanting corner of His eye:
"Take off the covering just once — let Me see how beautiful [your] hair is."
Understanding this, I shall smile slightly and cover my face with the veil.
Seeing me abashed, He, pleased, will again give a sign to my friend.
My clever friend will say to me: "Stand turned away,
or else, looking at Gauranga, you will lose all your composure."
At these words, forgetting myself, I shall stand with my back to Gora —
and my friend with her own hand will quickly throw the covering off my head.
At that moment Gora, quite overcome in the rapture of rasa,
will forget everything and gaze with unblinking eyes.
I, greatly abashed, will hurriedly pull the covering back over my head
and, seemingly angry, will scold my friend: "Shameless one!"
I shall go and stand a little way off from my friend —
but she, admitting her fault, will pull [me] back and hold [me], swearing by her head.
Seeing this play of mine, playful Gauranga will again float in merriment,
and Narahari, the Friend's companion, will laugh within himself.[^09-11]

*And another — yatharaga*

**9.11.** Gazing at the moon Gauranga, I shall wink at my friend with a sidelong glance
and, smiling sweetly, sweetly, joyfully begin a sweet tale.
When, carried away by rasa, I toss my head, the knot of my hair will come loose;
sweetly, sweetly will swing my nose-ring and the champa in my ears.
But how the jhapa sways on my back — that He will not be able to see,
and the Nagara, giving a sign with the corner of His eye, will speak [of it] with a slight smile.
On some pretext I shall lift the covering with my left hand and show it to Him —
and at once Rasika-raya, the life of Narahari, will be overcome.[^09-12]

*And another — yatharaga*

**9.12.** I shall stand beside my friend, half my face covered by the veil,
and, glancing little by little, unnoticed, I shall fulfil my heart's longing.
When the Friend, half-smiling, looks at me,
I, understanding, will at once turn my eyes away and keep looking elsewhere.
The Beloved of my life, from shyness, will sweetly lower His eyes —
seeing this, I shall again glance sidelong at the moon of His face.
Unable to meet [my] eyes, He will grow restless; seeing this, I shall keep gazing [at Him] —
Narahari's Lord will float in happiness when eyes meet eyes.[^09-13]

**9.13.** I shall carefully stand in Ai's courtyard, holding my friend by the hand.
Gora, the jewel of virtues, looking at me, will say with a sign of His eyes:
"Take the covering from your breast just once — dispel the sorrow of [My] heart."
Understanding these words, I shall be abashed and at once turn my face away.
My friend is very clever: understanding [this], she will stretch out her hand
and, crying "What is this, what is this?" as if alarmed, will draw the covering from [my] breast.
Seeing this, Rasika-raya will cover His face with the end of His cloth and laugh;
Madana will torment [Him] — Narahari will calm Him.[^09-14]

*And another — yatharaga*

**9.14.** I shall stand beside my friend — the Nagara will not look at me,
but, smiling, smiling, will merrily exchange signs with my friend.
Unable to understand anything, I shall take my friend by the hand and ask:
"What fault have you seen in me that you laugh together?"
Hearing this, my friend will say: "Yes, there is a fault in you:
the Nagara wants to embrace [you] — and you are angry at that."
Hearing this, I shall say [something], giving a secret sign, and laugh as if pouring nectar —
Narahari will not be able to hold [Him] back: Gora will lose His composure.[^09-15]

*And another — yatharaga*

**9.15.** Seeing the smiling face of the moon Gauranga, in the fullness of rasa,
with the end of my cloth about my neck and palms joined, I shall say by a sign of the eyes:
"Very well, O Crown of rasikas, why do You practise deceit?
I do not know where You learned this — how skilfully You can deceive!
What more is there to say? Not once do You come and show Yourself [to me] on the road.
The Creator made me a woman — that is the sorrow; were it not so, I would stay beside You."
Hearing this, Narahari's Friend of life will be abashed
and will swear with the corner of His eye: "I shall surely come."[^09-16]

*And another — yatharaga*

**9.16.** Staying among my friends, glancing just once at that face,
with a sorrowful look I shall say to the Nagara with the corner of my eye:
"Very well, very well, O You — the inmost heart of love You have never known.
You can wander through this quarter and that — I alone am deprived.
You are the Crown of rasikas: You always stay absorbed in bliss.
But I, unfortunate one — what has not befallen me because of You!
My elders loved me more than life — [now] they regard me as poison;
those at whom I used to laugh when I saw them now laugh [at me] when they see me.
If even so You remained gracious to me, regarding me as Your own,
then all this disgrace I would count as no more than a straw.
It is not enough that day and night my body cannot bear the cruel burning —
on top of it You are pitiless: how then can life remain?
So dispel the doubt of my heart: why have You become angry?
Is such Your nature, or have You found some fault [in me]?"
Understanding this, He, overcome by rasa, will look at me
and, unseen, joining His palms, will say with the corner of His eye:
"A curse on My nature: I am stained with every fault!
[But] at every moment in My heart I know only this: you are dearer to Me than life."
Understanding this, I shall smile slyly and give a sign to my friend —
the heart of Narahari's Beloved, seeing this, will be at peace in great joy.[^09-17]

*Vilapa-chhandah*

**9.17.** Thus the host of young women, rejoicing, indulge more and more in longings;
the ever-new deeds of the charming, beautiful, excellent Gaura sport in their hearts.
Their bodies tremble intensely, like lightning, and tears stream from their eyes.
The new ways of prema, ever new and new, cannot be described even by hundreds upon hundreds of mouths.[^09-18]

*Raga lalita*

**9.18.** As night turned to dawn, all the beautiful women went with graceful, most charming gait;
wondrous was their attire; the sweet sound of their girdles and jewelled anklets captivates the minds of sages.
None can conceive who fashioned them: with the rays of every limb they illumine the world.
Seeing them, hundreds upon hundreds of Manmathas swoon; drawn by the fragrance of their bodies, bees come flying from all sides.
Rejoicing together, with supreme delight in their hearts, they quickly entered the lovely house.
The courtyard is an excellent lake of happiness: in it, as it were, a host of new lotuses has blossomed.
Approaching Ai, with great care, all of them, group after group, bow down —
as though the Creator were worshipping [her] feet with handfuls of champaka buds, says Ghanashyama.[^09-19]

*Yatharaga*

**9.19.** The bands of young women, bending most wondrously, offer obeisance with charming grace:
the slender ones bow like new golden creepers weighed down by a burden of flowers.
[…]
They lay their heads at the edge of [Shachi's] beautiful feet; their hair is loosened — black, of lovely sheen.
Lightly, lightly they stretch out their bud-like hands: their hearts long to take the rare dust [of her feet].
Their nails sparkle — as if jewels, with incomparable eagerness, were offering [themselves] as gifts out of respect.
Covering their lotus faces with the edge of their garments, they smile softly, softly — a radiance spreads,
as if, carefully straining fresh nectar, they were sprinkling [it] abundantly, says Narahari Dasa.[^09-20]

*Mukta-chhandah*

**9.20.** Resplendent is the wondrous gathering of young women; marvellous the radiance of their bodies, lovely their splendour.
What poet can describe their charming conduct and qualities? With humility they bow at Shachi's feet.[^09-21]

*Raga tudi*

**9.21.** Shachi — the Mother of the world, knower of the truth of motherhood, renowned for the loveliness of her deeds and ways —
regards them all in her heart as daughters-in-law dearer than her own life and shows them all the greatest affection.
She asks each and every one about her welfare and, placing her hand on her head, blesses her abundantly:
"May your wealth ever increase, and may your husbands and all [your kin] live long and remain well."
Hearing this, the young wives, laughing inwardly, floating in happiness, speak sweet words:
"Ah, tell us, how could any misfortune remain here once one has seen these auspicious feet?"
Having said a little with great shyness in their hearts, they stand nearby with joined palms.
Seeing this, the mother of Narahari's Lord of life — her eyes brimming with tears — is overwhelmed with affection.[^09-22]

*Yatharaga*

**9.22.** O, the young nagaris of Nadiya! They remain enraptured — who else could understand even a little of their deeds?
Glancing at the Beloved quite unseen, they tremble at heart from the arrows of Madana.
One, reflecting, says to herself: "Today, it seems, I have become shameless in front of everyone."
One, winking at another, cannot restrain herself and at once smiles a little, a little.
One, taking another by the hand, gently, gently entreats her; the heart overflows with great joy.
One tells another a tale of love, bringing veil a little close to veil.
One makes signs to another, hand touching hand — so many, many words arise in the mind!
One steadies another, warning her much with the lovely corner of her eye.
One, to show her own composure, carefully wipes another's face with the end of her cloth.
One, after whispering, realizes that things may turn out badly, and stays hidden to one side.
Thus all the chaste women of noble families were immersed in the ocean of the rasa of prema for Gaura.
What will Narahari say? To the Lord of their life they gave over their souls, their lives and their youth.[^09-23]

*Yatharaga*

**9.23.** May my mind remain at the feet of the nagaris of Nadiya — in life and at my passing.
Never have I seen or heard the like: for the sake of the Beloved they give up everything so easily.
The bodies of them all are fashioned of prema for Gora. Who can understand this? Whose power is it?
This is the hope in Narahari's heart: ever to sing of their virtues.[^09-24]

*Thus [ends] the ninth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Longings and So On of the Nagaris of Shri Navadvipa", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^09-25]

[^09-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon at the end of the kirana: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্রচিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে শ্রীনবদ্বীপ-নাগরীণাং মনোরথাদি-বর্ণনং নাম নবমঃ কিরণঃ ॥৯॥" — "the ninth kirana, called 'The Description of the Longings (*manoratha*) and So On of the Nagaris of Shri Navadvipa', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subtitle "Again, in the morning period — the going [to Shachi's house], the longings, the darshan and so on of the nagaris of Shri Navadvipa" (*punaḥ prātaḥkāle śrī-navadvīpa-nāgarīṇāṁ gamana-manoratha-darśanādikaṁ*. Kirana 9 comprises verses 9.1–9.23. The nagaris are the young married women of Navadvipa (see notes 1 and 3 of Kirana 7).

[^09-2]: The verse has no metre or raga heading; it begins and ends with "॥১॥". Here a new kind of the nagaris' mood begins — longings (*manoratha*) about how they will come to Shachi's house and see Gaura. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati and the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women or even look at them), reject *gaura-nagari-vada*, the worship of Gaura as an enjoying lover (*nāgara*) and of oneself as His nagari: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer. The longings of the nagaris in the poem are understood as a glorification of Gaura's irresistible beauty and of the prema it awakens in hearts; in reality they only bow to Shachi and look at Gaura from afar (see 9.3, 9.5). Further in this kirana, references are made to this note (cf. the main notes of Kirana 7 — note 3, and of Kirana 8 — note 3). "Young, young" — *naba naba*. "Dear as life" — *prāṇapriya*. "Hoping" — *āse* (*āśe*). "Schemes" — *yukati*. "Most clever rasikas" — *parama caturā rasikinī*. "Now weeps, now laughs" — the word before *hāsi* is smudged in print: probably *jhure*; the reading is unclear. Lines 5 and 6 are badly smudged in print; "her mother-in-law's disposition" — *śāśuṛī-rīti* (possibly *-bhīti*, "fear of the mother-in-law"); the reading is unclear. "Astrologer" — *daibajña*. "The old woman" — *buṛī*: the mother-in-law. "Children" — *bāchā saba*. Lakshmi-puja is the domestic worship of Lakshmi, the goddess of prosperity, performed in Bengal by women; "the tale of Lakshmi" (*lakṣmīra kathā*) is read at this rite. The daughters-in-law use it as a pretext, and the mother-in-law, hoping for prosperity, is pleased: hence Narahari's jest about the desire for wealth. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-3]: The heading is "বৃদ্ধাহ—যথারাগ" (*vṛddhā āha*, "the elderly [woman] says"): the mother-in-law speaks. "Daughters-in-law" — *badhū*. "During a prahara of the day" — *dina-prahara-samaye*: a prahara is a three-hour "watch" of the day; probably "one prahara after sunrise", the exact time is unclear. "He knows what is in one's heart" — *jānaye manera kathā*. "Clasped his feet" — *dharinu tāhāra pā*: a gesture of supplication. Shachi-devi is the mother of Gaura (Nimai, Nimaichanda).
