"The threefold miseries" — *tritāpa*: those arising from one's own body and mind, from other beings, and from the forces of nature. "Burns and perishes in the fire of misery" — *dukha-anale puṛiyā mare*. Ugrachanda is a fierce form of the goddess Chandi (Durga). "Abandoning the guile of your mind" — *manera kapaṭa chāṛi*. "The venerable astrologer" — *daibajña-rāya*. "She is like my own family" — *se haya āmāra ghara*, lit. "she is my home". "Didi" — "elder sister", an affectionate address. "This morning" — *e belā*, "this part of the day". "So on what pretext would you come back early? I myself will go there too" — *tāhe kona chale āsibe sakāle āmi ye yāiba sethā*; another understanding is possible: "then come back early on some pretext, for I shall be going there"; the sense is uncertain. "Very pressing" — *ati ātura*. "For propriety's sake" — *ādara lāgiyā*: out of deference the daughters-in-law show that they are in no hurry. "Covering their faces with their cloth" — *basana jhā̃piyā mukhe*. "There must be no delay" — *nā sahe byāja*. Narahari playfully takes up the mother-in-law's words. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-4]: The verse begins and ends with "॥৩॥" (the numeral is cut off in print). "In various ways" — *kateka bhā̃ti*. "Intoxicated with prema for Gaura" — *gaura-premete māti*. "Veils" — *ghũghaṭa*. "With sidelong glances" — *terachha nayana bhaṅgī kari*. "Their bodies thrill with rapture" — *pulakita tanu*: pulaka, the hair standing on end from prema. "Their feet do not touch the ground" — *bhūme nā paṛaye pā*. The end of line 7 is smudged in print (*paridhā…*): by sense, "put on"; the reading is unclear. Line 8 is partly smudged but legible. "Permission" — *ādeśa*. "Jubilant" — *ulasa haiyā*. "It will be hard" — *biṣama habe*. "Stay calm" — *susthira haiyā rabe*. "To my heart's content" — *manera sādhete*. "Narahari's Lord of life" — *narahari prāṇapati*: a signature formula. On the longings of the nagaris, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-5]: Lalita is a morning raga. The language of the verse is Brajabuli. The mark "(?)" after the first and fifth lines stands in the edition itself: the editor marked these lines as doubtful. The first line is *yata yubatī jana jana janita moha mahāmadhuragata gatiratā*: understood as "enchanting (*janita moha*, 'producing enchantment') everyone (*jana jana*), set on going (*gatiratā*) with great sweetness of step (*mahāmadhuragata*)"; the understanding is uncertain. "Do not tell path from pathlessness" — *pathi pantha bipatha na heri*. "Creeper-like bodies" — *tanulatā*. "Their hair has come all loose" — *ati śithila keśa*. "How can poets describe it?" — *racaba ki kabigaṇe*. "Not a trace of memory" — *kiñcita smṛti*. Line 5 is *bara gaura-ruci-caya rocaka cintāhi sukha atiśaye*: "thinking of the wondrous radiance (*ruci-caya*, 'mass of rays') of the beautiful Gaura"; the understanding is uncertain. "Holding hands" — *kara kara pakari*. "His deeds" — *uha carita*. "Rasa blossoms" — *rasa bikasa*. "Enthusiasm" — *umaṅga*. "Longings" — *manoratha*. "Will tell" — *bhaṇaba*. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-6]: The prose line before the verse is Sanskrit *eteṣāṁ manorathāni krameṇāha* ("their longings [he] relates in order"). The heading is "কাচিদাহ—যথারাগ" (*kācid āha*). The verse begins and ends with "॥৫॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a). The whole verse is the speaker's longing about how she will come to Shachi's house. "All bashful" — *ati salajjita*. "Veil" — *ghũghaṭa*. "With the curved corner of my eye" — *baṅkima nayana-koṇe*. "Will lose His calm" — *athira haibe*. "The King of nagaras" — *nāgara-rāya*: an epithet of Krishna in the padas. "Lean against my friend" — the print has *paṛinu saiyera gāya* (past tense, "leaned"), probably for *paṛiba*; understood by the sense of the longing. "The Beloved of my life" — *prāṇapiya*. All this is only the speaker's longing; in reality, according to the *Chaitanya-bhagavata*, the Lord did not look at women; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-7]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" (*kācic ca*, "and a certain [other]"; cf. 8.17). The verse begins in the middle of; the top four lines of are the end of 9.5 (see the previous portion). The longings (*manoratha*) of the nagaris continue, which Narahari "relates in order" (see note 6); hence all the verses are in the future tense: "I shall go", "He will look". In the language of these verses (Brajabuli) the future form in *-iba* is often used with a third-person subject as well (here "the other [women]… will laugh" — *āne āne kariba kateka hāsi*; cf. 9.8, 9.9, 9.10); the person is determined from the sense. Shachi-devi is Gaura's mother; "courtyard" — *aṅgana*. "Soothe my eyes and heart" — *juṛāba nayana hiyā*. "Irresistible sign with His eyes" — *biṣama ā̃khira ṭhāra*. "Neither composure nor restraint" — *dhairaja dharama*. "Under the onrush of Madana" — *madana-bhare*; Madana (Kamadeva) is the god of love; in the poem his name is the usual convention of the padas, and what is spoken of is prema for Gaura. Sweat, trembling, "drenched with sweat" (*ghāmete titibe tanu*) are signs of ecstasy (sattvika-bhava). "My garment will slip" — *khasibe basana*. "With all my strength I shall rush" — *adhika udyata haba*. "End of my sari" — *ācare* (= *ā̃care*). "The other [women] among themselves" — *āne āne*. "A little abashed" — *kiñcita saṅkoca bāsi*. All this is the speaker's longing; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. "The Lord of Narahari's life" — *narahari-prāṇanātha*. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-8]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins at the bottom of and ends with "॥৭॥". "Gaura-nagara" — *gaura-nāgara* (on the word *nāgara* see note 2). "Will say with the corner of His eye" — *nayana-koṇete kabe*; the word *kabe* is damaged in print, the reading is probable. The end of line 5 and line 6 (*dāruṇa roṣa*, *laoye doṣa*) are blotted in print but legible. "Persuade her to forgive" — *kṣemā karāha*. "No one holds a grievance against one's own" — *āpana janera keha nā laoye doṣa*. "Entreat her in My name" — *āmāra śapatha diyā*, lit. "giving [her] My oath". "Veil" — *ghũghaṭa* (cf. note 18 of kirana 8). "Pretend not to hear" — *śuni nā śuniba*. "Stopping her" — *nibāriyā*. "Gauranga-raya" — *gaurāṅga-rāya*: *rāya*, "sovereign", a respectful appellation. "Look sidelong" — *terachha nayāne raba*. "To the Friend" — *bãdhuyāre* (*bandhu*, "friend", an address to the beloved in the padas; cf. note 7 of kirana 8). Feigned anger (*māna*) is a common motif of the padas. The speaker's longing; see note 2. Signature: Narahari (*narahari-prāṇapati*).

[^09-9]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ";. "Ocean of rasa" — *rasera sāgara*. "Talk of rasa" — *rasera kāhinī*. "Wondrous manner" — *aparūpa bhaṅgī*. "Will give a sign with curving eyes" — *ṭhāriba baṅkima ā̃khe*: the form *ṭhāriba* with the subject "He" (see note 7). "Bite my lip" — *daśane adhara dābiyā*, a gesture of shyness. "Taking aim with my brows" — *bhuru-sandhāne*: the brows are a bow, the glance an arrow. "King of rasikas" — *rasika-rāja*. The last line is the signature formula: Narahari, Gaura's companion, holds Him back, reminding Him of public talk (*lokera lāja*). The speaker's longing; see note 2.

[^09-10]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins at the bottom of and ends with "॥৯॥". Ai (*āi*, "mother") is Shachi, Gaura's mother. "Hunger of many days" — *aneka dinera kṣudhā*. "The chakoras of my eyes" — *nayāna-cakore*: the chakora is a bird which, by poetic convention, feeds on moonlight. "With a sign of His eyes" — *nayāna iṅgite*. "Companion" — *saṅginī*: the speaker. "Bring me before her" — *āpana sammukhe niba* (an *-iba* form with the subject "friend", see note 7). After line 11 ("Yet my friend…", which has no paired line) the print has a row of asterisks: a gap in the manuscript; rendered "[…]". "Look — here is Your [own dear one]" — *dekhaha āpana jane*. "Crest of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara*. "Tricks" — *cāturī*. "Will still be angry with me" — *mora prati tathāpi kariba roṣa*: the subject is taken as "Gora" (an *-iba* form, see note 7); "I shall still be angry" is also possible, but *mora prati* ("towards me") favours the former. The last two lines are unclear: *narahari tāhe mānāba māniyā dekhābe galāra hāra* — understood as: Narahari will win Gaura over (*mānāba*), and He, relenting (*māniyā*), "will show the necklace on the neck"; the sense of the latter is unclear (perhaps a sign of reconciliation — cf. 8.17: "to make [me] a necklace and wear [me] round His neck"). "Says" — *kahena*, a respectful present-tense form (of Gaura). The speaker's longing; see note 2.

[^09-11]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins at the bottom of and ends with "॥১০॥". "Heavy daze" — *biṣama bhola*. "Earrings" — in print *kuṇḍala*; possibly *kuntala* ("hair"): then "my hair will come loose down to the ground" (cf. line 6: "how beautiful [your] hair is"); reading unclear. "Cover myself with the end of my sari" — *añcale jhā̃piba*. "The bangles will jingle" — *jhana jhana kara-kaṅkaṇa*, *jhana jhana* being an onomatopoeia for the jingling. "Will convey" — *jānāba* (an *-iba* form with the subject "Gauranga", see note 7). "Who enchants the worlds" — *bhubana-lobhā*. "Give a sign to my friend" — *sakhī prati… jānābe*. "Lose all your composure" — *athira haibā*. "Throw the covering off my head" — *śire basana dibeka pheli*. "Will gaze" — *rahiba cāyā* (of Gora; see note 7). "Shameless one" — *nilajī*. "Swearing by her head" — *māthāra śapatha diyā*. "Playful Gauranga" — *gaurāṅga raṅgī*. "The Friend's companion" — *bandhuyāra saṅgī*: the signature formula, Narahari as Gaura's companion. "Within himself" — *manera mānase*. The speaker's longing; see note 2.

[^09-12]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" (*anyā ca*, "and another");. "Wink" — *ṭhāriyā*. "Knot of my hair" — *culera khopā*. "Nose-ring" — *nāsāra besara*. "The champa in my ears" — *kāṇera cā̃pā*: earrings shaped like a champaka flower. "Jhapa" — *jhāpā*: apparently a tassel ornament on the braid hanging down the back; reading and meaning unclear. "Nagara" — *nāgara* (see note 2). "Will speak [of it]" — *hāsite kabe*: He asks to be shown. "Rasika-raya, the life of Narahari" — *narahari-parāṇa rasika rāya*. The speaker's longing; see note 2.

[^09-13]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins at the bottom of and ends with "॥১২॥". "Fulfil my heart's longing" — *purāba manera sādha*. "Half-smiling" — *ādha ādha hāsi*. "Elsewhere" — *āne*. "From shyness" — the print apparently reads *lāje* (not *lāge*); probable reading. "Lower His eyes" — *locana saṅkoca karibe*, lit. "will contract His eyes". "Sidelong" — *āṛa nayanete*. "Unable to meet [my] eyes" — *ā̃khe ā̃khi dite nā pāre*. "Narahari's Lord" — *narahari pa̐hu*. The speaker's longing; see note 2.

[^09-14]: In print this verse has no heading (neither "অন্যা চ" nor "কাচিচ্চ"): after 9.12 there is only a blank space; no heading is added in the translation. The number "॥১৩॥" at the end is in place. The first word in print is "অ'ইয়ের" with a damaged letter; understood as *āiyera* — "Ai's" (Shachi; see note 10). "Jewel of virtues" — *guṇa-maṇi*. "The covering from your breast" — *uroja basana*. "Dispel the sorrow of [My] heart" — *ghucāha manera duḥkha*. "Stretch out her hand" — *pasāri āpana kara*. "What is this, what is this?" — *iki iki*: the friend pretends to be alarmed (*dekhāyā ḍara*) and on that pretext draws the covering back. "Rasika-raya" — *rasika-rāya*. "Madana will torment [Him]" — *madane pīṛita habe* (on Madana see note 7). "Will calm" — *susthira karibe*. One of the most outspoken longings of the nagaris — translated exactly; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2.

[^09-15]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ";. The verse ends at the bottom of with "॥১৪॥" and does not run over to; "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". "Exchange signs" — *ṭhārāṭhāri*. "Yes, there is a fault in you" — *āchaye tomāra doṣa*. "Giving a secret sign" — *saṅketa kariyā*: *saṅketa* means both a sign and an agreed meeting place. "As if pouring nectar" — *amiya-pārā*, lit. "like nectar". "Will not be able to hold back" — *thira karite nāribe*. "Will lose His composure" — *adhīra haibe*. The speaker's longing; see note 2.

[^09-16]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" ("and another"). The verse is wholly; 9.14 ends at the bottom of and does not continue. The longings (*manoratha*) of the nagaris continue (see note 6), hence everything is in the future tense. "The moon Gauranga" — *gaurāṅga-cāndera*. "With the end of my cloth about my neck and palms joined" — *galāya basana diyā kara yuṛi*: a gesture of humble entreaty. "By a sign of the eyes" — *ā̃khira ṭhāre*. "Crown of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara*. "Deceive" — *bhā̃ṛāte*. "Not once do You come and show Yourself [to me] on the road" — *bāreka āsiyā dekhāṭi nā deha pathe*. "That is the sorrow" — *tẽi dukha*. "Narahari's Friend of life" — *narahari-prāṇabandhuyā*: a signature formula. "Will swear" — *śapatha khābe*, lit. "will eat an oath". All this is the speaker's longing; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-17]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "॥১৬॥". "To the Nagara" — *nāgare* (on the word *nāgara*, see note 2). "The inmost heart of love" — *pirīti-marama*. "Deprived" — *bañcita*. "Absorbed" — *bhora*. "My elders" — *gurujana*: the mother-in-law and other senior relatives. "Regard me as poison" — *biṣasama bāse*. A motif of the padas: for love's sake the heroine bears disgrace (*kalaṅka*) and mockery. "No more than a straw" — *tṛṇera adhika gaṇitu*, lit. "would count [it] as more than a straw"; understood by the sense ("would count it as nothing"); perhaps *adhama* ("lower") should be read. "Burning" — *jvālā*: the fever of separation. "How then can life remain?" — *kirūpe parāṇa raya*. "Found some fault [in me]" — *pā'yācha kona bā doṣa*. "Overcome by rasa" — *rasabaśa*. "A curse on My nature" — *maruka āmāra sbabhāba*, a common exclamation of self-reproach. "Give a sign to my friend" — *ṭhāriba saiyera prati*. "Narahari's Beloved" — *narahari-piyā*. The speaker's longing; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-18]: The heading is "বিলাপ ছন্দ" (Vilapa-chhandah; printed without visarga).. The author's words: here the account of the nagaris' longings (9.5–9.16), begun in 9.4–9.5, comes to an end. "Host" — *kadamba*. "Indulge more and more in longings" — *karata manorathādhika ulasaye*. "Charming" — *sughara*. "Sport in their hearts" — *citta bilasaye*. "Like lightning" — *śampā-sadṛśa*. Trembling and tears are signs of ecstasy (sattvika-bhavas). "Ways of prema" — *prema-gati*. "Mouths" — *bayana* (*badana*). No signature.

[^09-19]: Lalita is a morning raga (cf. 9.4). The language of the verse is Brajabuli.. The nagaris arrive at Shachi's house. According to the pada glossary, verses 9.18–9.22 also appear in the *Gaura-pada-tarangini* (2.4, nos. 4–7), and Narahari placed the same padas in the wedding narrative of the *Bhakti-ratnakara*. "Graceful gait" — *lalita gati*. "Girdles" — *rasanā* (a girdle with little bells). "Jewelled anklets" — *maṇi nūpura*. "None can conceive who fashioned them" — *anubhaba nahai kaune sirajala*. "Illumine" — *ujora*. "Hundreds upon hundreds of Manmathas swoon" — *manamatha śata śata muruche*: Manmatha (Kamadeva, cf. note 7) is the standard of beauty; a conventional figure of the padas: the women's beauty surpasses that of hundreds of Kamadevas. "Bees" — *madhupa*. "The lovely house" — *rucira geha*: Shachi's house. "Lake" — *sarasi*. Ai is Shachi (see note 10). "Group after group" — *yūtha yūtha*. "As though the Creator were worshipping [her] feet with handfuls of champaka buds" — *campaka-kali añjali bhari bhari bihi pūjata pada bujhi*: the fingers of the women's joined palms are like golden champaka buds. Signature: Ghanashyama.

[^09-20]: The heading is "যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "॥১৯॥". The language is Brajabuli. After the second line the print has a row of asterisks: a gap in the manuscript; rendered "[…]" (cf. 9.9, note 10). The fifth line ends with "॥" without a paired line — perhaps its pair is missing as well. "Bending most wondrously" — *nati gati ati adabhuta*. "The slender ones" — *sutanu*. "Weighed down by a burden of flowers" — *kusuma-samūha-bhāragata bhārī*. "The edge of the beautiful feet" — *surucira caraṇa upānta*. "Their hair is loosened" — *śithila śiroruha*. "Bud-like hands" — *kara-pallava*. "The rare dust" — *durlabha reṇu*: the dust of Shachi's feet (cf. 9.2). "Their nails sparkle — as if jewels… were offering [themselves] as gifts out of respect" — *jhalakata nakha mariyāda hetu janu bheṭata maṇigaṇa anupa uchāha*; *bheṭata* — "present a gift (on meeting)"; the understanding is uncertain. "Lotus faces" — *ambuja-badana*. "Straining fresh nectar" — *naba makaranda chāni*: the women's smile is like a sprinkling of nectar. Signature: Narahari Dasa.

[^09-21]: The heading is "মুক্তা ছন্দঃ" (Mukta-chhandah).. Each printed line consists of two short halves ("।" in the middle, "॥" at the end). "Gathering" — *ghaṭā*. "Splendour" — *chaṭā*. "With humility" — *binaya pūrba*. No signature.

[^09-22]: The heading is "তুড়ি রাগ" (raga tudi, i.e. todi).. "Mother of the world" — *jagata-jananī*. "Knower of the truth of motherhood" — *jananī-tattvabida*. "As daughters-in-law" — *badhū-sama* (*badhū* means both "daughter-in-law" and "young married woman"). "Placing her hand on her head" — *śirete kara dhari*: a gesture of blessing. "Husbands" — *pati*. "Laughing inwardly" — *mane mane hāsi*: Shachi blesses them as ordinary married women, while their hearts are given to Gaura (see note 2). "How could any misfortune remain here" — *ki lāgi aśubha rahibe ethā*. "The mother of Narahari's Lord of life" — *narahari-prāṇapati mātā*: Shachi. "Her eyes brimming with tears" — *ā̃khi chala chala*. "Overwhelmed with affection" — *bibaśa snehe*. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-23]: The heading is "যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "॥২২॥". The author's words about the nagaris in Shachi's house. "O" — *go*, an exclamation. "Remain enraptured" — *bibhori raya*. "Even a little" — *thori*. "From the arrows of Madana" — *madana-bāṇe* (on Madana, see note 7). "Shameless" — *nilajī*. "Winking" — *ṭhāri*. "Entreats" — *sādhe*. "Bringing veil a little close to veil" — *alapa ghũghaṭe ghũghaṭa diyā*: leaning their veiled heads together. "Hand touching hand" — *kare karete*. "Warning her much" — *kata bhaya dekhāiyā*. "The end of her cloth" — *paṭāñcala*. "After whispering" — *kāṇākāṇi kari*. "Things may turn out badly" — *jāni biparīta*; the sense is uncertain. "Chaste women of noble families" — *kulabatī satī*. "Gave over their souls, their lives and their youth" — *prāṇa jībana yaubana sãpiyā dilā*: the nagaris' devotion to Gaura; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-24]: The heading is "যথারাগ".. Each printed line consists of two short halves. The closing verse of the kirana — the author's words. "May my mind remain" — *mati rahu mora*. "In life and at my passing" — *jībane maraṇe* (of the author himself). "So easily" — *amani*. "Fashioned" — *gaṭhita*. Narahari glorifies not worldly feelings of the nagaris but their self-giving to Gaura; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^09-25]: The colophon is: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্রচিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে শ্রীনবদ্বীপ-নাগরীণাং মনোরথাদি-বর্ণনং নাম নবমঃ কিরণঃ ॥৯॥". Kirana 9 comprises verses 9.1–9.23.


## Kirana 10. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Delights and So On of the Heavenly Women Filled with Prema for Him[^10-1]

*Morning: the heavenly women, circling in the sky above Navadvipa, gaze at Gaura and marvel at the prema of the people and the nagaris of Nadiya; talking among themselves, they dispute about Gaura's nature — some speak of His secret plays as a nagara, others defend Him as the One who has conquered the senses — and one of them longs to become a companion of the nagaris of Nadiya*

*Again, in the morning period — the various delights and so on of the heavenly women, eager for the rasa of beholding Him.*

*Matanga-chhandah*

**10.1.** At the close of night the house of Shachi, the ornament of the world, is lovelier than ever.
From house to house through the town of Nadiya, charming men and women hasten in all directions.
What poet can describe it? Jubilation surges like the waves of an ocean of joy.
The women of the city [of the gods], with their companions, moving through the sky, glide gracefully and unceasingly.
With hearts most pure yet tremulous, they ponder whether to descend to earth;
with great, great effort they keep their composure, while their bodies tremble violently and irrepressibly.
With restless eyes they taste the lovely, delightful radiance of the excellent Gaura.
Balihari! — [says] Narahari: they are all overwhelmed, and the touch [of that radiance] again holds them in its power.[^10-2]

*Rangamala-chhandah*

**10.2.** The host of heavenly women, gazing at the Beloved, their hearts rejoicing, are beautiful in their wondrous manner;
their abundant new ornaments and garments sparkle, their limbs all thrill with rapture.
In shapely bands, unapproachable, lovely, beautifully attired, they shine in many ways;
risen into the sky, they illumine its vault like rows of heavenly lightning.
Filled with auspicious bhava, with incomparable grace, they move very slowly;
again and again the veil slips and falls, again and again the new knot of the waist-cloth loosens.
They smile softly, they bite their sweet lips — their teeth gleam;
holding one another by the hand, they say something to one another — Narahari Dasa is delighted.[^10-3]

*A certain one [of them] says — yatharaga*

**10.3.** Friend! Thinking day and night, we have understood for certain:
Gora has appeared in order to drive the people of Nadiya mad.
No one can stay apart from the moon Gauranga even for a moment;
on their beds and in their dreams — always in meditation [on Him]: their minds do not stay at home.
What more is there to say? As witness to it — look toward this quarter and that:
even lying down, they repeat "Gora, Gora" and bring no other [word] to their lips.
Listen, filling your ears — a wondrous sweet sound at dawn:
in singing the deeds of Narahari's Lord they are all supremely skilled.[^10-4]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.4.** Look, look, ah, what bliss there is today in the beautiful city of Nadiya!
All the auspicious splendour of the world is in the homes of the people of Nadiya.
Ah, ah! How to understand their ways — one is left bewildered:
leaving their beds, they wage a fierce struggle with the night.
One says: "Come, come quickly, no delay can be borne now:
come what may — let all the work just lie where it is."
How many such conversations pass among them!
To see Narahari's Lord of life, no one stays at home.[^10-5]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.5.** Blessed, blessed, O, in all the world are the residents of Nadia:
surely the treasure of life for all of them is Gauranga, the abode of virtues.
Be that as it may, the nagaris of Nadia are the most blessed among them:
I do not know how many, many hundreds of yugas they performed pious deeds!
Look, look, what wondrous love they have for the moon Gauranga:
at the end of the night they run from all sides, unable to restrain themselves.
Ah, ah, would that I could take upon myself all the misfortunes of these young women!
Though they are wives of [respectable] families, they count shame and propriety as nothing.
Shame, shame on dwelling in the city of the gods, shame on the burden of this body!
Shame, shame on these eyes! What use now is such beauty and youth?
If only I had such good fortune — to live in Nadia even once,
then, joining the company of these [women], I would fulfil my heart's wish.
Happy in Gora's happiness, they do not know any happiness of their own;
not seeing that face for a moment, they count it a grief greater than dying.
Singing their virtues, my heart rejoices day and night;
Narahari is witness to it: I always love them more than life.[^10-6]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.6.** Who has the power to understand the deeds of the nagaris of Nadia?
Such beauties, such connoisseurs of rasa, there are no others in the world.
Look, look, O — ah, would that I could take upon myself all the misfortunes of this wondrous beauty! —
the roads of Nadia are [like] rivers, and on them, it seems, rows of lotuses have bloomed.
How wondrously skilful their gait, how wondrously they glance at one another!
Laughing and jesting, overcome by rasa, how many graceful gestures they make!
Their new garments and ornaments glitter beyond compare;
I do not know why — again and again the necklaces sway on their breasts.
On their heads the lovely locks have come loose, strings of flowers are falling;
not even for a moment can they slow their hurried gait.
Rejoicing, [eager] to meet Narahari's Lord, they enter the house [of Shachi];
as if knowing that they will offer [Him] the treasure of their youth as a gift, the anklets ring auspiciously.[^10-7]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.7.** Ah, ah, O, what wondrous beauty in the midst of Nadia!
I do not know who built Shachi's house, which enchants the whole world.
On all sides glitter rows of new golden mansions;
in the golden courtyards so many golden men and women sport.
And another wonder — look: the town of Nadia has become golden;
the golden trees, the kadambas, are beautifully adorned with golden creepers.
The beasts and birds are golden, and all the insects and moths are like gold.
Who has stolen the whiteness [of the Ganga]? Jahnavi has become a golden stream.
The sky has become golden — what is this? Look: the whole world is like gold!
Surely this is how great is the power of the beauty of Narahari's Lord.[^10-8]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.8.** Look, look, O — the radiance of the beauty of Gauranga, who enchants the world!
Is this a mass of lightning that has left the cloud and [come down] upon the earth?
Is this a heap of pure, beautiful buds of the golden lotus?
Is this a heap of pure, lovely, finely ground gorochana?
Is this the fresh layer of kunkuma on the breasts of the young maidens of Vraja?
Is this the champaka garland on the necks of the nagaris of Navadvipa?
My heart feels: if only I could keep Him always within my heart!
The eyes are not sated looking [at Him] — Narahari is witness to it.[^10-9]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.9.** Look, look, O — the world-enchanting attire of the moon Gauranga!
His lovely curling hair, wreathed with kunda buds, has come loose.
On His graceful forehead is a tilaka of kunkuma, beautifully adorned with dots of sandal paste —
as if the lord of the stars had risen in the midst of a golden sky.
In His ears glitter earrings — who can find a comparison for them?
I understand: He will steal everything — composure, propriety and modesty.
On His neck is a garland that enchants young women, falling in incomparably graceful tiers.
I look at Narahari's Lord — what a form! — I cannot understand what play this is.[^10-10]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.10.** Listen, listen, O you who are dear as life: I will whisper in your ear —
what you have reasoned has come to my mind as well.
He seems somehow strange today — as you yourself see, clever one:
so overpowered, overcome by rasa, I have never seen Him.
And if I ever did see it, still I could make out nothing.
Tell me, tell me then: who has stolen the heart of the moon Gauranga?
Narahari's Moon is truly a nagara: one can understand it from [His] deeds;
and yet I cannot say this firmly to anyone, out of shyness.[^10-11]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.11.** Look, come here, O: I have understood all the thoughts of your heart.
Until now you did not know these deeds of Gorachanda.
So I tell you for certain: do not puzzle over it any longer —
Gauranga is enchanted by the love of these young women.
Today He spent the night with some fortunate woman
and, granting her the touch of this body, fulfilled all her heart's wishes.
She is a nagari most expert in rasa: I understood this, floating in supreme bliss —
she did not let the young crown of the nagaras of Nadia sleep at night.
If you say, "How am I to know this?" — look at the witness to it:
His two lovely reddened eyes — languid, overpowered, drooping [with sleep].
And besides, such disorder in His attire — what doubt can there be?
The secret pastimes of Narahari's Lord are manifest to rasikas.[^10-12]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.12.** What shall I say, O: you have felt it well and judged it rightly.
All the nagara-ways of the moon Gauranga I know,
[but] I am shy to tell you those stories;
and so, watching Gauranga's deeds, I laugh within.
He always thinks of Himself thus: "I am the king of the clever:
secret is My descent, and secret are all My deeds;
secret My walk and speech, secret the curves of My dance,
secret with the nagaris of Nadia the play of love;
I show My nagara-ways secretly — no one can notice it."
Thinking much in this way, He covers the rays of the sun with His hand.
Before one who is clever above the clever, can any cunning hold?
Not understanding this, whom does Narahari's Lord fear?[^10-13]

*And another — yatharaga*

**10.13.** That it is all so with the moon Gauranga — do not grieve over it:
what is open brings sorrow; in what is secret there is greater happiness.
Even on gaining a little wealth, [people] hide it more carefully than life;
if you say this is impossible, look at the people of the world.
Love is a supreme jewel: it is fitting to keep it hidden;
if it becomes open, shame arises in the hearts of rasikas.
Narahari's Lord is the crown of the adroit: does anyone know another such?
He sports in secret without cease — [and by this] reveals His adroitness.[^10-14]

*And another — yatharaga*

**10.14.** Say what you will, [but] to hide love is too heavy a burden:
love never stays hidden — its nature is to become manifest.
He acts like the sun — can I consider this secret?
You know [His pastimes] as "secret, secret," but I know them as manifest.
In the town of Nadia is the crown of rasikas, Shachi's darling, Gora,
the thief of the jewel of composure of all the young wives of good families.
I have seen: nowhere in all the world is there such a nagara;
know for certain: no one can escape Him.
If some young woman, holding to her composure, wishes to keep her propriety,
He, glancing [at her] with the irresistible corner of His eye, enchants her.
Day and night He plays endlessly with the young nagaris;
Narahari's Lord, the beloved of the nagaris, pines for the nagaris.[^10-15]

*And a certain [other] — yatharaga*

**10.15.** Listen, listen, O: I say it for certain — we are utterly unknowing:
we could not understand that Gora displays such nagara-ways in Nadia.
As we see Him outwardly — supremely noble —
so we know Him; what is within Him cannot be known.
Blessed, blessed, it seems, are you — the supreme connoisseurs of rasa in the city of the gods!
Who but all of you could understand these pastimes?
Be that as it may, now at last the darkness of my mind seems to have gone:
Narahari's Lord has appeared in the world subject to young women.[^10-16]

*Yatharaga*

**10.16.** Of all the nagara-ways of the moon Gauranga everyone speaks — so many, many opinions!
It is as if streams of nectar were raining down; I do not know what happiness is in everyone's heart.
Then yet another woman — from another, new group — came there, hearing these words.
Narahari does not know her nature: she boldly addresses them all.[^10-17]

*Yatharaga*

**10.17.** What shall I say to you, O? I have fallen into bewilderment:
why do you so revile such a worthy [person] — the moon of Nadia?
The most learned Jagannatha Mishra — who does not know him?
[He is] the lamp of his spotless family, and the world sings of Him;
He conquered the conqueror of the quarters, and the pandits of Nadia are subject to Him;
always devoted to the path of dharma, He knows nothing but the Vedas and the other [scriptures].
He never listens to talk about women: to hear it pains Him;
never, even inadvertently, does He look at the faces of others' wives.
If ever, while bathing in the Suradhuni, a woman's garment touches His body,
He at once performs the proper atonement — and still He cannot regain His composure.
Therefore, to cast false blame on Him deliberately will be an offence.
Narahari is witness to it: I teach you all — never speak these words.[^10-18]

*Yatharaga*

**10.18.** Yet another woman, her heart full to overflowing,
coming to that place, looks in all directions.
Who can understand her heart? There is no fear in her at all.
Taking her friend by the hand, she says to her:[^10-19]

*Yatharaga*

**10.19.** Look, come here, O: why are you quarrelling with all of them?
An empty commotion has arisen in the city of the gods — do not be troubled by it.
All good conduct has gone far away: no one respects anyone;
this is a great misfortune: they do not understand at all what comes from what.
"It is forbidden to censure even one who has faults" — everyone says this;
and whoever blames the faultless surely becomes faulty in the world himself.
The son of Shachi is a most worthy [person]: this is known throughout the world.
Let alone looking at anyone — He does not even raise His face, out of modesty.
He never touches even the shadow of other women —
I do not understand how [one can speak of Him] touching bodies and the like.
Knowing that young women crowd the ghats of the Suradhuni, He does not go there:
He goes to a pond and bathes where He sees it is secluded,
or else He performs His rites in His own house: His heart trembles for fear of disgrace.
The supreme Conqueror of the senses, dear to all — who does not praise Him?
Alas, alas! How can one speak such words about such a person,
whose incomparable fame, [like] an elixir, fills the world?
Who would believe such words about Him? I am ashamed of this.
Does a worthy person revile a worthy person? That is the work of the unworthy.
Still I speak, knowing [you as my] companions: you will accept the essence of my words.
Never, even inadvertently, let anyone revile Narahari's Lord any more.[^10-20]

*Yatharaga*

**10.20.** All these mocking women, [hearing] the words of instruction —
hearing them — were greatly amused at heart.
Looking at her, they laugh to themselves
and, slyly, speak with mocking gestures:[^10-21]

*Yatharaga*

**10.21.** Good, good, O! We are not afraid of such words:
as worthy a person as you are, so worthy is your Gora.
Aha, ah, ah! He knows nothing [of such things] — I have never seen such a person!
Supremely the Conqueror of the senses, like the king of sages — His righteousness is known to all.
He does not listen to talk about women — this fame is well known throughout the world;
leaving His own house, He never goes to anyone's home on any business.
How many more such incomparable virtues will you list?
And whatever else is within Him, He does not reveal outwardly.
So I say, listen: others have no power to know those virtues:
only in the flashes of the sidelong glances of these young women do they become manifest.
I see your eyes are like a bird that does not see the moon of the night:
how would others understand that Narahari's Lord is a snare enchanting women?[^10-22]

*Her ally says — yatharaga*

**10.22.** Look, come here, friend dear as life! There is no joy in my mind from this:
in vain you speak all these secret words of rasa with her.
Can anyone but a woman who knows rasa understand the heart of a connoisseur of rasa?
And besides, she is very simple-hearted — she never walks this path.
However much you explain to her, it is no concern of hers:
can she whose attachment is to the formless ever come to love form?
If ever she sincerely shares your rare company,
then she will be able to understand what the sports of the moon of Nadia are like.
Let all these words rest for now; just ask her once:
how does He, being supremely the Conqueror of the senses, play like this?
He who knows nothing [of such things], who has not a trace of [such] joy —
why, in the midst of the city of Nadia, does He take on the guise of a nagarini?
Nowhere have I heard of this: is this the act of a noble person?
By the fragrance of His body He breaks the dignity and modesty of the bee-like women.
He who is so composed — are these His deeds? How does it seem to you?
Seeing a man's face, He mistakes it for a woman's face and swoons.
This is very strange: one who has so much modesty — are such deeds His?
In the assembly of the righteous He dances incessantly, repeating a woman's name.
He who never listens with His own ears to talk about women —
how does He constantly repeat [the name of] a woman in His mind?
He who in the world is above all, endlessly righteous —
why does He gather so many young women in His own house?
Tell her: let her answer about this conduct of Narahari's Lord.
How has such faith in such a person become fixed in her heart?[^10-23]

*Yatharaga*

**10.23.** The mocking women said much in this way.
Who could answer? That young woman was abashed at heart.
Then yet another woman, who knows great skill [in speech],
looking at her own [companions], speaks sweet words:[^10-24]

*Yatharaga*

**10.24.** Listen, listen, O: I have understood everything — she is not at fault at all;
having considered it, I am angry with all of you.
If someone, not understanding, says something — why laugh at it?
If you explain it as she is able to understand, I will consider you wise.
She is of good character — ah! — and you do not know how to explain to such a person.
Be silent now and listen to what I say.
Look, come here, O worthy beauty, do not grieve at heart:
hearing your words, I have found supreme happiness.
You say that Gora does not look at other women even from the corner of His eye —
how am I to believe all these words?
If I could see even a little of what you praise Him for,
then, though you assert it and swear oaths, I would still come to believe.
The nagara-ways in the city of Nadia are beyond counting;
whatever others may say — this I have seen myself.
If you say, "How is this possible in this descent?" —
there is a reason for it, a well-known one: all people speak of it.
Whatever nature one has, no one can ever give it up:
one acts according to one's nature, heeding no one's prohibition.
If you think, "Nowhere have I seen such a nature in Him" —
then I tell you about this, listen: the whole world is witness to it.
This son of Shachi is the son of Yashoda: do you not know this?
What shall I tell you of all the secret pastimes in Vrindavan?
For the gopis' sake — herding the cows, lifting the hill and all the rest;
and how much can I tell of the pastimes with the gopis — where and what [they were]?
He is utterly subject to them all: not seeing them for a moment, He feels it as an aeon;
under how many pretexts He wanders constantly, hoping for the touch of [their] bodies!
If some proud one, in mana, does not turn to look at Kanu,
then, to end her mana, He clasps the feet of [her] sakhi.
He carries [her] on His shoulder, deeming it His own supreme good fortune;
hearing words of reproach, He rejoices more than at the hymns of the Vedas.
For the sake of young women He takes no account of grave disgrace in the world.
Tell me, tell me: how will He give up such a nature?
Therefore know this for certain, having considered it in your hearts:
[His] nature makes [Him] do all these deeds — you will understand it yourselves.
Narahari's Lord is the Crown of connoisseurs of rasa, Who has no equal:
who does not know these deeds? What further doubt is there?[^10-25]

*Yatharaga*

**10.25.** The special plays of rasa in the city of Vraja — who can tell of them all?
Keep hidden in your heart what I shall tell you, knowing you as my own:
this One is that very One, that very One is this; together with all those same dear companions
He ever sports in the city of Nadiya, absorbed in His own virtues.
First giving His own people to drink the nectar of the wondrous sweetness of [His] beauty,
He makes their minds and gait like those of the mad — and so they reckon with nothing.
Ever new hosts of wives of respectable families, having given tilanjali to the disgrace of their families and to shame,
have made their very essence Gora, full of joy, the Beloved, dear as life — Narahari is witness to it.[^10-26]

*Yatharaga*

**10.26.** Hearing again and again of the lovely deeds of the moon Gauranga, that young woman was filled with happiness;
she cannot keep her composure — again and again both her eyes fill with tears of prema.
Joining her palms, she bows and again speaks soft, sweet words:
"Accepting me as your own, you have, as it were, at last dispelled all the pain of my heart.
I pray: how may I find a dwelling, even once, in this city of Nadiya,
and how may I live as a companion of these ever new playful ones?
Keeping in my heart Narahari's Beloved, dear as life, I shall remove the grievous obstacle.
Tell me, tell me, O — by what means will all these desires be fulfilled?"[^10-27]

*Yatharaga*

**10.27.** Hearing the lovely words and accepting [them], the beautiful one rejoiced and, reflecting again in her heart,
she told generously of all that the other had asked and all that was in her mind.
That account is like streams of nectar: with one mouth I cannot describe it.
The hearts of all thrill with rapture — what more can I say? — they float in bliss.
Their bodies tremble again and again — a matchless manner! — and not a trace of composure can they hold.
One, leaning her body against another's, makes so many graceful movements from the fullness of rasa;
exchanging winks from the corners of their eyes, they laugh, absorbed in the sports of Ananga;
so many jests with one another intoxicate them — [as they] gaze at the Lord of Narahari.[^10-28]

*Thus [ends] the tenth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Delights and So On of the Heavenly Women Filled with Prema for Him", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^10-29]

[^10-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon at the end of the kirana: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র বর্ণনে দেবরমণীনাং তৎ প্রেমাবিষ্ট-কৌতুকাদিবর্ণনং নাম দশমঃ কিরণঃ।" — "the tenth kirana, called 'The Description of the Delights (*kautuka*) and So On of the Heavenly Women (*deva-ramaṇī*) Filled with Prema for Him', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the various delights and so on of the heavenly women, eager for the rasa of beholding Him" Kirana 10 comprises verses 10.1–10.27. The heavenly women (*deva-ramaṇī*, *sura-nārī*) are the wives and maidens of the gods, who watch Gaura's pastimes from the sky; according to the pada glossary, they are the speakers in 10.10–10.24.

[^10-2]: The metre is "মাতঙ্গ ছন্দঃ" (Matanga-chhandah); the language is Brajabuli. The verse begins and ends with "॥১॥". Here begins a new form of the mood described in kiranas 7–9: the heavenly women, seeing Gaura from the sky, are filled with prema for Him and long to descend to earth. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati and the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not even look at them), reject gaura-nagari-vada — the worship of Gaura as an enjoying lover (*nāgara*) and of oneself as His nagari: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer. The rapture of the celestial women is understood in the poem as a glorification of Gaura's irresistible beauty and of the prema it awakens even in the world of the gods; later in this same kirana some of the heavenly women defend Gaura before others as the One who has conquered the senses (*jitendriya*) and does not look at others' wives. Further notes in this kirana refer to this note (cf. the main note of kirana 9 — note 2). "The ornament of the world" — *bhubanaka bhūṣaṇa*. "Lovelier than ever" — *adhika suhāi*. "Charming" — *sughara*. "Hasten in all directions" — *cahũdiśa dhāi*: the people of Nadiya hasten to Shachi's house. "Jubilation surges like the waves of an ocean of joy" — *raṅga-jaladhi taraṅga sadṛśa umaṅgaye*. "The women of the city [of the gods]" — *pura-ramaṇī-gaṇa*; by the subheading and the words "moving through the sky" (*gagana-gata gati*) these are the heavenly women, *pura* being the city of the gods (cf. *surapura*). "With their companions" — *gaṇa-saha*. "Unceasingly" — *abhaṅgaye*; the understanding is uncertain. "Tremulous" — *tarala*. "To earth" — *khiti* (*kṣiti*). "Composure" — *dhṛti*. "Irrepressibly" — *anibāra*. "Taste" — *cākhaye*. "Balihari!" — *balihāri*, an exclamation of admiration ("I offer myself for this!"). The last half-line is *bibaśa saba punaḥ paraśa parabaśa rākhaye*: understood as "they are all overwhelmed, and the touch [of Gaura's radiance] again holds them in its power"; possibly also "Narahari… again restrains the overwhelmed ones"; the sense is uncertain. Signature: Narahari.

[^10-3]: The metre is "রঙ্গমালা ছন্দঃ" (Rangamala-chhandah); the language is Brajabuli.. "The host of heavenly women" — *sura-nārī nikara*. "The Beloved" — *piya*. "Beautiful in their wondrous manner" — *sohe sudhaṅga*. "Thrill with rapture" — *pulakita*: the hairs standing on end from prema. "In shapely bands" — *sama yūtha yūtha*. "Unapproachable" — *agamya*: probably inaccessible to human eyes; the understanding is uncertain. "The vault of the sky" — *gagana maṇḍala*. "Rows of heavenly lightning" — *dibya bidyuta pā̃ti*. "Filled with auspicious bhava" — *śubha bhāba-bhara paripūrṇa*. "The veil slips and falls" — *ghũghaṭa paṭa laṭaki paru*. "The new knot of the waist-cloth loosens" — *khasata naba nībibandha*: *nībi* is the knot by which the lower garment is tied; a loosening garment is, in the poetics of the padas, a sign of self-forgetfulness from bhava (cf. 9.6, note 7 of kirana 9). Translated exactly; on the Gaudiya Vaishnava position, see note 2. "Bite their sweet lips" — *madhurima adhara daṃśata*. "Say something" — *kahu kachu*. Signature: Narahari Dasa.

[^10-4]: The heading is "কাচিদাহ—যথারাগ" (*kācid āha*, "a certain one says"): by the subheading and 10.1–10.2, one of the heavenly women speaks, looking at the people of Nadiya.. "Friend" — *sajanī*. "Understood for certain" — *nicaya jāninu* (*niścaya*). "Has appeared" — *prakaṭa haiyāche*. "Drive mad" — *pāgala karite*. "In meditation [on Him]" — *dhiyāna* (*dhyāna*). "Their minds do not stay at home" — *mana nā thākaye ghare*. "As witness to it" — the print has *tāra sāthī* ("companion"); understood as *sākhī* ("witness"), as in the signature formula *narahari sāthī* (per the pada glossary). "Even lying down" — *śayane thākiyā*. "Bring no other [word] to their lips" — *mukhe nā ānaye āne*. "Sweet sound" — *madhura dhuni*: the morning singing about Gaura by the people of Nadiya. "Narahari's Lord" — *narahari-pãhu*: a signature formula. "Supremely skilled" — *parama guṇī*. Signature: Narahari.

[^10-5]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" (*kācic ca*, "and a certain [other]"): another heavenly woman speaks. The verse begins and ends with "॥৪॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a); the next portion begins with 10.5. "The beautiful city of Nadiya" — *sundara nadīyāpure*. "Auspicious splendour" — *śubha śobhā*. "Ah, ah!" — *mari mari* (cf. note 21 of kirana 8). "One is left bewildered" — *bujhite lāgaye dhanda*. "Leaving their beds, they wage a fierce struggle with the night" — *śayana tejiyā rajanī sahite karaye dāruṇa dbandba*: they do not sleep and impatiently await the end of the night. "No delay can be borne now" — *nā sahe byāja* (cf. 9.2). "Come what may" — *ye habe se habe*. "Narahari's Lord of life" — *narahari-prāṇanātha*. Signature: Narahari.

[^10-6]: Heading — "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" (Skt. *kācic ca*, "and a certain [other]"). The speaker is a heavenly woman (*devaramaṇī*), one of the celestial women of Kirana 10 who at dawn look down from the sky on Navadvipa; here she glorifies the nagaris of Nadia. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas on the words of the heavenly women see note 2. "Treasure of life" — *prāṇa-sarabasa* (*-sarvasva*). "Abode of virtues" — *guṇera rāśi*. "Be that as it may" — *ye hauka se hauka*. "Yugas" — world ages. "Love" — *neha* (*sneha*). "Unable to restrain themselves" — *dharite nāraye theha*. "Ah, ah, would that I could take upon myself all the misfortunes…" — *mari mari yena … bālāi laiyā*: *mari mari* is "ah, ah" (see note 5); *bālāi laiyā [mari]* is a Bengali expression of tender admiration (lit. "taking [your] misfortune [upon myself], let me die"). "Wives of [respectable] families" — *kulera ramaṇī*. "Shame and propriety" — *lāja dharama*. "The city of the gods" — *surapura*. "Shame, shame on these eyes!" — *dhik dhik rahu nayana*: *rahu* is taken as "let [them perish]"; the understanding is uncertain. "Beauty and youth" — *e-rūpa-yaubane*. "In Nadia" — the print has *nagyāte*, taken as *nadyāte* (cf. *nadyāra* in Kiranas 7–8); the reading is unclear. "Joining the company" — *saṅgati kariyā*. "Happy in Gora's happiness, they do not know any happiness of their own" — *gorā-sukhe sukhī … nā jāne āpana sukha*: the mark of pure prema is to rejoice only in the Beloved's joy (cf. CC Adi 4, on the gopis). "A grief greater than dying" — *maraṇa adhika … duḥkha*. "Narahari is witness to it" — *narahari sāthī* (*sākṣī*). Signature: Narahari.

[^10-7]: "Connoisseurs of rasa" — *rasikinī*. "Ah, would that I could take upon myself all the misfortunes of this wondrous beauty!" — *e naba śobhāra bālāi laiyā mari*; see note 6. "The roads of Nadia are [like] rivers" — *nadīyāra patha-nadī*: a play on *nadīyā* / *nadī* ("river"); the nagaris walking in lines are like rows of lotuses on a river. "Glance at one another" — *cāhani parasapare*. "Graceful gestures" — *bhaṅgimā*. "Necklaces on their breasts" — *hiyāra hāra*. "Have come loose" — *śithila āyulāyyā* (*āulāiyā*). "Strings of flowers" — *kusuma-tati*. "Not even for a moment" — *tila ādha*, lit. "for half a sesame seed". "To meet" — *bheṭite*. "Narahari's Lord" — *narahari pahu*. "As if knowing" — *jāni*: taken as "as if" (Brajabuli); possibly "I know: they will offer…"; the understanding is uncertain. "The treasure of their youth" — *yaubana sampada*: so the heavenly woman describes the nagaris' self-giving; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. "Auspiciously" — *maṅgala*: the ringing of anklets (*nūpura*) is considered auspicious. Signature: Narahari.

[^10-8]: The whole verse is about Gaura's radiance, which makes everything around seem golden. "Enchants the whole world" — *bhuvana-lobhā*. "Mansions" — *kanaka-mandira*: *mandira* here means "house, mansion". "Sport" — *bilasaye*. "Adorned" — *sājiche*. "Like gold" — *kanaka-pārā*. Jahnavi is the Ganga (daughter of the sage Jahnu); whiteness is her usual epithet in poetry. "What is this?" — *iki* (= *e ki*). "Like gold" (line 9) — *kanakamata*. "Power" — *pratāpa*. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pãhu*).

[^10-9]: This and the following verse (10.8–10.9) are also included in the anthology *Gaura-pada-tarangini* (3.1, nos. 129–130; not checked). "Radiance" — *chaṭā*. "Mass of lightning" — *bijurī-ghaṭā*. "Cloud" — *dhārādhara*. "Buds of the golden lotus" — *kanaka kamala-kalikā*: one letter has dropped out in print (*kamala?likā*); read by sense. "Finely ground" — *atiśaya marddita*. Gorochana is a bright yellow pigment used for tilaka. "The fresh layer of kunkuma on the breasts of the young maidens of Vraja" — *braja-naba-yubatī-kucera nabīna kuṅkuma-bhāra*: a poetic figure for Gaura's golden complexion; cf. CC Adi 4: Gaura took on the complexion and mood of Radha. Champaka is a golden-yellow flower. "Narahari is witness to it" — *narahari-sāthī*. Signature: Narahari.

[^10-10]: "Attire" — *beśa*. "Has come loose" — *āulāyyā paṛiche*. "Wreathed with kunda buds" — *kunda-kali-beṛā*: kunda is white jasmine. "Curling" — *cā̃cara*. "Beautifully adorned with dots of sandal paste" — *candana-bindu susāje*. "The lord of the stars" — *uṛupati*, the moon; the golden sky is Gaura's golden forehead. "I understand: He will steal" — *bujhiye … hariba se*: the form *hariba* is taken by sense as 3rd person. "Composure, propriety and modesty" — *dhairaja-dharama-sarama*. "Falling in incomparably graceful tiers" — *ati anupama kramabhaṅga*: *krama-bhaṅga*, lit. "successive curve", taken as the graceful tiers of the garland; possibly the curves of His figure; the understanding is uncertain. "What play this is" — *e kona raṅga*. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^10-11]: The heavenly woman answers her friend. "Dear as life" — *prāṇasama*. "Has come to my mind as well" — *haiyāche āmāra mane*. "He seems somehow strange" — *kemana kemana lāge*. "Clever one" — *catura*. "Overpowered, overcome by rasa" — *rasera āveśe abaśa*. "Make out" — *lekhite*: taken as "discern, notice" (*lakh-*); possibly "describe"; the understanding is uncertain. A nagara is the lover-hero of the padas. "Narahari's Moon" — *narahari cānda*. "Firmly" — *daṛa kari*. These are the heavenly women's guesses; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^10-12]: Here a new kind of mood: a heavenly woman, seeing in the morning Gaura's languid, reddened eyes and His disarranged attire, decides that He spent the night with some nagari. This is only her guess — the gossip of heavenly women watching from the sky; her friends at once argue about the "secret pastimes" (10.12–10.14), and further on another heavenly woman rebukes them for such words: Gaura is the son of the most learned Jagannatha Mishra, the lamp of his pure family, always walking the path of dharma and knowing nothing but the Vedas. According to the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15), in His lila the Lord did not jest with women or look at them. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. "Look, come here" — *hera āisa*. "Gorachanda" — *gorācānda*. "Do not puzzle over it any longer" — *āra nā bhābiha cite*. "Love" — *prīte*. At the end of line 5 the print has "॥" instead of "।". "Spent the night" — *rajanī karila bāsa*. "Granting her the touch of this body" — *e aṅga paraśa diyā*. "A nagari most expert in rasa" — *ati rasikā nāgarī*. "Crown of the nagaras" — *nāgara-śekhara*. "Did not let … sleep" — *ghumāite nā dile*. "Witness" — *sāthī* (*sākṣī*). "Languid … drooping" — *alase abaśa ḍhulu ḍhulu*. "Disorder in His attire" — *beśa biparīta*. "The secret pastimes … are manifest to rasikas" — *gupata bihāra bekata rasika-kāche*. Signature: "Narahari's Lord".

[^10-13]: "You have felt it well" — *anubhabi bhāla*. "Nagara-ways" — *nāgarālī*. "I am shy" — *saṅkoca bāsi*. "King of the clever" — *catura-rāja*. "Secret is My descent" — *gupata āmāra abatāra*: cf. *Shrimad-Bhagavatam* 7.9.38 — in the Kali-yuga the Lord comes hidden (*channaḥ kalau*). "The curves of My dance" — *naṭana bhaṅga*. "The play of love" — *pirīti-raṅga*. "Notice" — *lakhite*. "Covers the rays of the sun with His hand" — *dinakara-kiraṇa jhā̃paye kare*: a proverb for a vain attempt to hide the obvious. "Before one who is clever above the clever, can any cunning hold?" — *catura upare catura ye jana tāhe ki cāturī raya*; the understanding is uncertain. The speech of a heavenly woman; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord".

[^10-14]: Heading — "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" (Skt. *anyā ca*, "and another"). Another heavenly woman speaks. "Do not grieve" — *nā bāsiha duḥkha*. "What is open brings sorrow" — *bekata biṣaye biṣāda ghaṭaye*. "More carefully than life" — *parāṇa adhika*. "It is fitting to keep it hidden" — *gupata karile kāja*. "Crown of the adroit" — *sughara-śekhara* (*sughaṛa*, "adroit"). "Does anyone know another such?" — *jāne ki emana janā*; the understanding is uncertain. "His adroitness" — *sugharapanā*. Signature: "Narahari's Lord".

[^10-15]: Heading — "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "…নাগরী লাগিয়া ঝুরে ।"; the number "॥১৪॥" is missing in print, and the verse is immediately followed by a new heading "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ"; the number 10.14 is restored by sequence; the verse is translated in full (§ 3a). "Say what you will" — *ye bala se bala*. "Its nature is to become manifest" — *bekata svabhāba tāra*. "Like the sun" — *dinakara-sama*: an answer to the figure of 10.12 (covering the sun's rays with the hand). "Shachi's darling" — *śacīra dulāla*. "Young wives of good families" — *kulabatī yubatī*. "Thief of the jewel of composure" — *dhairaja-ratana-corā*. "Escape Him" — *eṛāite … ihāra ṭhā̃i*. "Young woman" — *dhanī*. "Irresistible" — *biṣama*. "Plays" — *bilāsa kare*. "Beloved of the nagaris" — *nāgarī-ballabha*. "Pines" — *jhure*. All this is the gossip of a heavenly woman; further on another heavenly woman objects to her friends, glorifying Gaura's strictness and learning (see note 12). For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^10-16]: Heading — "কাচিচ্চ—যথারাগ" (Skt. *kācic ca*, "and a certain [other]"). The verse is. The number at the end of the verse is missing in print (as at the end of 10.14); the next verse ends with "॥১৬॥", so the number 10.15 is restored by sequence. A heavenly woman speaks, agreeing with her friends (10.11–10.14); the tone of her praise (lines 5–8) may be either sincere or mocking. "Utterly unknowing" — *adhika abodha*. "Nagara-ways" — *nāgarāli*. "In Nadia" — in print *nagyāte*, as in 10.5 (see note 6); taken as *nadyāte*. "Supremely noble" — *parama udārapanā*. "Supreme connoisseurs of rasa" — *parama rasikinī*. "Pastimes" — *bihāra*. "Be that as it may" — *ye hauka se hauka*. "The darkness of my mind has gone" — *manera ā̃dhāra gela*. "Subject to young women" — *yubatī adhīna*: so says the heavenly woman; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari pãhu*).

[^10-17]: Heading — "যথারাগ" (yatharaga). A narrative verse: lines of two half-lines, rhymed in pairs. "Opinions" — *mata*. "Streams of nectar" — *amiyāra dhāra*. "From another, new group" — *āra eka naba yūthera ramaṇī*: *yūtha* is a group, a band of friends; she is not one of those who have spoken so far. "Narahari does not know her nature" — *narahari tāra rīti nā jānaye*: the poet speaks of himself in the signature. "Boldly" — *sāhase*..

[^10-18]: The verse begins and ends with "॥১৭॥". A new heavenly woman defends Gaura before her friends; her words agree with the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15): in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not look at them; cf. notes 2 and 12. "I have fallen into bewilderment" — *mui se paṛinu dhande*. "The moon of Nadia" — *nadyāra cānde* (in print the same ligature as *nagyāte* in 10.15). "The lamp of his spotless family" — *niramala kulera pradīpa*; at the beginning of line 4 a letter is damaged, read as *tāra*. "Conquered the conqueror of the quarters" — *digbijayī-jayī*: Gaura defeated in debate the digvijayi ("conqueror of the quarters") Keshava Kashmiri (CC, Adi 16). "Talk about women" — *prakṛti-prasaṅga* (*prakṛti*, "woman"). "Even inadvertently" — *bhuliyā*. "The faces of others' wives" — *para-ramaṇīgaṇera mukha*. The Suradhuni is the Ganga. "The proper atonement" — *ucita … parācita* (*prāyaścitta*). "Cannot regain His composure" — *nā sambita pāya*. "Deliberately" — *sādha kari*. "False blame" — *apabāda*. "Offence" — *aparādha*. "Narahari is witness to it" — *narahari-sāthī* (*sākṣī*). Signature: Narahari.

[^10-19]: Heading — "যথারাগ". A narrative verse of short half-lines;. "Her heart full to overflowing" — in print apparently *ati bharāntarī* (*bharā antara*, "a full heart"); the first letter is unclear (possibly *tbarāntarī*, "very hasty"); the reading is unclear. "To that place" — *sei thāne*. "There is no fear in her at all" — *nāhi kichu ḍara*. "Her friend" — *nija sakhī*: judging by 10.19, the one who spoke in 10.17.

[^10-20]: The verse begins and ends with "॥১৯॥". The heavenly woman of 10.18 speaks — first to her friend, then to all. "Empty commotion" — *michā prapañca*. "Do not be troubled" — *nā bāsaha dhanda*. "Good conduct" — *sadācāra*. "This is a great misfortune" — *e baṛa biṣama*. "It is forbidden to censure even one who has faults" — *doṣayukta jane duṣite niṣedha*. "Most worthy" — *parama sujana*. "Let alone looking at anyone" — *kāru pāne kabhu dekhibe thākuka*. "The shadow of other women" — *para-prakṛtigaṇera chāyā*. "Touching bodies and the like" — *aṅga-paraśādi*: an answer to 10.11 ("granting her the touch of this body"). "The ghats of the Suradhuni" — *suradhunī-ghāṭe*: ghats are bathing steps on the Ganga. "Crowd" — *ghaṭā*. "To a pond" — *sarobare*; the word is smudged in print; read by sense. "Rites" — *krīyā* (*kriyā*): the daily rites after bathing. "For fear of disgrace" — *kalaṅka-ḍare*. "The supreme Conqueror of the senses" — *mahājitendriya*. "Elixir" — *rasāyana*. "Companions" — *sahabāsī*, "those who live together", i.e. friends in the city of the gods. "The essence of my words" — *bacana-sāra*. Cf. CB, Adi 15 (see note 18). Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^10-21]: Heading — "যথারাগ". A narrative verse;. "Mocking women" — *raṅginī*, "playful, merry women": the friends who spoke in 10.10–10.15. "Words of instruction" — *upadeśa bāṇī*. "Amused" — *kautuka*. "Slyly" — *kariyā cāturī*. "With mocking gestures" — *bhaṅgi kari*.

[^10-22]: The verse begins and ends with "॥২১॥". One of the mocking women speaks; her praises of Gaura (lines 1–7) mock the defender. "Aha, ah, ah!" — *āhā mari mari* (see note 5). "Like the king of sages" — *munīndra-sadṛśa*. "Righteousness" — *dhārmikapanā*. "On any business" — *konai kāje*. "In the flashes of the sidelong glances of these young women" — *e naba yubatī kaṭākṣa chaṭāye*; possibly "of this young woman"; the understanding is uncertain. "Like a bird that does not see the moon of the night" — *pākhīsama … nā dekhe rajanīcānda*: probably an allusion to birds that, unlike the chakora, do not drink the moonlight; the understanding is uncertain. "A snare enchanting women" — *ramaṇī-mohana-phānda*. So says the heavenly woman; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^10-23]: Heading — "অস্যাঃ স্বপক্ষাহ—যথারাগ" (Skt. *asyāḥ svapakṣā āha*, "her ally says"): a friend of the mocking woman of 10.21 addresses her, pointing to Gaura's defender. The verse begins and ends with "॥২২॥". "Friend dear as life" — *prāṇa sajani*. "Secret words of rasa" — *nigūṛha rasakathā*. "Simple-hearted" — *saralā*. "It is no concern of hers" — *nāhika uhāra dāya*. "Can she whose attachment is to the formless ever come to love form?" — *nirākāre yāra ārati tāre ki ākāra kakhana bhāya*: a jibe — the defender is likened to worshippers of the formless Brahman, to whom the beauty of form is alien. "Rare" — *dulaha* (*durlabha*). "Sports" — *raṅga*. "How does He … play like this" — *kemane erūpa bilāsa kare*. "Not a trace of [such] joy" — *nāhi konai sukhera leśa*. "Take on the guise of a nagarini" — *dhare nāgariṇī beśa*: probably an allusion to how Gaura, playing in a sacred drama in the house of Chandrashekhara, took on the form of Lakshmi (CB, Madhya 18). "Dignity" — *bharama*. "Bee-like women" — *nārī-bhramarī*: women fly like bees to the fragrance of His body. "Swoons" — *muruchā yāya*; to which event this refers is unclear. "Such deeds" — *emana kāma*: here *kāma* means "deed" (*kāja*). "Repeating a woman's name" and "repeat [the name of] a woman in His mind" — *laiyā nārīra nāma*, *prakṛti japaye mane*: probably an allusion to how the Lord repeated the name "gopi, gopi" (CC, Adi 17); the understanding is conjectural. "Gather so many young women in His own house" — *nija bhabane … eta yubatī karaye jaṛa*: the women of Nadia come to Shachi's house to see Him (cf. 10.6). "Let her answer" — *balaha uttara dite*. "Faith" — *pratyaya*. All this is the mockery of a heavenly woman; in these pastimes Gaura is absorbed in Radha's bhava and in the bhava of a devotee; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pãhu*).

[^10-24]: Heading — "যথারাগ". A narrative verse;. "Mocking" — *raṅgiyā*. "That young woman" — *se dhanī*: Gaura's defender (10.17). "Abashed" — *lajjita*. "Great skill [in speech]" — *cāturī bhāri*. "At her own [companions]" — *nija-gaṇa pāne*: she is one of the mocking women and first addresses them.

[^10-25]: The verse begins and ends with "॥২৪॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a). At the end of line 18 the print has "।" instead of "॥". The heavenly woman first reproaches her friends (lines 1–6), then addresses Gaura's defender. "She is not at fault at all" — *ihāra nāhika doṣa*. "I will consider you wise" — *subuddhi bāsi*. "Of good character" — *sucarita*. "The corner of His eye" — *nayāna-koṇe*. "Then, though you assert it and swear oaths, I would still come to believe" — *niścaya baliyā śapatha khāiyā tathāpi pratyaya yāi*; the understanding is uncertain. "Beyond counting" — *nāhika tāhāra lekhā*. "In this descent" — *ei abatāre*. "Nature" — *sbabhāba*. "The whole world is witness" — *jagata sākhī*. "The son of Shachi is the son of Yashoda" — *śacīsuta yaśodānandana*. "Lifting the hill" — *giri-dhāraṇa*: Govardhana. "A moment … an aeon" — *tileka … kalapa*: *kalpa*, an aeon. "Hoping for the touch of [their] bodies" — *aṅgera paraśa-āśe*. "Proud one, in mana" — *mānabatī … māna kari*: mana is the jealous pique of the beloved. "Clasps the feet of [her] sakhi" — *dharena sakhīra pāya*. "Carries [her] on His shoulder" — *kāndhete kariyā bahe*: cf. Bhag. 10.30 (the gopi's request to be carried). "Hearing words of reproach, He rejoices more than at the hymns of the Vedas" — *bedastuti haite parama ānanda śuniyā bhartsana bāṇī*: cf. Krishna's words in CC, Adi 4: the reproaches of the beloved in mana captivate Him more than the hymns of the Vedas. "He takes no account" — in print *nā gaṇe keha* ("no one takes account"); by sense and by the next line (*teṃha*, "He") — of Krishna. "Crown of connoisseurs of rasa" — *rasikaśekhara*. This heavenly woman derives Gaura's nagara-ways from His being Krishna. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas agree that the son of Shachi is Krishna Himself, the son of Yashoda, but He came having accepted Radha's bhava and complexion in order to taste Her love (CC, Adi 1 and 4); in this lila He plays the role of a devotee and, according to the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15), did not jest with women and did not look at them. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pãhu*).

[^10-26]: Heading — "যথারাগ".; the whole of is taken up by the end of 10.24 (previous portion). The heavenly woman of 10.24 (see note 25) continues speaking, addressing Gaura's defender (10.17). "The special plays of rasa" — *rasa-bilāsa-biśeṣa*. "Keep hidden in your heart" — *gupate rākhiyā dio cita*. "Knowing you as my own" — *āpana jāniyā tore*. "This One is that very One, that very One is this" — *ei sei sei ei*: Gaura is the same Krishna of Vraja, with the same companions; this in itself is the teaching of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (cf. CC, Adi 1), but the heavenly woman's conclusion about Gaura's "ways of a nagara" is her own; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see notes 2 and 25. "Absorbed in His own virtues" — *nija guṇagaṇe magana haiyā*; possibly a hint that Gaura tastes His own sweetness (cf. CC, Adi 4); the understanding is uncertain. "First" — *āge*. "His own people" — *āpana jane*. "Minds and gait like those of the mad" — *unamata-mata mati gati*. "Reckon with nothing" — *kichu nā gaṇe*. "Hosts of wives of respectable families" — *kulabatī-kula*. "Having given tilanjali" — *tilāñjali diyā*: an idiom of complete renunciation (cf. 7.2). "The disgrace of their families" — *kula-kalaṅka*. "Have made their very essence" — *sāra kaila*. "Full of joy" — *sukhamaya*. "Narahari is witness to it" — *narahari sāthī* (= *sākhī*, cf. note 4).

[^10-27]: Heading — "যথারাগ".. "That young woman" — *dhanī*: Gaura's defender (10.17), who was abashed in 10.23 (note 24); having heard 10.24–10.25, she now asks to be taught how to settle in Nadiya and become a companion of the nagaris (cf. the heavenly woman's wish in 10.5); for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. "Lovely deeds" — *sucāru carita*. "Tears of prema" — *premanīre*. "Joining her palms" — *yuṛi kare kara*. "Accepting me as your own" — *nijajana jāni*. "At last" — *eta dine*, literally "after so many days". "I pray" — *nibediye*. "Even once" — *bāreka*. "A dwelling" — *basati*. "Playful ones" — *raṅginī-gaṇa*: the nagaris of Nadiya. "The grievous obstacle" — *dāruṇa bādhā*. "Desires" — *sādhā* (*sādha*). Signature: "Narahari's Beloved, dear as life" (*narahari prāṇapiyā*).

[^10-28]: The stanza begins and ends with "॥২৭॥" — the last stanza of kirana 10. The author's words. "The beautiful one" — *sundarī*: the heavenly woman of 10.24–10.25. "Accepting [them]" — *māni*. "Generously" — *adhika mate*, literally "in many ways". "With one mouth I cannot describe it" — *nāri bibarite tā eka mukhe*: the poet speaks. "Thrill with rapture" — *paripulakita*. "A matchless manner" — *anupama rīti*. "Not a trace of composure" — *dhṛti leśa*. "Leaning her body against another's" — *kāru aṅge aṅga helāiyā*: in their rapture the heavenly women lean on one another. "Exchanging winks" — *ṭhārāṭhāri kari*. "Absorbed in the sports of Ananga" — *anaṅga-raṅgete magana haiyā*: Ananga ("the Bodiless") is Kamadeva (per the glossary of padas); the picture of the heavenly women's longing is translated exactly; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. "Intoxicate" — *māte*. "Gaze at the Lord of Narahari" — *narahari-pãhu pānete cāyā*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari".

[^10-29]: The colophon was: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র বর্ণনে দেবরমণীনাং তৎ প্রেমাবিষ্ট-কৌতুকাদিবর্ণনং নাম দশমঃ কিরণঃ।"; there is no kirana number ("॥১০॥") in print. Kirana 10 comprises stanzas 10.1–10.27.


## Kirana 11. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Quarrels Born of Prema and So On among the Heavenly Women[^11-1]

*Morning: the heavenly women, gazing from the sky at Gaura in Shachi's courtyard, dispute among themselves about the nagaris of Nadiya and about Gaura Himself; one heavenly woman angrily defends the women of Nadiya as faithful wives, others answer her mockingly and tell how the women of Nadiya seek Gaura's glance on the roads and by the Ganga, while He, abashed, lowers His head and goes away; at the end Narahari prays to the Lord to keep him in Nadiya*

*Again, in the morning period — the quarrels born of prema and so on among the heavenly women.*

*Raga lalita*

**11.1.** Night has turned to dawn — what joy today! In the courtyard the moon of Nadiya sports.
The host of heavenly women, staying in the sky, drink the abundant nectar of Gora's beauty.
One says: "O, look, behold the young nagaris of my Nadiya:
gone mad for the Beloved, dear as life, they wander about, with nothing else in their minds."
One says: "Nadiya is a perilous [place], I have understood: it seems disgrace awaits all [there]."
One says: "It is fitting to do good — why does Gora do such deeds?"
One says: "Why, do you not know Him? What did He not do with the women of Vraja!"
One says: "In this business alone He finds joy: never has He spared shame — neither His own nor another's."
One says: "Why do the women of Nadiya act so, not knowing the fear of disgrace?"
One says: "She whose heart is inclined to love — will she reckon with such trifling disgrace?"
One says: "She whose cunning is strong — will ill fame ever spread about her?"
One says: "Narahari knows well: if you love, disgrace cannot be escaped."[^11-2]

*Yatharaga*

**11.2.** Many such things the heavenly women say.
In what wondrous attire they sport in the midst of the sky!
Their bodies are filled with the thrill of rapture — they cannot hold their composure.
How much rises in their hearts — can others understand this?[^11-3]

*Yatharaga*

**11.3.** The heavenly women are most virtuous — who can find a comparison for them?
Seeing the perfection of the prema of the young women of Nadiya, they are enchanted at heart.
Telling one another of the deeds of the nagaris of Nadiya, they grew unsteady at heart;
friends and companions float in tears — who can console and restrain [them]?
All are as if gone mad — what can be said of it! Becoming calm again, the women,
exclaiming, "Blessed, blessed are the young nagaris!", keep gazing toward the city of Nadiya.
Narahari says: at that moment a heavenly woman came with an impetuous gait,
and, swinging her arms, with many gestures, she angrily says to all:[^11-4]

*Yatharaga*

**11.4.** "Living in the city of the gods, why have you grown so proud?
Have you no fear at heart of reviling the women of Nadiya?
Alas, alas! Hearing such perverse words, how can I bear it?
Not knowing [them] — what wrong have all these women of Nadiya done to you all?
You judge others by your own ways, and do not know what lies deep in others;
you understand nothing — what comes from what; that is why I say: in vain do you bear a body.
What you speak of is possible only among the young women in the city of Vraja;
all the young women of Nadiya are excellent faithful wives: the people of the whole world know it.
They do not see the face of another's husband even in a dream; they never walk the wrong path;
they know no trickery, deceit or guile — all of them always have simple hearts.
They are steadfast, judicious in their work, their lovely inclination is on the path of the highest dharma;
guarding beyond compare the modesty and honour of their families, never, even by oversight, do they sit with wicked people.
They all love their elders as their life; their auspicious virtues I cannot count.
In my heart there is one thing: always to keep them all with care within my eyes.
That is why I speak [to you], knowing you as friends — you will surely accept my words; to others I do not say [this].
Whoever sings of another's disgrace is herself disgraced — this Narahari knows."[^11-5]

*Yatharaga*

**11.5.** Hearing these words, a certain rasika
with her own [friends] laughs inwardly.
Who can fathom her cunning? She exchanges winks [with them]
and, looking at that one, speaks with a [sly] gesture:[^11-6]

*Yatharaga*

**11.6.** "Fine, fine! There is no need to teach us this — as if I did not know all this!
On earth the faithful wives are the women of Nadiya, and in the city of the gods — you alone!
Ceaselessly singing of others' disgrace, we shall all become disgraced;
do not worry about it: we shall never make you a sharer in it.
But you, so wise a woman — how will you live alone in the city of the gods?
If you live with the unchaste, you will easily become unchaste yourself.
So I say: go to this city of Nadiya, taking with you your dharma, modesty and the rest —
Narahari is glad at this — and live always with care, in the company of the chaste."[^11-7]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.7.** "Look, come here, O: why are you saying all this to [that] faithful wife?
Between equals happiness arises; with one who is unequal, pain increases.
Never think this in your mind: 'Since they are women of the gods, are they all excellent?'
As the owl does not see the sun — know that many people are like this.
All the young women of Nadiya, youthful and skilful — who in the world is their equal?
Their very secret stories I cannot reveal for shame.
Here, look, look: the life of our life, the beautiful, noble Gora:
He will not raise His face and speak to anyone — a wondrous nature, wholly absorbed within;
always careful on the path of dharma — what shall I say! — He knows nothing of all this.
And such a Lord of Narahari they have captivated, making signs with the corners of their eyes!"[^11-8]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.8.** "What shall I say, O, of the ways of the young women of Nadiya!
What is in their hearts they do not reveal; outwardly they are always most gracious.
Beside their mothers-in-law and sisters-in-law they always keep well-mannered;
whatever everyone praises, that they do, taking great pains.
In how many ways they accomplish their own aims — no one can ever detect it;
to bring the moon of Nadiya under their sway, they go about most secretly.
With my own eyes I saw that day with how many gestures they enchanted [Him].
Who would have restrained the women? Narahari was not with Gauranga."[^11-9]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.9.** "How many, many such amusements there are in Nadiya — how much of it will you tell!
What all these young women are like — chaste, excellent faithful wives — that I know.
That day I saw with my own eyes, standing under a young kadamba tree:
Gora was walking alone toward Murari Gupta's quarter in the afternoon.
At that time the faithful wives come with their stern mothers-in-law and sisters-in-law.
And still she stops and, on some pretext, with a gesture, finding the moon Gora on the nearby road,
making signs again and again, she entices Him, not keeping even half the end of her garment over her breast.
Narahari's Lord, His face lowered in shame, stands far off, to one side."[^11-10]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.10.** "What shall I say, O? I feel much hesitation to speak of all these things.
For fear of young women the noble, beautiful moon of Nadiya is always trembling.
I do not know on what errand that day Gora went alone with the gait of an elephant.
He does not look at anyone even once — exceedingly gentle, wholly absorbed within.
On that road the faithful women, stopping, look toward Gauranga;
unnoticed, again and again they shoot the sharpest arrows from the corners of their restless eyes.
[One], with a gesture, letting her own shadow touch Gora's body, smiles slightly.
Another, as if by chance, lets the sound of the bracelet on her hand be heard — and floats in an ocean of joy.
Another, taking a fine pomegranate in her hand, says: 'To whom shall I give this wonder?'
Another says: 'He whose body is new gold will take it Himself, unasked.'
Such words they speak to one another — who, hearing that, could remain steady?
The Lord of Narahari, collecting Himself, in embarrassment did His errand and quickly went home."[^11-11]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.11.** "Of the chaste young women of Nadiya — to whom shall I tell the story of their conduct?
Ai, ai, ai! Who could keep composure before the signs of their eyes?
That day at the ghat of the Suradhuni, at bathing time, they found Gauranga, [dear to them] as life.
Unnoticed, laughing, holding on to one another, they splash water near Gora as if by chance.
One, rubbing turmeric on another's body, says: 'Golden-fair one! Listen to what I say:
to Him whose body is gold I will give you — then the pain of your heart will go.'
Washing their clothes, rubbing them with their hands, placing a lovely fine silk cloth over the breast,
some, turning half aside, wring out their hair, looking toward Gora.
At this Narahari, his heart full of shame, seeing it from afar
and knowing it was untoward, quickly finished bathing and, taking Gauranga, went home."[^11-12]

**11.12.** …Having bathed in the Suradhuni, [He] hurries along the road with bowed head.
\* \* \* \*
"There the most faithful wives, ever-new young women, intoxicated with the pride of youth,
gazing at Gora, smile softly, softly, [making] all kinds of gestures with the corners of their eyes.
One says to another: 'O, if you obtain this new golden form,
