then day and night worship this God deep in your heart — and be content.'
'Look, see: the lotus of my breast, without the worship of the God, has closed in sorrow.'
Hearing these words, the moon Gaura covers His face with the end of His cloth for shame.
Seeing that, another says: 'Let this simple one hide like this — what of it?
I shall hide Him in the temple of my heart — no one will be able to see.'
Saying this, playfully, with a restless heart she devises many pretexts to touch His body.
The Lord of Narahari's life, realizing this, trembles; His cloth is soaked with heavy sweat."[^11-13]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.13.** "Even to speak of the ways of all the faithful wives of Nadiya brings shame to the heart.
On various pretexts they go out on the road and, finding the moon Gora, make Him hear — in how many ways!
One says to another: 'This is Nanda's son, dark Kanu.
I have heard from people's lips: there — a dark form; here — a body that surpasses gold.'
Another says: 'I do not think so: if He were the dark nagara,
would the jewel of youth of the young maidens of the town of Nadiya have remained [intact]?'
Another says: 'I have considered it well: He is surely the son of Dasharatha;
otherwise why, at a tender youthful age, would He be so devout?'
Another says: 'The son of the king of brahmanas knows austerity and japa — where would He learn this?'
Another says: 'By staying long with Him I shall easily teach [Him] this here.'
Another says: 'Friend, [He] is entrusted to you — teach [Him] right now, and let the heart be soothed.'
Hearing this, the Lord of Narahari, in fear, goes home and performs atonement."[^11-14]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.14.** "Listen, listen: I shall tell you yesterday's story, casting off shame.
Many young women, in great joy, take counsel with great care.
One says: 'O, do not delay — take your pitchers, let us go for water:
the moon of Nadiya will come to the ghat of the Suradhuni — the time for His coming has arrived.'
Another says: 'Why go like this? Dress yourselves in all sorts of ways,
so that by a mere trace of that splendour the young Kishora cannot keep His composure even for half a sesame seed.'
Another says: 'Braid your hair and adorn your heads with all kinds of flowers,
so that by their fragrance even the Conqueror of the senses cannot turn His nose away even once.'
Another says: 'Rub your faces with kunkuma, brighten your eyes with kajal,
so that Gauranga's eyes are charmed, seeing a graceful new charm.'
Another says: 'Put on your necks various jewelled necklaces, like a lovely noose,
so that by it the moon of Nadiya, beautiful Gora, is easily caught.'
Another says: 'Bring and put on jewelled nupuras and belts of little bells — choose ringing ones —
so that Gaura's two ears are charmed, hearing the sweet, sweet sound.'
Saying much like this to one another, they all adorned themselves in delight;
each one looked at her face in a mirror and quickly chewed tambula.
Wearing colourful garments, all very lively, with pitchers on their hips,
they came out from this house and that — with what joy in their hearts!
Laughing and laughing, all of the same age, full of rasa — that assembly of faithful wives —
they go along the bank of the Suradhuni, lighting it up — what shall I say of the wondrous splendour of their beauty!
In the rapture of rasa, holding hands, glancing with slight, slight gestures,
on many pretexts they tell stories of rasa, having found Gaura in the middle of the road.
Then the excellent Gaura, the supreme pandita, stands with bowed head, keeping His composure.
Suspecting something most untoward, He turned once and looked toward them.
At that moment they all rain thick arrows of sidelong glances from the corners of their eyes.
At once abashed, the Jewel of virtues again ponders in fear of disgrace.
What happened — what shall I say! Only because Narahari was with Gauranga
did His modesty and dignity remain; otherwise there would have been trouble on the road."[^11-15]

*And another — yatharaga*

**11.15.** "What shall I say? [She] spoke rejecting all this — how much can one bear?
What the ways of the women of Nadiya are — with that the whole world is filled.
Their mothers-in-law, sisters-in-law, husbands and the rest all scold them constantly;
every day [they] worship Old Shiva with such fervour, [fearing] that there will be disgrace;
all the time they carefully keep them at home and do not let them go out on the road;
if [one of them] wishes to bathe in the Suradhuni, her sister-in-law goes with her.
The neighbour women ceaselessly hold [them] back; no one trusts [them] in what they do.
What more shall I say? Hearing those reproaches, Narahari is consumed with shame every day."[^11-16]

*Yatharaga*

**11.16.** All the playful women
spoke much in this way.
Hearing it, that young woman was abashed —
no word comes from her lips.
Seeing this, a certain playful one
cannot contain herself for joy
and, looking toward her own [companions],
speaks with cunning:[^11-17]

*Yatharaga*

**11.17.** "In the city of the gods, who does not know what the ways of the nagaris of Nadiya are?
Why then are you so needlessly angry with her?
What can I say? All that she said is most secret — none of you knows it.
Have patience, all of you, and listen carefully to what I say."
Having thus stopped her own [friends], she quickly took the other by the hand
and, praising [her] in many ways, speaks very softly, brimming with rasa:
"What the ways of all the young women of Nadiya are — who can tell?
On how many days, how many amusing things I have seen with my own eyes — and I tell no one of them.
But let that be: no one looks at the bracelet on her own hand in a mirror.
Just look: why does a woman come running in the morning to Ai's house?
If you say: at the auspicious word of an astrologer she came to Ai on her own business —
then why does she, unblinking, keep gazing at Gora, making her brows dance?
And her body trembles again and again, her garment and ornaments slip, the knot of her hair comes loose;
a wave of the thrill of rapture, sweat trickles, the champaka at her ear sways without pause.
Who acts like this — tell me, tell me? Why should anyone not believe it?
Narahari's Lord is the Master (*pati*) of all: have no doubt of this in your heart."[^11-18]

*Yatharaga*

**11.18.** The beauty, with words full of rasa dispelling all the doubts of [her] heart —
the supremely skilful rasika — made her her own once more.
All, becoming one, staying in the sky, were deeply immersed in the rasa of Gauranga;
holding on to one another, jesting, they float in an ocean of happiness.
In Shachi's courtyard the moon Gaurahari sports amid [His] dear devotees.
Gazing at Him, their hearts thrilled with rapture, distraught, they bade farewell to shame;
intoxicated, as if utterly mad — no one listens to anyone;
ceaselessly they sing songs of the lovely deeds of the Lord of Narahari's life.[^11-19]

*Modaka-chhandah*

**11.19.** The host of heavenly women is immersed in the greatest delight and rasa.
The incomparable course of the new prema — how can it be described? There is no end to it.
Joining both palms, Narahari entreats Him:
"O Lord full of rasa, keep [me] in Nadiya together with Your associates!"[^11-20]

*Thus [ends] the eleventh kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Quarrels Born of Prema and So On among the Heavenly Women", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^11-21]

[^11-1]: The name of the kirana is taken from its colophon: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্রচিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে দেবরমণীনাং প্রেমকলহাদিবর্ণনং নাম একাদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১১॥" — "the eleventh kirana, called 'The Description of the Quarrels Born of Prema (*prema-kalaha*) and So On among the Heavenly Women (*deva-ramaṇī*)', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". *Prema-kalaha* — literally "a quarrel of prema", a dispute born of prema: the heavenly women argue about the nagaris of Nadiya and about Gaura. The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the quarrels born of prema and so on among the heavenly women" Kirana 11 comprises stanzas 11.1–11.19.

[^11-2]: Raga lalita is a morning raga (cf. 9.18).. Here a new kind of the heavenly women's mood begins — a dispute (*prema-kalaha*) about the nagaris of Nadiya and about Gaura Himself: some speak of Him as a nagara who "did everything" with the women of Vraja (lines 7–8). Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Shrila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati and the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not even look at them), reject gaura-nagari-vada — the worship of Gaura as an enjoying lover (*nāgara*) and of oneself as His nagari: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer. The heavenly women's words are their own conjectures: in 11.4 one of them angrily rejects them (the women of Nadiya are faithful wives who do not see the face of another's husband even in a dream; what her friends speak of "is possible only in the city of Vraja"), and further on in the kirana, on meeting the women of Nadiya, Gaura lowers His head in shame and stands aside or hurries home (11.10–11.11). Further notes in this kirana refer to this note (cf. the main note of kirana 10 — note 2). "In the courtyard" — *aṅgane*: the courtyard of Shachi's house. "The moon of Nadiya" — *nadyāra śaśī*. "Abundant nectar" — *amiyā-rāśi*, literally "a heap of nectar". "My Nadiya" — *mora nadīyāra*: literally "my"; perhaps an affectionate turn of phrase; the understanding is uncertain. "Gone mad" — *pāgalī haiyā*. "Perilous [place]" — *biṣama*. "Disgrace" — *kalaṅka*. "Never has He spared shame — neither His own nor another's" — *nija para lāja nā thuila*. "Act so" — *kena kare*: the last syllable is indistinct in print, possibly *kaye* ("say"). "Inclined to love" — *pirītera mana*. "Cunning" — *cāturi*. "Will ill fame spread" — *kalaṅka raṭe*. Signature: Narahari.

[^11-3]: Heading — "যথারাগ".; the print is faint, and the lines were checked under magnification. The stanza consists of short half-lines with a caesura (cf. 10.23). The author's words. "Attire" — *sāja*. "Thrill of rapture" — *pulaka*. "Composure" — *thehā* (cf. 10.5). "Can others understand this" — *ihā ki bujhibe āne*.

[^11-4]: . The author's words. "Most virtuous" — *guṇabatī ati*. "The perfection of prema" — *prema-paripāṭī*. "Unsteady at heart" — *athira hiyā*. "Friends and companions" — *sāthī sahabāsī*. "Console and restrain" — *rākhite pāre prabodhiyā*. "Keep gazing" — *cāhi rahe*. "A heavenly woman" — *debāṅganā*. "With an impetuous gait" — *cañcala-gati*. "Swinging her arms" — *jhulāiyā kara*. The last line ends with "says something to all" (*kahe kichu sabāra prati*); the speech follows in 11.4.

[^11-5]: The stanza begins and ends with "॥৪॥". The angry heavenly woman of 11.3 speaks. "Reviling" — *paribāda dite*. "Perverse words" — *biparīta bāṇī*. "What lies deep" — *marama kathā*. "In vain do you bear a body" — *deha dharile bṛthā*. "Possible only among the young women in the city of Vraja" — *sambhaba kebala brajapure naba ramaṇī-gaṇe*: what her friends say of the nagaris of Nadiya, in her words, befits only the love of the young gopis of Vraja for Krishna, while the women of Nadiya are faithful wives; this accords with what is said in note 2. "Excellent faithful wives" — *supatibratā*. "The wrong path" — *kupatha*. "Steadfast" — *dhairyabatī*. "Judicious in their work" — *kārye bicakṣaṇā*. "The modesty and honour of their families" — *kulalāja bhaya*. "With wicked people" — *kujana-sāthe*. "Elders" — *gurujana*. "To keep within my eyes" — *ā̃khimājhe rākhi*. "Knowing you as friends" — *sahabāsī jāni*. "To others I do not say [this]" — *nā kahi āne*; possibly "I say nothing else (nothing untrue)"; the understanding is uncertain. "Is herself disgraced" — *sei kalaṅkī*. Signature: Narahari.

[^11-6]: . The stanza consists of short half-lines with a caesura (cf. 11.2). "Rasika" — *rasikinī*, one who knows rasa. "Laughs inwardly" — *hāse mane mane*. "Exchanges winks" — *ṭhārāṭhāri kare*. "At that one" — *kāru pāne*, literally "at someone": at the defender of 11.4. "With a [sly] gesture" — *bhaṅgī kari*.

[^11-7]: . The mocking woman of 11.5 speaks; the whole speech is ironic. "As if I did not know all this" — *e sakala kathā nā jāniye āmi*, literally "all this I do not know"; understood as mockery; the understanding is uncertain. "On earth" — *abanīte*. "Ceaselessly" — *anukhaṇa*. "A sharer" — *bhāgī*. "So wise" — *ati caturā* (ironic). "The unchaste" — *asatī*. "Modesty and the rest" — *lajjādi*. "With care" — *sābadhāne*. "In the company of the chaste" — *satī saṃhati haiyā*: among the "chaste" women of Nadiya whom she praised. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. Signature: Narahari.

[^11-8]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ" (Skt. *anyā ca*, "and another").. Verse 11.6 ends at the bottom of. The speaker is a friend of the mocking woman of 11.5–11.6, addressing her: there is no point in arguing with the defender of the women of Nadiya in 11.4 (whom she ironically calls a "faithful wife"); the whole speech is mocking. "To [that] faithful wife" — *pativratā-saha*. "Between equals… with one who is unequal" — *samāne samāne… asamāna sane*. "Excellent" — *subara*: the reading in the 1947 edition is clear; the sense is taken as "excellent, wise"; the understanding is uncertain. "As the owl does not see the sun" — *bhāskara yaiche nā here uluka*: the defender does not see what is obvious. "Youthful and skilful" — *nabīnā prabīṇā*. "The life of our life" — *prāṇa jībana*. "Wholly absorbed within" — *parama bhorā*. "Captivated" — *bhulāila*. "Making signs with the corners of their eyes" — *ṭhārāṭhāri kariyā ā̃khira koṇe*. The words that Gaura is "captivated" are the mocking guesses of the heavenly women; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^11-9]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ".. Another of the mocking heavenly women speaks; her praise of the women of Nadiya is ironic. "Most gracious" — *udāra ati*. "Well-mannered" — *sujana haiyā*. "Detect" — *lakhite*. "Most secretly" — *adhika gupata haiyā*. "I saw" — *dekhinu* (the speaker is a heavenly woman). "Narahari was not with Gauranga" — *narahari gaurāṅgera saṅge nā chila*: through his signature the poet places himself in the scene as Gaura's companion; cf. 11.11 and 11.14, where Narahari protects Gauranga. See note 2.

[^11-10]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins at the bottom of and ends with "॥৯॥". "Amusements" — *kautuka*. "Excellent faithful wives" — *supatibratā* (ironic; cf. 11.4). "Under a young kadamba tree" — *nabīna kadamba-tale*. "Murari Gupta's quarter" — *murārī-guptera pāṛā*: Murari Gupta, the physician, Gaura's companion in Navadvipa. "In the afternoon" — *bikāla bele*: the last letter of the first word looks in print like "শ"; the reading *bikāla* is adopted; the story is about a past day, not the present morning. "Stern" — *biṣama*. "Entices" — *lubhāiye*. "Not keeping even half the end of her garment over her breast" — *ādha-paṭāñcala nā rākhi ure*: this candid passage is translated exactly; it is the account of a mocking heavenly woman, while Gaura Himself lowers His face in shame and stands far off; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^11-11]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ".. "I feel much hesitation" — *adhika saṅkoca bāsi*. "The moon of Nadiya" — *nadyāra śaśī*. "With the gait of an elephant" — *kuñjara-gamane*: with a stately gait. "Wholly absorbed within" — *parama bhorā* (cf. 11.7). "The sharpest arrows" — *kharatara śara*. "Letting her own shadow touch Gora's body" — *nija chāyā gorā-aṅge paraśa kariyā*. "The sound of the bracelet" — *kaṅkaṇa śabada*. "A fine pomegranate" — *sudāḍimba phala*; "will take it Himself, unasked" — *ayācita tẽha āpani niba*: in poetry the pomegranate is a conventional comparison for the breast; the double meaning is rendered literally; see note 2. "Collecting Himself" — *dhṛti dhari*. "Did His errand" — *kāja sādhi*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari pa̐hu*).

[^11-12]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "॥১১॥". "The story of their conduct" — *carita-kāhinī*. "The signs of their eyes" — *ā̃khera ṭhāra*. "Gauranga, [dear to them] as life" — *prāṇa-gaurāṅga*. "At the ghat of the Suradhuni" — *suradhunī-ghāṭe* (the Suradhuni is the Ganga). "Holding on to one another" — the reading is unclear: after *āne āne* the print has *dhara* or *khara*, the end of the word worn away; *dhari* ("holding") is adopted; another reading is possible. "Splash water" — *jala pheleye*. "Turmeric" — *haladi*. "Golden-fair one" — *hema-gauri*, addressing a friend. "Him whose body is gold" — *hema tanu yāra*: Gaura. "Fine silk cloth" — *cina cīra* (*cīna-cīra*, "Chinese cloth"). "Turning half aside" — *terachha*. "Wring out" — *niṅgāṛaye*. "Untoward" — *biparīta*. "Finished bathing" — *sināiyā*; possibly "having bathed [Gauranga]". Here Narahari is a participant in the scene, Gaura's companion (cf. note 9). This candid passage is translated exactly; see note 2.

[^11-13]: . There is no metre heading before the verse. After "॥১১॥" a separate line is printed — "সুরধুনী স্নান করিয়া তুরিতে অবনত মাথে চলয়ে পথে ॥". The line and the asterisks are kept as in the edition; the rest of the text up to "॥১২॥" is 11.12. The sense of the line: Gaura, having bathed, hurries along the road with His head bowed. "There" — *tathā*. "Intoxicated with the pride of youth" — *yaubana-garabe māti*. "Golden form" — *kanaka-pratimā*: Gaura's golden form. "Deep in your heart" — *manera mānase*. "Be content" — *prasanna hao*. "The lotus of my breast… has closed in sorrow" — *uraja-kamala… mudita duḥkhe*: apparently spoken by the one who was addressed (the text does not say); this candid passage is translated exactly; see note 2. "The moon Gaura" — *gaura-śaśī*. "Simple one" — *abodha*. "In the temple of my heart" — *hṛdi mandire*. "Trembles… soaked with heavy sweat" — *kā̃pe ghana ghāme basana tite*: from fear and shame (cf. 11.13, "in fear"). Signature: "the Lord of Narahari's life" (*narahari-prāṇanātha*).

[^11-14]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "॥১৩॥". "Make Him hear" — *śunāyaye*: [their words]. "Nanda's son, dark Kanu" — *nandera nandana kāliyā kānu*: Krishna. "There… here" — *sethā… hethā*: in Vraja and in Nadiya. "The dark nagara" — *kāliyā nāgara*. "Would the jewel of youth… have remained [intact]" — *yaubana-ratana raita*: lit. "would have remained". "The son of Dasharatha" — *daśaratha-suta*: Rama, the model of faithfulness to one wife. "At a tender youthful age" — *naba kiśora-bayase*. "The son of the king of brahmanas" — *dvijarāja-suta*: the son of Jagannatha Mishra. "Austerity and japa" — *tapa japa*. "Where would He learn this" — *ihā śikhibe kothā*: [the science of love]. "By staying long with Him" — *ihā saha sahabāsa bahu*, lit. "through long staying with Him". "Entrusted" — *sa̐pila*. "Let the heart be soothed" — *juṛāuka hiyā*. "Performs atonement" — *parācita* (*prāyaścitta*); cf. 10.17. The words of the heavenly women are their own guesses; Gaura goes away in fear; see note 2. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari pa̐hu*).

[^11-15]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ". The verse begins and ends with "॥১৪॥" (28 lines). "Yesterday's story" — *kālikāra kathā*. "Casting off shame" — *nilaji haiyā*. "Take counsel" — *yugati* (*yukti*). "Let us go for water" — *jalake cala*. "The young Kishora" — *naba-kiśora*. "Even for half a sesame seed" — *tila ādha*: not even for a moment. "The Conqueror of the senses" — *jitendriya* (cf. 10.19). "Kunkuma" — *kuṅkume*; "kajal" — *kājare*. "Noose" — *phānda*. "Nupuras" — ankle bells; "belts of little bells" — *kiṅkiṇī*. "Choose ringing ones" — *mukharita dekhi*. "Tambula" — *tāmbula*, betel. "With pitchers on their hips" — *kalasī laiyā kā̃khe*. "Of the same age" — *samabayaḥ*. "Full of rasa" — *rasiyā*. "Assembly of faithful wives" — *pativratāra ghaṭā* (ironic). "Stories of rasa" — *rasa-kāhinī*. "The supreme pandita" — *parama paṇḍita*. "Suspecting something most untoward" — *ati biparīta kriyā anumāni*. "Arrows of sidelong glances" — *kaṭākṣa bāṇa*. "The Jewel of virtues" — *guṇamaṇi*. "Modesty and dignity" — *sarama bharama*. "Trouble" — *biṣama*. Here Narahari is Gaura's companion, protecting Him (cf. notes 9 and 12). Gaura stands with bowed head and ponders in fear of disgrace; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2.

[^11-16]: The heading is "অন্যা চ—যথারাগ".. The whole speech is ironic: the "faithfulness" of the women of Nadiya consists in their relatives guarding them. "Rejecting all this" — *iha sabāre nirasi*: of the defender of 11.4; the understanding is uncertain. "Scold" — *pāṛaye gāli*. "Old Shiva" — *buṛā śibe*: possibly Buro Shiva, the Deity of Shiva worshipped in Navadvipa; who worships — the relatives or the women themselves — is not clear from the text; "[fearing] that there will be disgrace" — *kalaṅka haiba bali*, lit. "saying: there will be disgrace"; the understanding is uncertain. "Sister-in-law" — *nanadī*. "Neighbour women" — *paṛasinī*. "No one trusts [them] in what they do" — *nā pratyaya karaye kāje*. "Reproaches" — *gañjanā*. "Is consumed with shame" — *maraye lāje*. Signature: Narahari.

[^11-17]: The heading is "যথারাগ". The verse begins at the bottom of and ends at the top of with "॥১৬॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a); the next portion begins with 11.17. The verse is in short half-lines with a caesura (cf. 11.2, 11.5). The author's words. "Playful" — *raṅgiyā*, *raṅgiṇī*. "That young woman" — *dhanī*: the defender of the women of Nadiya from 11.4. "Cannot contain herself" — *nā thāke theha*. "With cunning" — *cāturī kari*.

[^11-18]: Heading — "যথারাগ".; the two top lines of are the end of 11.16 (previous portion). The speaker is the "playful woman" of 11.16 (lines 3–4): first to her friends (lines 1–4), then, taking by the hand the abashed defender of the women of Nadiya from 11.4 (lines 5–16). "With her" — *ihāra prati*: with the defender. "Most secret" — *ati gūṛha*: possibly a hint that the defender knows more than she says; the understanding is uncertain. "Having stopped" — *nirasiyā*. "Quickly" — *turite*: the first letter is indistinct (possibly *bhurite*). "Brimming with rasa" — *rasera bhare*. "Ways" — *bhaṅgī*. "No one looks at the bracelet on her own hand in a mirror" — *keha nija kara-kaṅkaṇa nā dekhe darpaṇa diyā*: a proverb (cf. Bengali *hātera kā̃kana dekhite ārasi lāge nā*) — the obvious needs no proof. "Look" — printed *dega*, a misprint for *dekha*. "Come running" — *... ki lāgi dhāyā*; the word after *prātaḥkāle* is unclear (*āisa*? *āila*?) — reading unclear; the sense of the line is not affected. "Ai" — *āi*, Shachi. "At the auspicious word of an astrologer" — *śubha daibajña-bacane*: i.e. on a plausible pretext. "Unblinking" — *animikha ā̃khe*. "Making her brows dance" — *bhrū nācāyā*. "The knot of her hair" — *culera khõpā*. "A wave of the thrill of rapture" — *pulakera ghaṭā*. "Sweat trickles" — *gharama cuiche*. "The champaka at her ear" — *kāṇera cā̃pā*, a champaka flower behind the ear. "Master of all" — *pati sabākāra*: *pati* is "master, lord" and also "husband"; in the playful woman's mouth it is a hint in the spirit of the heavenly women's disputes; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari pahũ*).

[^11-19]: Heading — "যথারাগ". The stanza begins and ends with "॥১৮॥". The author's words. "The beauty" — *sundari!*: printed with an exclamation mark, as an address; understood as the subject — the playful woman of 11.17; the understanding is uncertain. "Dispelling all the doubts of [her] heart" — *mana sandeha sakala kariyā dūre*. "The supremely skilful rasika" — *parama caturā rasikiṇī*. "Made her her own once more" — *puna āpana kariyā nilena tāre*: the defender is reconciled with her friends. "Becoming one" — *sabhe eka haiyā*. "Holding on to one another" — *paraspara kara dharādhari*, literally "holding one another's hands". "The moon Gaurahari" — *gaurahari-śaśī*. "Bade farewell to shame" — *dilā tilāñjali ... lāje*: tilanjali is an idiom of complete renunciation (cf. 10.25). "Distraught" — *āola*: understood as *āula* ("bewildered, beside oneself"); another understanding ("to the shame [that had come]") is possible; the understanding is uncertain. "As if utterly mad" — *parama prabala pāgalīra pārā*. "Intoxicated" — *māti*. "The Lord of Narahari's life" — *narahari-prāṇanātha*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2.

[^11-20]: Heading — "মোদক ছন্দঃ".. The author's words. "Immersed in delight and rasa" — *kautuka rasabhora*. "Course" — *gati*. "There is no end to it" — *nahu ora*. "Joining both palms" — *jori yugala kara*. "Entreats Him" — *minati tāya*. "Keep [me] in Nadiya together with Your associates" — *rasamaya pahũ parikarasaha rākhaha nadīyāya*: the word "me" is not expressed in the text; it may also be understood as "O Lord [abiding] with Your associates, keep [me] in Nadiya".

[^11-21]: The colophon was:, immediately after 11.19 — "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌর-চরিত্রচিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে দেবরমণীনাং প্রেমকলহাদিবর্ণনং নাম একাদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১১॥".


## Kirana 12. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Anuraga and So On of the Hosts of Gods[^12-1]

*Morning: the hosts of gods, motionless in the sky above Shachi's courtyard, behold Gaura amid His associates; they tell one another of the love of the people of Nadiya for Him and of His childhood plays, glorify His beauty — the union of Gauri and Shyama — dancing, sing garlands of His names, and at last, taking human form, descend to earth*

*Again, in the morning period — the anuraga and so on of the hosts of gods.*

*Raga bhairava*

**12.1.** Today night has turned to dawn — Shachi's courtyard shines, perfectly pure, wondrously lovely.
There the beautiful Gaurachanda sports, surrounded by His associates, the Remover of others' sorrow.
Who can describe this delight? There is no end to it.
The hosts of gods with their retinues, on the path of the sky, motionless, are immersed in boundless happiness. *(Refrain)*
The charming face that subdues the pride of hundreds of millions of Madanas, the nectarean sweet smile.
They gaze — their eyes do not blink, their thoughts tremble, in their hearts ever more rejoicing.
They deem [their lives] fulfilled, they blossom [with joy]; again and again a strong trembling, a fresh thrill of rapture in lovely rows.
Intoxicated with the highest rasa, they worship the pair of lotuses — the hands and feet of Narahari's Lord.[^12-2]

*Keshari-chhandah*

**12.2.** The hosts of gods, absorbed [in bliss], beautiful, glitter in the midst of the sky;
throng upon throng, in heart-delighting circles, they sway, brimming with rasa.
From their eyes, as from the clouds of the month of Shravana, tears of prema pour unceasingly;
in incomparable rejoicing they again speak sweet words to one another.[^12-3]

*Someone says — raga lalita*

**12.3.** "Look: the supreme, incomparable mystery — in Mishra's house!
In the lovely morning hour, cries of 'Glory, glory!' resound there.
Seeing Gaura's face, the people rejoice and cannot keep their composure;
in dense thrills of rapture, trembling ceaselessly, they shed streams of tears from their eyes.
Carried away by the stream of the nectar of [His] beauty, they give their very lives for Him;
ceaselessly telling of His charming virtues, lost to themselves, they speak of nothing else.
Some come and go, but they have no strength to stay at home;
all surround on all four sides the Lord of Narahari, His servant."[^12-4]

*And another*

**12.4.** "Who can tell of the incomparable love of the men and women of the town of Nadiya?
Gaurahari is their life and their all; without Him they have not the least composure.
The old men, most eager, come taking staffs in their hands;
wishing [Him] well, their hearts overflowing, on seeing [Him] they become like young men.
The women full of parental love perform various auspicious rites with great tenderness;
having gazed [at Him] to their eyes' content, walking around [Him] many times, they give abundant blessings.
Never do they think that He is the Supreme Lord; they remember nothing else — incomparable [is their] way.
Constantly they speak to one another of the childhood plays of Narahari's Lord."[^12-5]

*And another — raga vibhasha*

**12.5.** "Many lovely, wondrous deeds were hidden in this age;
He reveals them, ever compassionate — He keeps nothing secret.
The devotees constantly sing His glory — who, hearing it, would not be filled with anuraga?
Knowing Him as their own chosen Deity, all the virtuous cling to His feet.
Whatever feeling has risen in anyone's heart, they never give it up;
they guard it as a miser guards his wealth, and cannot forget it even for a moment.
In the Lord of Narahari, His servant, this avatara has become full of rasa;
now this intoxicating form, entering the heart, has taken away everything."[^12-6]

*And another — raga vibhasha*

**12.6.** "The complexion of Gaurasundara, which steals people's minds — I have grasped [its secret]:
in it is the union of Gauri and Shyama; that is why it has become so intoxicating.
In the world there are many various comparisons — none of them ever fits it.
It is ever new and new — who can describe [it]? There is no such poet.
Trying to convey even a drop of [its] lovely sweetness, I rejoice at heart,
but all the many comparisons, on beholding it, are helpless — they show only a little.
He whose fortune is great, great, is immersed in this beauty;
Narahari, His servant, filling his eyes again and again, has drunk in the beauty of such a Lord."[^12-7]

*And another — raga lalita*

**12.7.** "Amid His associates the youthful Gaura shines in incomparable attire;
His royal splendour surpasses crushed kunkuma, gold and heaps of champakas.
His beautiful curling hair gleams — glossy, with the sheen of kajal;
the orb of His face is spotless and radiant; His teeth are a row of pearls.
Lotus eyes, broad brows, ears, nose, soft cheeks;
a lion's neck, fresh grace, incomparable, most lovely arms;
broad chest, slender waist, knees — [all] steal [the hearts of] the sages;
by the rays of the toenails of the Lord of Narahari, His servant, the world is illumined."[^12-8]

*Ramanaka-chhandah*

**12.8.** Speaking thus, the hosts of gods
are greatly intoxicated — how much rises in their hearts!
Gracefully moving their arms, they dance
and sing a sweet garland of names:[^12-9]

*Someone says — raga karnata*

**12.9.** "Glory [to You], O Vishvambhara, Krishna, son of Shachi, golden of form, full of compassion!
O Madhava, Crusher of Mura, Giver of honour, Increaser of joy, Abode of all rasas!
O Plunderer of the bodices on the breasts of the hosts of gopis, whose deeds are lovely, Delighter of the people!
O You whose feet are caressed by Kamala's hands, tender as new shoots, Destroyer of the mighty threefold miseries!
O Keshava, Slayer of Kamsa, Fulfiller of desires, King of the brahmanas, Remover of fear!
O You who are eager for sports and plays on the bank of Jahnu's daughter, revered by the universe, of auspicious form!
O Holder of the mountain, praised by Dharanidhara, new Dancer, rare for the three worlds!
O You who are skilled in giving the treasure — the jewel of prema, Ocean of virtues, Beloved of Narahari!"[^12-10]

*And another — raga deshapala*

**12.10.** "Glory, glory [to You], O Shri Jagadisha, son of Shachi, beautiful youthful Gaura, ocean of rasa,
O Keshava, [word unclear], kajal for the eyes of the daughters of the cowherds of Gokula!
Remover of the burden of the age of Kali, incomparable Preacher of prema, lovely of body,
most Complete One, Moon of the line of the twice-born, Dispeller of the darkness of a mass of afflictions!
Skilled in unprecedented dance, Ocean of virtues, Nagara, Increaser of the joy of the rasikas, Madhava,
devoted to playful quarrels, Fulfiller of desires, Beloved, You who put [the beauty of] Kama to shame!
Ornament of the bank of Jahnu's daughter, Krishna, foremost in water-sports in the daughter of Mount Kalinda,
lovely One, Lord of Narahari, Delighter of the people of the world!"[^12-11]

*Raga vibhasha*

**12.11.** The incomparable, sweetest name "Gaura", surpassing [even] the fullness of the intoxication of nectar,
the hosts of gods in the sky repeat again and again; water streams from their eyes.
Descending to the earth, stirred, they at once took on human form;
no one can recognize them — they enter the precincts of [His] abode.
Staying near the skilled associates, at the sight of the Lord they are full of sweet delight;
offering body and mind, joining their palms, how many longings they cherish!
Counting [this] their good fortune, they spread out in obeisance at the feet of the Lord and [His] associates.
The servant Narahari gives himself up to this anuraga [and exclaims]: "I offer myself, I offer myself!"[^12-12]

*Thus [ends] the twelfth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Anuraga and So On of the Hosts of Gods", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^12-13]

[^12-1]: The name of the kirana follows its colophon: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্রবর্ণনে সুরগণানুরাগাদি-বর্ণনং নাম দ্বাদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১২॥" — "the twelfth kirana, called 'The Description of the Anuraga and So On of the Hosts of Gods', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The reading in the 1947 edition is *sura-gaṇa-anurāga-ādi*, "the hosts of gods". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the anuraga and so on of the hosts of gods" *Anuraga* is deep, ever-increasing loving attachment. Unlike kiranas 10–11, where the heavenly women speak, here the speakers are gods (*sura-gaṇa*, *deba-bṛnda*; the headings "কোঽপ্যাহ", "কোঽপি চ" are masculine). Kirana 12 comprises stanzas 12.1–12.11; 12.11 and the colophon are in the next portion. The summary line is based on 12.1–12.10 and a quick look at 12.11.

[^12-2]: Raga bhairava is a morning raga (cf. 2.1).. Lines 3–4 are the refrain (*dhru*). The author's words. "Shines" — *bhāti*. "Perfectly pure, wondrously lovely" — *parama śuci surucirakārī*. "Gaurachanda" — *gauracanda*. "The Remover of others' sorrow" — *paraduḥkha-hārī*; it may also refer to the associates. "With their retinues" — *gaṇasaha*. "On the path of the sky" — *gagana-pantha-gata*. "Motionless" — *gati-birahita*. "Hundreds of millions of Madanas" — *madanārbuda*: *arbuda* is a hundred million; Madana is Kamadeva, the type of perfect beauty (cf. 2.4). "Their thoughts tremble" — *saṅkalpa(?) cañcala*: the first word is indistinct — reading unclear. "They deem [their lives] fulfilled" — *mānata saphala*. "In lovely rows" — *supā̃ti*. "The pair of lotuses — the hands and feet" — *kara-caraṇa-kañja-yuga*. In print the number "১" lacks the closing "॥". Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pahũ*).

[^12-3]: Heading — "কেশরী ছন্দঃ".. The author's words. "Glitter" — *jhalamalāt*. "Throng upon throng" — *jhuṇḍa jhuṇḍa*. "In circles" — *maṇḍalī*. "Sway, brimming with rasa" — *rasa ṭalamalāt*. "As from the clouds of the month of Shravana" — *ghana śāona sama*: Shravana is a month of the rainy season. "Pour unceasingly" — *jharata jāta*; the understanding is uncertain.

[^12-4]: Heading — "কোঽপ্যাহ—রাগ ললিত—" ("someone says"; raga lalita is a morning raga, cf. 11.1).. One of the gods speaks. "Look" — *paśya* (Sanskrit). "In Mishra's house" — *miśra-bhabana*: the house of Jagannatha Mishra, Gaura's father. "In the lovely morning hour" — *prāta samaya suhata*: the word *suhata* (or *suhṛta*) — reading unclear; understood as *suhāta*, "pleasant". "Cries of 'Glory, glory!' resound" — *hota jayajayakāra*. "Composure" — *dhīra*. "In dense thrills of rapture" — *pulakakula-yuta*. "Shed streams" — *nijharai*. "They give their very lives for Him" — *niramañchi nija nija prāṇa*: from *nirmañchana* (cf. *nichani*), the rite of waving something precious around a beloved person to ward off misfortune; an expression of utmost love. "Lost to themselves" — *mana bhuli*. "They speak of nothing else" — *bhaṇata na āna*. "No strength" — *śakati na hoi*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari, His servant" (*dāsa narahari pahuka*).

[^12-5]: Heading — "কোঽপি চ—" ("and another"), with no raga name.. The stanza number is missing in print: the stanza ends "…বালচরিত ॥", and the next one, bears the number "॥৫॥"; the number 12.4 is restored by sequence. "Love" — *neha* (*sneha*). "Their life and their all" — *prāṇa-sarabasa*. "Not the least composure" — *tanaka dharai nā theha*. "Most eager" — *ati hi ātura*. "Staffs" — *laguṛa*. "Come" — *gahi āna*; the understanding is uncertain. "Their hearts overflowing" — *umagi hiya*. "The women full of parental love" — *bātsalyabatī*: women who love Gaura as a son (vatsalya). "Perform auspicious rites" — *maṅgala nītarata*; the understanding is uncertain. "With great tenderness" — *ati heta*. "Having gazed to their eyes' content" — *neta-diṭhi bhari*. "Walking around [Him] many times" — *bipula bana(?) bhrami*: the second word — reading unclear; the understanding is uncertain. "That He is the Supreme Lord" — *parameśa-budhi*: their love is free from awareness of His majesty (*aiśvarya*), like that of the residents of Vraja. "They remember nothing else" — *śudhi na rahu*. "Childhood plays" — *bāla-carita*. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pahũka*).

[^12-6]: Heading — "কোঽপি চ (বিভাষ)".. "In this age" — *iha yuga māhi*: in the age of Kali. "Reveals" — *bekata karu*. "Would not be filled with anuraga" — *nahu anurāgī*. "Chosen Deity" — *iṣṭa*. "The virtuous" — *śiṣṭa-samūha*. "Cling to His feet" — *uha paga lāgi*. "Feeling" — *bhāba*. "As a miser guards his wealth" — *kṛpaṇa-dhana-sama*. "Even for a moment" — *na thori*. "This avatara has become full of rasa" — *aru aba-tāra rasamaya bheli*: printed with a hyphen — *aba-tāra*, "now His [form]"; probably a play on *abatāra*, "avatara"; the understanding is uncertain. "Intoxicating form" — *mādaka mūrati*. "Has taken away everything" — *sarabasa neli*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari, His servant".

[^12-7]: Heading — "কোঽপি চ (বিভাষ)".. "Complexion" — *baraṇa*, bodily colour. "I have grasped" — *anubhaba kela*. "The union of Gauri and Shyama" — *gaurī śyāmara milana*: Gauri ("the golden one") is Radha, Shyama ("the dark one") is Krishna. This is the teaching of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas: Shri Krishna Chaitanya is Radha and Krishna united in one form; His golden colour is Radha's, and He Himself is Krishna, who has taken on Her mood and radiance in order to taste Her love (CC, Adi 1.5; Adi 4). "Intoxicating" — *mādaka*. "Fits" — *ghaṭata*. "Trying to convey" — *biracane*. "Helpless" — *bikala*. "They show only a little" — *deta kachu daraśāya*. "Fortune" — *bhāga*. "Has drunk in" — *nela*, literally "took". Signature: "Narahari, His servant".

[^12-8]: Heading — "কোঽপি চ (ললিত রাগ)". The stanza begins and ends with "॥৭॥". A description of Gaura's form from hair to feet. "The youthful Gaura" — *gaura-kiśora*. "Attire" — *sāja*. "Crushed kunkuma" — *dalita-kuṅkuma*. "Royal splendour" — *ruci-rāja*. "Curling hair" — *cā̃cara cikura*. "With the sheen of kajal" — *kājara kā̃ti*: hair black as kajal. "The orb of His face" — *badana-maṇḍala*. "A row of pearls" — *motima-pā̃ti*. "A lion's neck" — *siṁha-grība*. "Arms" — *bhuja-daṇḍa*, literally "staff-like arms" (long and mighty). "Broad chest" — *bakṣa parisara*. "Slender waist, knees" — *khīṇa kaṭi yuga jānu*. "Steal [the hearts of] the sages" — *munigaṇa-cora*. "Is illumined" — *ujora*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari, His servant".

[^12-9]: Heading — "রমণক ছন্দঃ".. The stanza consists of short half-lines with a caesura (cf. 11.16). The author's words. "Greatly intoxicated" — *atimatta*. "Rises" — *upajata*. "Gracefully moving their arms" — *bhuja bhaṅgi-sañe*; "gracefully" — *bhalī*. "Garland of names" — *nāmābalī*: the names follow in 12.9–12.10.

[^12-10]: Heading — "কোঽপ্যাহ (রাগ কর্ণাট)".. The gods glorify Gaura with names of Krishna; each line ends with the address *he*. "Golden of form" — *gaurākṛti*. "Crusher of Mura" — *muramardana*. "Giver of honour" — *mānada*. "Abode of all rasas" — *nikhila rasālaya*. "Plunderer of the bodices on the breasts of the hosts of gopis" — *gopīkula-kuca-kañcu-biluṇṭhaka*; *kañcu(ka)* is a bodice. "Kamala" — Lakshmi. "Tender as new shoots" — *kiśalaya*. "Threefold miseries" — *tāpa-traya*. "King of the brahmanas" — *bhūsura-bhūpa*. "Jahnu's daughter" — *jahnu-sutā*, the Ganga: on her bank Gaura plays in Navadvipa. "Holder of the mountain" — *bhūdhara-dhara*: of Govardhana. "Praised by Dharanidhara" — *dharaṇīdhara-nuta*: by Ananta-Shesha, who holds up the earth (possibly Balarama — Nityananda). "New Dancer" — *naba-nartana*. "Beloved of Narahari" — *narahari-ballabha*. Here the gods glorify Gaura with names of Krishna of Vraja — including "Plunderer of the bodices on the breasts of the hosts of gopis" (line 3), and in 12.10 "Nagara" and "devoted to playful quarrels". For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas Gaura is Krishna Himself (CC, Adi 1–2), and these names speak of His identity with Krishna of Vraja; but Shrila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, following the *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not even look at them), rejected gaura-nagari-vada — the worship of Gaura as the enjoying lover (*nāgara*) of the women of Nadiya: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer (cf. 12.6 and note 7; cf. the main note of kirana 11 — note 2). Later in this kirana — references to this note.

[^12-11]: Heading — "কোঽপি চ (দেশপাল)". The stanza begins and ends at the top of with "॥১০॥"; translated in full (§ 3a); the next portion begins with 12.11 (, "(বিভাষ)", "গৌর সুমধুর নাম নিরুপম অমিয়-মদভরহারি…"). "Jagadisha" — *jagadīśa*, "Lord of the world". "[Word unclear]" — *keśabalambhata*(?): the print is indistinct; possibly *keśa-abalambita* ("with flowing hair") — reading unclear. "Kajal for the eyes of the daughters of the cowherds of Gokula" — *gokula-gopasutā-nayanāñjana*. "Most Complete One" — *pūrṇatama*. "Moon of the line of the twice-born" — *dvija-baṁśa-śaśadhara*: Gaura was born in a brahmana family. "Nagara" — *nāgara*; "devoted to playful quarrels" — *keli-kalaha-rata*; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 10. "Beloved" — *kānta*. "You who put [the beauty of] Kama to shame" — *kāma-kṛta(?)-gañjana*: the print is smudged; possibly *kāma-koṭi-gañjana* ("putting millions of Kamas to shame") — reading unclear. "The daughter of Mount Kalinda" — *kalinda-dharādhara-nandinī*: the Yamuna. "Foremost in water-sports" — *bāri-bihāra-dhurandhara*. "Lovely One" — *bandhura*. Signature: "Lord of Narahari" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^12-12]: Heading — "(বিভাষ)", without "কোঽপি চ": the author's words.. "Surpassing [even] the fullness of the intoxication of nectar" — *amiya-mada-bhara-hāri*; it can also be understood as "taking from nectar the fullness of [its] intoxicating power". "Streams" — *ḍharakata*. "Stirred" — *tarala*. "Human form" — *mānaba-beśa*: the gods, like the heavenly beings before them (cf. 12.9–12.10 and note 10), see in Gaura Krishna Himself and, in order to be near Him, descend to earth unrecognized. "Enter the precincts of [His] abode" — *parisara bhubane karu prabeśa*: *bhubana* here probably in the sense of *bhabana* ("house" — Shachi's house, cf. 12.3); it can also be understood as "enter the surroundings of the world [of Nadiya]". "Skilled" — *sughara*. "Sweet delight" — *sarasa hulāsa*. "Offering" — *sõpi*. "How many longings they cherish" — *karata kata abhilāṣa*. "At the feet of the Lord and [His] associates" — *pahũ-gaṇa caraṇe*: literally "at the feet of the Lord [with His] host". "Spread out in obeisance" — *praṇati bithāri*. "Gives himself up" — *nichani*; "I offer myself, I offer myself!" — *bali balihāri*, an exclamation of devoted love. Signature: "the servant Narahari".

[^12-13]: The colophon was:, immediately after 12.11 — "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্রবর্ণনে সুরগণানুরাগাদি-বর্ণনং নাম দ্বাদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১২॥".


## Kirana 13. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Longings and So On of the Gandharvas and Kinnaras[^13-1]

*Morning: the gandharvas and kinnaras, rejoicing, behold Gaura in Shachi's house and confide their longings to one another — to fall at His feet on the bank of the Ganga, to hear His kirtana, to live in Nadiya, to drink His beauty and to serve His feet; then, standing in circles in the sky, they sing of His plays in Nadiya, and their tears humble the pride of the clouds*

*Again, in the morning period — the longings and so on of the gandharvas and kinnaras.*

*Raga lalita*

**13.1.** The house of Shri Shachi-devi, rich in adornments — there is great merriment there: night has turned to dawn.
Amid the host of skilled, excellent associates the Lord shines, delighting the young women of the world.
His tender, spotless body, adorned with beautiful curves — charming, full of rasa, lovely, filled with radiance;
[surpassing] garlands of lightning, heaps of gold and champakas, He crushes the pride of Kandarpa.
His eyes are broad lotuses; His forehead a net of beauty; His lips beautifully red, [like] the rising sun;
His teeth [like] kunda flowers; from the fair moon of His face nectar-rasa gently flows — His lovely smile.
His hair, [darker] than water-laden clouds, puts crushed kajal to shame; glossy fresh curls are [His] ornament.
His glittering attire — not a trace of an equal to it; the world is enchanted, says Ghanashyama.[^13-2]

*Chanchala-chhandah (matra-vritta)*

**13.2.** Today the hosts of gandharvas and kinnaras are in great joy;
their bodies are filled with the thrill of rapture, their wondrous course of prema cannot be hidden.
Beholding their own Lord Gauranga, the best of Shachi's sons,
again and again they speak nectarean words to one another.[^13-3]

*Someone says — raga vibhasha*

**13.3.** "Hear my longing: when that Gaura, the Moon of Gokula,
goes to the bank of the Suradhuni, surrounded by His companions, with a very slow gait,
then, beholding from afar the sweet charm of the Lord's face,
I will come nearer, not blinking, holding on to what is left of my composure.
I will fall, rolling on the ground, at [His] feet, and bathe [His] feet with the tears of my eyes.
Saying, 'Rise!' and taking [me] by both arms, that Lord will comfort me.
Hearing, 'Worship Nanda-kishora', I will speak again, clinging to [His] feet:
'You are He — the life of Narahari, manifest in this [age of] Kali by [our] good fortune.'"[^13-4]

*And another — raga lalita*

**13.4.** "Gaura is the Lord of Gokula, the king of nagaras, the king of brahmanas;
He puts to shame the lovely gait of an elephant; His attire enchants [even] Manoja.
His face gladdens the minds of the people of the world and robs the autumn moon of its pride;
His teeth [like] kunda flowers, a beautiful smile, speech full of rasa, overflowing with nectar.
On the bank of Jahnu's daughter He will shine amid His associates,
He will perform the lovely play of kirtana — hearing [it], I will not keep my composure.
He will seek out the fallen; then, as soon as the Lord of Ghanashyama looks at me,
I will run, clasp [His feet] and sprinkle [them] with the tears of my eyes. — I have told you my longing."[^13-5]

*And another — raga bhairava*

**13.5.** "You ask again and again — so I will tell [you] my longing:
may my dwelling be in this Nadiya, revered by Shesha, Shiva and Vidhi.
The incomparable beauty of Gaurasundara is a stream of nectarean rasa:
I will drink it, filling the cups of my eyes again and again, carefully plunging [into it].
I will sing His most charming deeds — my chest will swell with joy;
I will forget eating, sleeping — everything; there will be no awareness of day or night.
'Keep [me] near, take away my sorrow,' I will say again and again in words of entreaty;
then the servant Narahari will serve the Lord's two feet."[^13-6]

*Raga todi*

**13.6.** Thus the gandharvas and kinnaras together voice many longings;
singing Gaura's qualities, they were carried away in supreme rapture.
In the sphere of the sky, standing in circles, they sing the alap of sweet ragas
and play many kinds of instruments — the sound, like nectar, pervades the world.
When in sweet voices they proclaim [Gaura's] wondrous plays in Nadiya,
all the wise were enchanted: then no one kept even a trace of composure.
Tears flow ceaselessly from [their] eyes, humbling the pride of the host of clouds.
The servant Narahari gives up his body — he cannot describe [their] anuraga.[^13-7]

*Thus [ends] the thirteenth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Longings and So On of the Gandharvas and Kinnaras", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^13-8]

[^13-1]: The name of the kirana follows its colophon: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে গন্ধর্বকিন্নরাভিলাষাদিবর্ণনং নাম ত্রয়োদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১৩॥" — "the thirteenth kirana, called 'The Description of the Longings (*abhilāṣa*) and So On of the Gandharvas and Kinnaras', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the longings and so on of the gandharvas and kinnaras" Gandharvas are heavenly singers and musicians, kinnaras heavenly musicians; here the speakers are men (the headings "কোঽপি আহ", "কোপি চ" are masculine; kirana 14 is devoted to the women of these races, the gandharvis and kinnaris). Kirana 13 comprises stanzas 13.1–13.6.

[^13-2]: Raga lalita is a morning raga (cf. 11.1).. The author's words: a description of Gaura's form. "Rich in adornments" — *bhuri-bhūṣaṇa*. "Great merriment" — *bhuri kutuka*. "Delighting the young women of the world" — *jagata yubati hulasāta*. Here and in 13.4 ("the king of nagaras" — *nāgara-bhūpa*) Gaura is described in words used of Krishna of Vraja. For the Gaudiya Vaishnavas Gaura is Krishna Himself; but Shrila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, following the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not even look at them), rejected gaura-nagari-vada — the worship of Gaura as the enjoying lover (*nāgara*) of women: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer (cf. the main notes of kirana 11 — note 2, and of kirana 12 — note 10). The gandharvas and kinnaras themselves in this kirana long only to fall at Gaura's feet, to hear His kirtana and to serve Him. "Adorned with beautiful curves" — *su-balani-balita*: the meaning is unclear; possibly "endowed with beautiful folds" (*bali*) or "with a beautiful gait"; the understanding is uncertain. "Filled with radiance" — *rucipūra*. "Garlands of lightning, heaps of gold and champakas" — *dāminī-dāma-bharma-bhara-campaka*; the next word is *-kara* (the print is heavy; possibly "making", i.e. "[eclipsing]") — the reading is unclear. "Crushes the pride of Kandarpa" — *kandarpa-darpaka karu cūra*: literally "crushes Kandarpa [and his] pride"; Kandarpa is Kama. "His forehead a net of beauty" — *bhāla chabi-jāla*. "The rising sun" — *bālarabi*. "Kunda flowers" — white jasmine. "Puts crushed kajal to shame" — *dalitāñjana-gañji*. "Ornament" — *lalāma*. "Not a trace of an equal" — *leśa naha samatula*. "The world is enchanted" — *bhulala bhubana*: the word *bhulala* is smudged — the reading is unclear. Signature: "Ghanashyama" — Narahari's other name.

[^13-3]: Heading — "মাত্রাবৃত্তে চঞ্চলা ছন্দঃ".. The author's words. The stanza consists of two long lines of two half-lines each (four lines in the translation). "Their wondrous course of prema cannot be hidden" — *agupana(?) su-prema-gati*: the first word is read with uncertainty (*agupana*, "unconcealed"; possibly *ahupana*) — the reading is unclear. "Their own Lord" — *nija-nātha*. "The best of Shachi's sons" — *śacī-kumara-bara*. "To one another" — *parasapara*.

[^13-4]: Heading — "কোঽপি আহ—(বিভাষ)" ("someone says").. One of the gandharvas (or kinnaras) speaks. "The Moon of Gokula" — *gokula-canda*, a name of Krishna. "Suradhuni" — "the heavenly river", the Ganga. "With a very slow gait" — *gati ati manda*. "Not blinking" — *nibāri diṭhi-pala*, literally "holding back the eyelids". "Holding on to what is left of my composure" — *alapa dhairaya roki*. "Rolling on the ground" — *dharaṇi loṭāi*. "With the tears of my eyes" — *diṭhi-lore*. "Will comfort me" — *samujhāba*, literally "will admonish". "Worship Nanda-kishora" — *bhajaha nanda-kiśora*: Nanda-kishora is the youthful son of Nanda, Krishna. Gaura, playing the role of a devotee, does not acknowledge Himself as God and tells him to worship Krishna; the one who longs answers that He is Krishna Himself, appeared in the age of Kali (cf. CC, Adi 3). "By [our] good fortune" — *bhāgi*. Signature: "the life of Narahari" (*narahari-ka jībana*).

[^13-5]: Heading — "কোপি চ—(ললিত)" ("and another").. "The Lord of Gokula" — *gokula-nāha*. "The king of nagaras" — *nāgara-bhūpa*: for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. "The king of brahmanas" — *bhūsura-rāja* (cf. 12.9). "Manoja" — Kama. "Robs the autumn moon of its pride" — *śarada-bidhu-mada-hāri*. "Overflowing with nectar" — *amiya ubhāri*. "Jahnu's daughter" — *jahnu-nandinī*, the Ganga. "The play of kirtana" — *kīrtana-keli*. "I will not keep my composure" — *rahaba na theha*. "He will seek out the fallen; then, as soon as…" — *patite khõjaba abahi-taba(?)*: the middle of the line is smudged; possibly *abahita* ("attentively") — the reading is unclear. "The Lord of Ghanashyama" — *ghanaśyāma-pahũ*: the author's signature. "Clasp [His feet]" — *dhari*, literally "seize"; the object is not expressed in the text (cf. 13.3).

[^13-6]: Heading — "কোপি চ—(ভৈরব)". The stanza begins and ends "॥৫॥". "Again and again" — *puna puna beri*. "Revered by Shesha, Shiva and Vidhi" — *śeṣa-śiba-bidhi-bandya(?)*: the first word of the line is badly smudged — the reading is unclear; Vidhi is Brahma. "The cups of my eyes" — *locana-casaka*. "Carefully plunging" — *yatane kari abagāha*. "My chest will swell" — *umaṛaba chāti*. "No awareness of day or night" — *smṛti nahaba iha dina rāti*. "'Keep [me] near, take away my sorrow'" — *rākhi niyare nebāri dukha*: it can also be understood as "[when He] keeps [me] near, taking away my sorrow, I will…" — the understanding is uncertain. "Words of entreaty" — *anunati bhāṣa*. "The servant Narahari will serve" — *karaba narahari dāsa*: the author's signature merges with the speech of the one who longs.

[^13-7]: Heading — "(তোড়ী)", without "কোঽপি চ": the author's words.. Raga todi is a morning raga. "Together" — *meli*. "Were carried away in supreme rapture" — *parama uddhata bheli*, literally "became utterly unrestrained". "Standing in circles" — *maṇḍalī raci*. "Sing the alap" — *ālāpi*: the introductory unfolding of a raga. "Proclaim" — *ucarata*. "[Gaura's] wondrous plays in Nadiya" — *nadīyā-bihāra-biśeṣa*. "All the wise" — *sudhī saba*. "Humbling the pride of the host of clouds" — *bārida-bṛnda-garaba nebāri*: their tears are more than the rain from the clouds. "Gives up his body" — *nichai tanu*. Signature: "the servant Narahari".

[^13-8]: The colophon was:, immediately after 13.6 — "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্র-বর্ণনে গন্ধর্বকিন্নরাভিলাষাদিবর্ণনং নাম ত্রয়োদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১৩॥".


## Kirana 14. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Gandharvis and Kinnaris Revealing Their Heart's Wishes and So On[^14-1]

*Morning: the gandharvis and kinnaris, unseen, keep their eyes on Gaura's face and are filled with prema; one after another they confide their heart's wishes to their friends — to watch from afar as Gora walks along the road of Nadiya, to be born a young wife in Nadiya and go with the nagaris of Nadiya to Vishnupriya, to become Vishnupriya's maidservant and serve Gaura and Her*

*Again, in the morning period — the gandharvis and kinnaris revealing their heart's wishes, and so on.*

*Raga bhairava*

**14.1.** What can I say! Today the blessed night has turned to dawn.
The hosts of gandharvis are absorbed [in bliss], full of joy; the throng of kinnaris shines together [with them]. *(Refrain)*
Unseen, they gaze at Gaurachandra's face without blinking — endlessly thirsting.
Their garments slip, they are overpowered by rasa; their loosened braids gleam; not a trace of composure remains.
Glittering, covered with abundant thrills of rapture, their bodies, utterly unstrung, tremble ceaselessly.
However hard they try, the secret is not kept: the feeling of the heart becomes manifest.
Deeming their lot wholly fulfilled, they give up the jewel of their fresh youth at the Lord's feet.
A pure, mighty, incomparable course of prema! Seeing [it], Narahari, overcome with joy, sings.[^14-2]

*Rasakala-chhandah (matra-vritta)*

**14.2.** The gandharvis are wholly absorbed in joy,
they shine together with the throng of young kinnaris.
Who can describe their incomparable attachment to Gaura?
Absorbed in prema, they say something to one another.[^14-3]

*One [of them] says — raga lalita*

**14.3.** "Listen, listen, O playful friend!
What arises in my heart I do not tell out of shame — [but,] becoming shameless, I will tell it.
This Gora, the delight of Nadiya,
a mine of the jewel of love, a jewel of virtues, the thief of women's lives —
throwing His curly hair back over His shoulders,
with how many graceful movements He will walk along the road, captivating the young women!
Seeing such a radiance of beauty,
throngs of young women, losing their composure, will run like madwomen.
Gora, looking at them all,
smiling ever so gently, raining nectar, will strike with the arrows of His eyes.
And I, overwhelmed by Madana,
will watch this play of the Lord of Narahari's life, staying apart."[^14-4]

*And another — raga todi*

**14.4.** "Friend, what arises in my heart,
finding [us] alone, casting off shame, I will tell you.
This Gora is the moon of Nadiya;
with what yearning in their hearts the wives of [good] families will run to see Him!
I, staying with them all,
laughing and laughing, with so much fun, will tell amusing stories full of rasa.
Hearing that, the Lord of Narahari,
His head bowed, will say so much to me with the corner of His eyes!"[^14-5]

*And another — raga vibhasha*

**14.5.** "Friend! Hear what is in my heart.
I will become a young wife in the town of Nadiya and live with those playful [women].
The young nagaris of Nadiya, joyfully,
will go to Vishnupriya, delighted, taking me with them.
Together with them I will go to Shachi's house;
seeing Gora with Vishnupriya, I will be lost in delight full of rasa.
The Lord of Narahari's life,
looking at me, smiling ever so gently, will shatter [my] dharma and composure."[^14-6]

*And another — raga ramakeli*

**14.6.** "Listen, I will tell you what is in my heart.
Devi Vishnupriya, making me her maidservant, will keep me near.
She, taking the hand of the Lord of [her] life,
will sit with such graceful movements — I will watch it, filling my eyes.
Receiving Vishnupriya's permission,
I will joyfully place a garland of malati around Gorachanda's neck.
Gorachanda, placing that malati garland
around Vishnupriya's neck, will say so much to me on the pretext of a jest!
I, smiling the sweetest smile,
having prepared tambula, will lift [it] to the full-moon faces of the Two.
I will fan [Them] with a lovely chamara,
[and] the Beloved — the life of Narahari — will recline on the couch with His beloved."[^14-7]

*And another — raga asavari*

**14.7.** "Friend! Listen to what is in my heart.
I will seat Gauranga with Lakshmi — Vishnupriya — where it is secluded.
They, sitting on a throne of jewels,
will sprinkle each other with heaps of nectar in all kinds of playful words.
Seeing that beauty, filling my eyes,
I will stand before [Them] with my own [friends], striking so many graceful poses!
Dense thrills of ecstasy will pervade [my] body;
joyfully I will play the mridanga, mandira, vina, pinaka and other instruments.
I will dance in many ways,
in many ragas I will sing of His qualities and deeds — [They] will listen, intoxicated with bliss.
The beloved of Narahari's Lord, [dear to Him as] life,
keeping me always near herself, will soothe my heart."[^14-8]

*Raga sindhura*

**14.8.** The joyful gandharvis and the hosts of kinnaris, gathered together,
playfully tell one another their wishes, full of rasa.
In the midst of the vault of the sky they ceaselessly sing of Gaura's qualities;
[the song] flows as if with fresh nectar, enters the cups of the ears and steals away the mind.
Softly, softly dancing, sounding "dhidhikat", they strike countless poses;
descending to the earth, they regain their composure and speak special words.
One says: "This Gaura, the Lord of Narahari, is the Lord of Gokula.
He will not be apart [from us]: day and night He will sport in the temple of [our] heart!"[^14-9]

*Thus [ends] the fourteenth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Gandharvis and Kinnaris Revealing Their Heart's Wishes and So On", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^14-10]

[^14-1]: The name of the kirana follows its colophon: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্রবর্ণনে গন্ধর্বিণী-কিন্নরীণাং মনোরথ-প্রকাশাদি-বর্ণনং নাম চতুর্দশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১৪॥" — "the fourteenth kirana, called 'The Description of the Gandharvis and Kinnaris Revealing Their Heart's Wishes (*manoratha-prakāśa*) and So On', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the gandharvis and kinnaris revealing their heart's wishes, and so on" The gandharvis and kinnaris are the women of the heavenly singers and musicians (cf. kirana 13). Kirana 14 comprises stanzas 14.1–14.8; 14.7–14.8 and the colophon are in the next portion. 14.8 — singing in the sky).

[^14-2]: Raga bhairava is a morning raga (cf. 12.1). The stanza begins and ends "॥১॥". Lines 1–2 are the refrain (*dhru*; the mark "॥ধ্রু॥" stands after line 2). The author's words. "Blessed" — *subhaga*. "Shines together [with them]" — *birājata jora* (the first letter of the verb in print resembles *n*: *nirājata*). "Unseen" — *alakhita*. "Endlessly thirsting" — *tṛṣita aśeṣa*. "Their garments slip" — *khasata basana*. "Overpowered by rasa" — *rasa-bibaśa*. "Loosened braids" — *ati śithila kabarī*. "Utterly unstrung" — *sugalita*. "The secret is not kept" — *na rahata gupata rīti*. "Give up the jewel of their fresh youth" — *naba yaubana-ratana nichai pahũ pāya*: *nichai* — "offer up, sacrifice". The frank passages (lines 4, 7) are translated exactly, without softening. Here a new kind of mood begins: heavenly women — the gandharvis and kinnaris — are filled with prema for Gaura, and further on (14.3–14.6) they dream of seeing Him as the nagara before whom the women of Nadiya lose their composure, and of serving Him and Vishnupriya. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Shrila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati and the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not even look at them), reject gaura-nagari-vada — the worship of Gaura as the enjoying lover (*nāgara*) and of oneself as His nagari: Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not an enjoyer. Even in these dreams Gaura Himself, on meeting women, bows His head (14.4; cf. 11.9). Cf. the main notes of kirana 11 (note 2) and kirana 12 (note 10). Further in this kirana there are references to this note. Signature: "Narahari".

[^14-3]: Heading — "(মাত্রাবৃত্তে রসকলা)".. The author's words. The stanza consists of two lines of two half-lines each (four lines in the translation). "Wholly absorbed in joy" — *parama magana raṅge*. "Young kinnaris" — *naba kinnarī-nikara*. "Attachment" — *laganā*, literally "clinging [of the heart]". "Absorbed in prema" — *prema-maganā*.

[^14-4]: Heading — "কাচিদাহ—(ললিত)" ("one [of them] says").. One of the gandharvis (or kinnaris) speaks; the stanza is in Bengali (not Brajabuli), with short odd lines. "Playful" — *raṅginī*. "Becoming shameless" — *nilaji haiyā*. "The delight of Nadiya" — *nadiyā-binoda*. "A mine of the jewel of love" — *pirīti ratana khani*. "The thief of women's lives" — *ramaṇī-parāṇa-corā*. "Captivating the young women" — *majāte yubati-kula*, literally "absorbing / undoing the young women". "Like madwomen" — *bāurira pārā*. "Will strike with the arrows of His eyes" — *hāniba nayāna-bāṇe*. "Overwhelmed by Madana" — *madane bibhora*: Madana is Kama. "Play" — *raṅga*. "Staying apart" — *birale raiyā*. This is the speaker's dream of what might be; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari's life" (*narahari prāṇanātha*).

[^14-5]: Heading — "কাচিচ্চ—(তোড়ি)" ("and another"). The stanza begins and ends "॥৪॥". "Finding [us] alone" — *birala pāiyā*. "The moon of Nadiya" — *nadiyāra bidhu*. "Yearning" — *sādha*, literally "wish". "The wives of [good] families" — *kulera badhū*: married women of respectable houses. "Amusing stories full of rasa" — *rasa kautuka-kāhinī*. "His head bowed" — *kari abanata mātha*: cf. 11.9, where Gaura, meeting the women of Nadiya, lowers His head out of shyness. "With the corner of His eyes" — *ā̃khi-koṇe*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari-nātha*).

[^14-6]: Heading — "কাচিচ্চ—(বিভাষ)".. "A young wife" — *naba badhū*. "With those playful [women]" — *se raṅginī sane*. "The young nagaris of Nadiya" — *naba nadiyā-nāgarī*. Vishnupriya is Gaura's consort in Navadvipa, revered by the Gaudiya Vaishnavas. "I will be lost in delight full of rasa" — *rasa-kautuke bibhora habo*. "The Lord of Narahari's life" — *naraharira parāṇapati*. "Will shatter [my] dharma and composure" — *nāśibe dharama dhṛti*: translated exactly. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2.

[^14-7]: Heading — "কাচিচ্চ—(রামকেলি)". The stanza begins and ends at the top of "॥৬॥"; it is translated in full (§ 3a); the next portion begins with 14.7 (, "কাচিচ্চ—(আসাবরী)", "সই! শুনহ মনের কথা…"). "Devi Vishnupriya" — *debī biṣṇupriyā*. "Her maidservant" — *anucarī*. "Taking the hand of the Lord of [her] life" — *prāṇanātha-kare dhari*. "With such graceful movements" — *kata bhaṅgi-sañe*. "Filling my eyes" — *nayāna bhari*. "Receiving permission" — *anumati pāñā*. "Gorachanda" — *gorācā̃da*, "the moon Gora". "On the pretext of a jest" — *kautuka chale*. "Tambula" — *tāmbula*: betel prepared as an offering. "The full-moon faces of the Two" — *dũhu badana-pūrṇima-cānde*. "Chamara" — *cāmara*: a yak-tail fan. "The Beloved — the life of Narahari" — *narahari-prāṇa-pira* (*pira* — "beloved"). "With His beloved" — *piyā-saha*. "Will recline on the couch" — *pālaṅke gaṛābe gā*, literally "will lay [His] body on the couch". The speaker's wish is to serve Gaura and Vishnupriya as a maidservant; for the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, see note 2.

[^14-8]: The heading is "কাচিচ্চ—(আসাবরী)".; the four top lines of are the end of 14.6 (translated in the previous portion) and are skipped. "With Lakshmi — Vishnupriya" — *lakṣmī biṣṇupriyā-saha*: Vishnupriya is called Lakshmi, the goddess of fortune, consort of Vishnu; one could also understand "with Lakshmi[priya] and Vishnupriya" (Lakshmipriya was Gaura's first consort), but this kirana speaks only of Vishnupriya (14.5–14.6), and in line 11 she is alone; the first understanding is adopted. "Where it is secluded" — *birala yathā*. "On a throne of jewels" — *ratna-siṁhāsane*. "Will sprinkle with heaps of nectar" — *siñcibe amiyā rāśi*. "With my own [friends]" — *nija-gaṇa sane*. "Graceful poses" — *bhaṅgimā*. "Mandira" — *mandirā*: small hand cymbals. "Pinaka" — *pināka*: a stringed instrument (literally "bow"). "Intoxicated with bliss" — *ānande māti*. "The beloved of Narahari's Lord, [dear to Him as] life" — *narahari pahũ prāṇapriyā*: Vishnupriya; one could also understand "the Lord of Narahari [and His] beloved". "Always" — *anukhaṇa*. "Will soothe" — *juṛābe*, literally "will cool". The speaker's wish is to serve Gaura and Vishnupriya with song, music and dance; for the position of Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 2. Signature: "Narahari".

[^14-9]: The heading is "(সিন্ধুড়া)", without "কাচিচ্চ": the author's words (lines 1–7); in lines 7–8 one of the heavenly women speaks. The stanza begins and ends at the top of with "॥৮॥". "Joyful" — *moda-gata*. "Playfully" — *kautuka bheli*. "In the midst of the vault of the sky ceaselessly" — *gagana-maṇḍala-madhya-[sa]ghana*: the first letter of the second word is covered by a blot in print; *saghana* is adopted — the reading is unclear. "The cups of the ears" — *śruti-puṭe*. "Steals away the mind" — *harata giyāna*. "Softly, softly dancing" — *naṭata(?) lahu lahu*: the second letter of the first word is indistinct — the reading is unclear. "Sounding 'dhidhikat'" — *ughaṭi dhidhikaṭa*: rhythm syllables of the mridanga. "Descending to the earth" — *dharaṇi utarata*: cf. 12.11, where the gods descend to the earth. "Special words" — *bacana-biśeṣa*. "The Lord of Gokula" — *gokula-nāha*: Gaura is Krishna Himself. "Will not be apart [from us]" — *na haba bhina*. "Will sport" — *ki bilasaba*: the particle *ki* is taken as emphatic (an exclamation); it can also be understood as a wishful question: "will He sport…?" "In the temple of the heart" — *hṛdaya-mandira māha*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari-ka pahũ*).

[^14-10]: The colophon is: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্রবর্ণনে গন্ধর্বিণী-কিন্নরীণাং মনোরথ-প্রকাশাদি-বর্ণনং নাম চতুর্দশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১৪॥". Kirana 14 consists of stanzas 14.1–14.8. Next begins kirana 15 (subheading "পুনঃ প্রাতঃকালে নাগগণোল্লাস-প্রকাশাদিকং—").


## Kirana 15. The Deeds of the Morning: The Description of the Manifestation of the Joy of the Hosts of Nagas and So On[^15-1]

*Morning: the nagas, coming out unseen from the clefts of the earth, behold Gaura in Shachi's courtyard; one after another they tell each other of His play on the bank of the Ganga, of the beauty before which the people of Nadiya lose their composure, of His mercy to the lame, the blind and the mute, and of the kirtan with Vasu Ghosha, Mukunda and Madhava — and they long to live in Nadiya among His associates and serve Him*

*Again, in the morning period — the manifestation of the joy of the hosts of nagas and so on.*

*Raga bhairava*

**15.1.** A wondrous play — the night has turned to dawn.
In Shachi's courtyard, putting to shame a multitude of suns, shines Gaura's form — it cannot be described. *(Refrain)*
The lovely moon-like face, before which a multitude of autumn moons perform arati, ceaselessly gleams;
the devotees, like chakoras, filling their eyes again and again, drink the nectarean stream of the rasa of [His] lovely smile.
What could [even] Shesha describe? [His] hair is rare for the eyes: the beautiful curling locks are like a fearsome mass of dense clouds;
rejoicing, the whole world gazes intently [at them] like chatakas; and more than this — the telling has no end.
The nagas, the hosts of nagas, rejoice, reflecting in countless ways on their great good fortune;
unseen, [coming out] from the clefts of the earth, beyond [every] state, overflowing [with feeling], they behold the Lord of Narahari.[^15-2]

*Chitravalli-chhandah (matra-vritta)*

**15.2.** Today, at the end of the night, auspicious, fortunate, all the nagas,
each deeming his fortune fulfilled, greatly stirred in heart —
who could describe their conduct? — seeing the beautiful, accomplished Gaurasundara,
overpowered by the rasa of prema, again say something to one another.[^15-3]

*One says — raga suhai*

**15.3.** "Gaurahari sports with His dear associates on the bank of the Suradhuni.
Seeing [this], the heart rejoiced, the body was filled with unbroken thrills of ecstasy.
How sweet, how sweet is His sweetness, which takes away all of Madana's pride!
The moon of His face is the abode of joy; a soft, soft smile pours out nectar.
Taking someone's hand, He seems to fall — the curves of His body cannot be described;
regaining composure, Hari listens again and again to talk of the city of Vraja.
Overflowing with prema, looking [at them], day after day He removes the sorrows of the fallen;
counting the qualities of the Lord of servant Narahari, the hosts of immortals pine at heart."[^15-4]

*And another — raga kamoda*

**15.4.** "Hearing of Gaura's qualities, [I] rejoiced in all my body with thirst to see [Him].
On the broad road I saw Him walking with His accomplished associates.
Ah, what a wondrous form! Flashing, it subdues garlands of lightning;
from the fragrance of the lovely curling locks how many Kamas fall in a swoon!
On the spotless face, surpassing a multitude of moons — what a sweet smile!
The composure and dharma of the young women are shattered; softly, softly they say [to one another]:
'What spell has suddenly come upon [us]!' — the young women of the city of Nadiya,
surrounding the Lord of Narahari, do not leave [Him]; their eagerness cannot be told."[^15-5]

*And another — raga suhai*

**15.5.** "Beneath a young nipa tree — what play Gaurahari displays!
Surging forward, men and women come; incomparably beautiful is the Lord — [they are] on all four sides.
The lame, gaining feet, run back and forth in great eagerness;
those blind from birth cannot contain their great joy, seeing the moon of [His] face.
He from whose mouth no word came forth speaks His name;
fools compose many poems, the learned give up their pride.
The sorrow of the poor and the destitute goes far away; in the city of the gods resound cries of 'Victory, victory!'
The hope of servant Narahari is fulfilled: [He] has taken away Yama's authority."[^15-6]

*And another — raga gurjari*

**15.6.** "What shall I say? How sweet is the form of Gaura the nagara — it cannot be described!
With His companions, in great merriment, He begins water play in the Jahnavi.
There the noble brahmanas, each absorbed in the worship of his chosen Deity,
forgot it all; their bodies overpowered, they gaze intently toward the Lord.
The young women, intoxicated at heart, became chakoris to the moon of His face:
their garments slip — there is no [strength] to hold them, not a little composure can they keep.
All the fine young men of the city, seeing [His] beauty, bowed their heads
and lost their wits and their [accustomed] ways — the Lord of Narahari enchants the whole world."[^15-7]

*And another — raga malava*

**15.7.** "Today I saw Gaura — the best of dancers — lost in the rasa of dance.
The curves of [His] body take away all of Ananga's pride; His attire is radiant.
How sweetly He utters with His moon-like mouth the name 'Hari, Hari'!
Hearing [it], people's hearts lose their calm; again and again streams of tears flow from [their] eyes.
Gathering His associates — Vasu Ghosha, Mukunda, Madhava and others —
He performs kirtan; drinking that nectar, the world went mad.
The cymbals ring 'jhanu-jhanu', the murajas and mridangas thunder in endless beats;
the beautiful sound pierces the sky — what more can Ghanashyama say?"[^15-8]

*Raga kamoda*

**15.8.** Ceaselessly, in so many ways, the nagas tell of Gaura's beautiful deeds.
Tears flow from [their] eyes as from rain clouds; intoxicated, they hold on to that rasa.
They cherish so many wishes: "Will we have a dwelling here in Nadiya?
Will we carefully hold in the heart the pair of lotus feet that grant fearlessness?
Will we live together with the Lord's associates and serve [Him] abundantly?
Knowing [us] as His own, will He give [us] bhakti? Will all sorrows go far away?
Will we ceaselessly sing the qualities of the Lord and His associates?"
So much [do they think] in their hearts, the hosts of nagas; Ghanashyama offers himself [to them].[^15-9]

*Thus [ends] the fifteenth kirana of the Shrimad Gaura-charita-chintamani, called "The Description of the Manifestation of the Joy of the Hosts of Nagas and So On", in the description of the deeds of the morning period.*[^15-10]

[^15-1]: The title of the kirana follows its colophon: "ইতি শ্রীমদ্গৌরচরিত্র-চিন্তামণৌ প্রাতঃকালীয়-চরিত্রবর্ণনে নাগগণোল্লাসপ্রকাশাদি-বর্ণনং নাম পঞ্চদশঃ কিরণঃ ॥১৫॥" — "the fifteenth kirana, called 'The Description of the Manifestation of the Joy of the Hosts of Nagas and So On', in the description of the deeds of the morning period". The subheading "Again, in the morning period — the manifestation of the joy of the hosts of nagas and so on" The nagas are semi-divine serpent beings dwelling in the subterranean worlds (Patala); their lord is Shesha (Ananta). The speakers are nagas (the headings "কোঽপ্যাহ", "কোঽপি চ" are masculine). Kirana 15 consists of stanzas 15.1–15.8, entirely within this portion.

[^15-2]: Raga bhairava is a morning raga (cf. 12.1).. Lines 1–2 are the refrain (*dhru*; the mark "॥ধ্রু॥" stands after line 2). The author's words. "A wondrous play" — *aparūpa raṅga*. "Putting to shame a multitude of suns" — *dinamaṇi-bṛnda nindi*. "Form" — *chabi*. "Before which a multitude of autumn moons perform arati" — *śarada-niśākara-nikara-nirājita*: *nīrājita* — "honoured with the waving of lamps" (arati); one could also understand "surpassed". "The devotees, like chakoras" — *bhakata-cakora*: the chakora is a bird said to feed on moonlight. "Drink" — *paripibata*, literally "drink their fill". "What could [even] Shesha describe?" — *racaba ki śeṣa*: Shesha (Ananta), lord of the nagas, who ceaselessly glorifies the Lord with a thousand mouths — fitting in a kirana about nagas. "Rare for the eyes" — *dṛśi dulaha*. "A fearsome mass of dense clouds" — *ghaṭā ghana ghora*. "Like chatakas" — *janu cātaka*: the chataka bird drinks only rainwater and gazes intently at the clouds. "The telling has no end" — *kahai na ora*. "The nagas, the hosts of nagas" — *nāga-nāgagaṇa*: a doubling; possibly "the lords of the nagas and the hosts of nagas". "Reflecting on their great good fortune" — *bhūri nija bhāga bicāri*. "Unseen" — *alakhita*. "From the clefts of the earth" — *bhūmi-bibara-gata*: the abode of the nagas is beneath the earth. "Beyond [every] state" — *gati para*: the understanding is uncertain. "Overflowing [with feeling]" — *garagara*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari pahũka*).

[^15-3]: The heading is "মাত্রাবৃত্তে চিত্রবল্লী" (chitravalli, matra-vritta).. The author's words. "All the nagas" — *sa[ba] nāgagaṇa*: the second letter is covered by a blot in print; *saba* is adopted — the reading is unclear. "Deeming his fortune fulfilled" — *māni nija nija saphala bhāga*. "Greatly stirred in heart" — *ati tarala mana*. "Conduct" — *carita*. "Accomplished" — *sughara*. "Overpowered by the rasa of prema" — *prema-rasa-bibaśa*.

[^15-4]: The heading is "কোঽপ্যাহ—(সুহই)" ("someone says"; raga suhai).. One of the nagas speaks. "Suradhuni" — the Ganga. "Which takes away all of Madana's pride" — *madana-mada-bhara-hārī*. "The abode of joy" — *muda-sadana*. "Pours out" — *ubhāri*. "Taking someone's hand, He seems to fall" — *kāhu kara gahi girata janu*: Gaura in ecstatic self-forgetfulness. "The curves of His body" — *tanu-bhaṅgi*. "Hari" — *hari*: the first letter is damaged in print. "Talk of the city of Vraja" — *brajapura-bāta*: Gaura, Krishna in the mood of Radha, listens to talk of Vraja. "Overflowing with prema" — *preme uchali*. "The sorrows of the fallen" — *patita-duḥkha*. "The hosts of immortals pine at heart" — *amaragaṇa mana jhura*: probably the gods long because they cannot be near Him. Signature: "the Lord of servant Narahari" (*dāsa narahari pahũka*).
