Becoming steady, again displaying cleverness, He speaks kindly to the messenger:
"Go, I shall quickly follow behind you." The messenger says: "I cannot go and leave [you].
Without you Chandravali cannot live; what can I say? — go and see her state.
Get up, get up: she can bear no more delay." Saying this, rushing forward, [She] seizes [His] feet.
At [that] touch the messenger is overjoyed: with what effort She keeps composure of mind (?)!
Receiving the messenger's lovely touch, the Shyama-moon loses control of Himself, as if a serpent had bitten Him.
Glancing with restless eyes at Vrinda, He says: "Tell me, what has happened to me?"
Vrinda says: "Why do you worry? All will be well: embrace the messenger once."
Hearing [this], the jewel of the clever, irresistibly embracing the messenger, floats in bliss.
Seeing this from afar, all the friends, looking at Vrinda, smile slightly.
Lalita, leaving [her hiding place] among the lovely mallika creepers, speaks angrily, knitting her brows:
"Go, go there! There is no point in staying here: your incomparable ways I cannot bear.
What would I not say, O you greedy for love-play! — [but] let all those words be set aside.
What [your dealings] with Chandravali are like — this I have learned through this messenger.
Ah, ah! [What] love of yours! I have never seen such thrills in a body.
Why be shy of any of us? Drink Chandravali's nectar to your heart's content."
Hearing [this], Kanu says: "Surpassing Chandravali, in this moonlike face is a treasure of nectar.
I have received permission — now I shall drink." Saying this, He, laughing, kisses [Her] face.
Holding [Her] by the chin with His hand like a tender shoot, He jests, full of rasa;
placing [Her] on His thigh, arranging [Her] lovely attire, He sports on a new couch.
Knowing the moment is right, the dear friends carefully relieve the weariness of Both;
receiving a sign, Narahari merrily fans [Them] with a chamara, gazing at that beauty.[^17-55]

*Raga dhanashi*

**17.55.** So many of the sweetest ways of Rai and Kanu did Yamuna tell to Suradhuni!
Yamuna — what joy was in her heart! — again speaks to Suradhuni in gentle words:
"O Suradhuni! Such are [Their] ways: you asked with your heart's wish — I have told a little.
Now listen: of the charming pastimes of the Two I shall tell something, in the order of day and night."[^17-56]

*First — nishanta.*

*Raga bhairava*

**17.56.** At the end of the night the nagara of the new nikunja, proud, breaker of [the gopis'] composure,
with Rasavati, in an excess of rasa, intoxicated in the battle of rasa, is greatly wearied.
In the lovely grove-house, a mass of beauty, the Two sleep sweetly in the fullness of happiness,
as if a dense mass of cloud entwined with lightning were shining upon the wide bed.
Vrinda, knowing the time, gives instructions to the beasts and birds dwelling in Vrindavana;
at her bidding, very alert, rejoicing, they display their skills.
Near the nikunja the beasts roam with many playful gestures, intoxicated with play;
sharis, shukas, kokilas, peacocks — flocks of birds — raise a fresh chorus in various ways.
Swarms of bees, utterly intoxicated with honey, hum in the lovely kunja;
there is no equal comparison on earth: who, drinking that honey, would not lose composure?
Hearing the sweetest sounds, the Two — the heaviness of sleep goes far away, their hearts thrill with rapture —
in a happy rush of joy remain lying again, pressing face to lovely face.
Reared by the Two, in their cages the shari and the shuka, birds playing charmingly,
skilfully, beautifully recite [the tales of] the glorious deeds of the Two — as if steeped in nectar.
Dhani, hearing the glory of the Holder of the mountain, praises the shuka with the greatest affection;
hearing of the qualities of the Beloved, dear as life, the dear Ghanashyama is enchanted in heart.[^17-57]

**17.57.** The bee and his mate, circling and circling, speak under the guise of an incomparable lovely humming:
"Get up, Shyama-moon! The night is over — how long will You sleep at daybreak?
You do not heed [our] words: if anyone from anywhere should see, then there will be trouble:
You will be put to shame, a stain of disgrace will remain, the love of many days will be gone in a moment."
Having said this, they say to the sakhis: "O Lalita and the rest, tell us, what happiness is there here?
You are women of good families; staying in the forest, why do you needlessly bring blame upon yourselves?
Radhika has no fear — I know what her nature is:
leaving love for her own husband, she has clung to another — in this [is her] great boldness.
The Dark One is an actor, a rogue, shameless, fickle, victorious in Vraja — who does not know it?
Whatever shame before [her] family there was, that fear she has forgotten — such are the qualities of Nanda's [son]!
Therefore I fear: the cruel sister-in-law, [her] enemy, may no longer keep Radhika at home —
Fate has made [her] a forest-dweller!" Hearing this, Ghanashyama laughs in the fullness of happiness.[^17-58]

**17.58.** Rasavati, very joyful, with a stirred heart, thrilling with rapture all over,
looking with the corner of Her eye at the face of the Shyama-moon, makes so many gestures in incomparable play.
Keeping composure, slowly, slowly the Holder of the mountain looks at Dhani's face;
speaking playful words, He leaves the bed, pained that the touching of [Their] bodies is broken off.
Again, overwhelmed with the fullness of rasa, taking that lovely chin with His sprout-like hand,
He seats Radhika on His left thigh, with great tenderness.
Seeing the beauty of the loosened fine hair and dress, He smiles softly, counting [it] great good fortune,
and with His own hands skilfully arranges [Her] attire — the beautiful jewel of the clever.
He braids a plait that surpasses a serpent, sets jewels [in it], places a dot of sindura in the parting;
carefully He adorns [Her] with nose-ring and necklace, anointing the moon of [Her] face with kunkuma;
putting fine bracelets on both hands, placing a dot of musk on the chin,
He puts the bodice on [Her] breast; the restless heart, thrilling again and again with rapture, sways.
He cannot be still; quickly, with great longing of heart, feeding [Her] betel,
placing mouth to mouth, taking [it], He says: "In this I find more sweetness, Radhe!"
Seeing Shyama's playful gesture, smiling, Rasavati playfully covers Her face with the edge of Her cloth;
seeing this, the dear Ghanashyama, enchanted, embraces [Her] closely in the highest happiness.[^17-59]

**17.59.** The Nectar-faced One, happy, happily speaking some words of reproach to the Beloved, dear as life,
seeing that the moon of [His] face is like the moon [with its mark], brings a mirror and holds it before Shyama.
The Nagara, seeing His own face, is abashed; Dhani, quickly taking the edge of [Her] cloth,
wipes away the kunkuma, the dot of musk, the sindura, in the greatest joy.
Smiling, Rasavati in the fullness of rasa arranges Shyama's attire, floating in bliss.
Making many jests with each other, They seem to rain down streams of nectar.
The beauty of the faces of the Two is unprecedented, humbling the pride of countless moons;
putting their faces to the window, the sakhis, unseen, watch happily, filling their eyes.
At the incomparable perfection of prema all are mad — no memory of anything remains.
Playful Vrinda, knowing this, gives an instruction to Kakkhati, skilled in cunning;
she, sitting on a tree, under the guise of a cry playfully calls out the name "Jatila!"
Hearing this, Their restless hearts startled, They deliberate much with the sakhis.
Vrinda-devi, seeing that fun, says by a sign: "Do not worry — go quickly."
Hearing [this], in alarm, having exchanged garments, [They] leave the bed (?); all are full of merriment.
The Two, holding hands, keeping composure, set out — abodes of supreme bliss;
They go, lighting up the forest paths; on all four sides the incomparable sakhis shine.
Alongside [go] the peacocks: having gazed their fill at the abundant beauty of the ornaments, happy,
with love, dancing, they go on ahead — restless, lovely, a delight to the eyes.
Flocks of kokilas on the mango trees sing charming songs in the panchama note;
in the vanjula groves bees hum, swarm upon swarm; on the lotuses lovely rows of khanjanas [play] merrily.
The buck, in the play of Ananga, runs together with the doe, unable to keep composure;
seeing this, the dear Ghanashyama, speaking playful words, laughs in the fullness of rasa.[^17-60]

**17.60.** Seeing the sign from the corner of the Nectar-faced One's eye, the happy sakhis, lovely, skilled in cleverness,
displaying many jests with Shyama, laugh and hurry one another to go.
Rupa, Rati, Rasa, Kasturi, Lavanga, Manjulali — the manjaris and others — merrily,
taking the water-jug (?), the broken necklaces, the golden cage and the rest, go, their bodies thrilling with rapture.
The Two — each on His and Her own way — their composure gone, look at Each Other from afar;
ceaselessly water streams, streams from [Their] lotus eyes; [Their] hearts are in distress.
No word comes from the mouth; touching the chin with a lotus hand, kissing,
[He] goes two or four steps, turns back again and embraces [Her], gazing at the sweetness of [Her] face.
Thus, again and again, They cannot go, distraught with the fullness of prema.
A companion, keeping composure, consoling Radhika, takes [Her] home.
Unseen, each going to Their own home, lying down, They meet Each Other in dreams.
[Such are] the wondrous pastimes of Ghanashyama, dear as life, with the Beloved at the time of nishanta.[^17-61]

*Raga vibhasha*

**17.61.** In the morning Paurnamasi, smiling and smiling, floating in rasa, coming to Yashomati,
speaks many words of blessing for Kanu, [her] life; the queen, listening, floats in an ocean of happiness.
Bowing her head to Paurnamasi's feet, slowly, slowly she goes to where her son is sleeping;
seeing her son on the moon-like bed, she wakes [Him], saying many things.
Hearing the queen's words, that Kanu, feigning sleep, stretches His body a little —
as if an emerald-dark creeper, the colour of dense collyrium, were swaying at the touch of the wind.
Seeing the blue cloth and other things on Shyama's body, the queen is much alarmed in her mind;
Bhagavati, very fond of fun, says many things to dispel her fear.
Drinking the nectar of mother's love, He leaves the bed and rises — with new grace, in lovely radiance;
smiling very softly, happily bowing to the feet of both, He goes to where the white cows are (?).
In many, many ways, the knower of fun gets ready with Subala and His other friends;
taking the milking pail and the hobbling rope, the dear Ghanashyama is busy with the milking.[^17-62]

*Raga vibhasha*

**17.62.** Paurnamasi, seeing on the bed the moon-faced [son] of Vraja, the treasure of rasa, rejoicing,
goes home with very swift steps, having again said so much to the queen of Vraja.
Ah, ah! Who knows the secret — what ways there are at the time when night turns to dawn!
Leaving her bed, with happiness in her heart, Mukhara goes to see her granddaughter.
Entering the house, she meets Jatila — that simple-minded mother of Abhimanyu;
[Jatila] very slowly tells Mukhara what Paurnamasi had said:
"'Honouring Yashomati's word, carefully send your daughter-in-law to worship the sun:
by [his] grace there will be cows, wealth, much welfare' — so said the Devi.
You yourself, too, tell your granddaughter, so that misfortune may be removed from the cows and wealth."
Having said this, [Jatila] together with Mukhara quickly goes to wake the daughter-in-law.
Entering the lovely bedchamber, she says again and again in sweet words:
"Today you have forgotten the worship of Bhanu! Wake up, wake up, daughter-in-law, the night is over!"
Having said this, [she] grew much alarmed, seeing yellow cloth on the daughter-in-law's body;
explaining [it] as the glow of the red rays [of dawn], a clever sakhi dispelled the alarm.
Jatila, having joyfully woken the daughter-in-law, quickly went off where she had work to do;
Mukhara, showing her merriment, went home, having gazed there at her granddaughter to her eyes' fill.
Surrounded by the sakhis, that beauty leaves the bed and sits — [Her] beauty has no comparison.
Merrily they rain rasa on one another; seeing [this], Narahari remains, offering himself [as a ransom].[^17-63]

*[Here the manuscript breaks off. Editor's note: in the Agartala manuscript the text was found only up to this point.]*

[^17-1]: The edition has no colophon for kirana 17: the text of the poem breaks off, where, according to the editor's note, the manuscript was found only up to that point. The title of the kirana is therefore given **after its subheading**:. (*punaḥ prātaḥkālaḥ*), "again — the morning period". Kirana 17 runs from 17.1 to the end of the edition; this portion has 17.1–17.7.

[^17-2]: Raga vibhasha is a morning raga.. The author's words. "Full of merriment" — *raṅgiyā*. "Absorbed in bliss" — *ānande bhorā*. "The night has passed: looking [that way]" — *pohāila niśi pāne heri*: *pāne heri*, literally "looking towards" — towards what is not said (probably Shachi's house) — the understanding is uncertain. "The people of Nadiya" — *nadyā-nibāsī* (so printed). "Greatly stirred" — *ati se cañcala*. "The ways of this anuraga" — *e anurāgera rīti*. "Entering the house" — printed *bhubane* ("into the world"); the sense requires *bhabane* ("into the house"), probably a misprint. "Secretly" — *gupate*: the Ganga and the Yamuna are the goddesses of the sacred rivers, who come unseen to behold Gaura. "Gives himself up for the love of the two" — *narahari duhũ lehera nichani*: *nichani* is nirmanchhana (see note 5 of kirana 16), here "I offer myself". "The two" — the Ganga and the Yamuna. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-3]: The heading is "যমুনাহ—(ভৈরব)" ("the Yamuna says"). The stanza begins and ends with "॥২॥". Lines 1–2 and 7–8 are the author's words, lines 3–6 the Yamuna's speech. "The Suradhuni" — *suradhanī* / *suradhuni*, the Ganga. "Showing [her] honour in every way" — *kata kata mate ādara kari*. "Most experienced" — *supravīṇā*. "You know rasa" — *rasikinī*. "Blessed" — *dhanī*. "Do not be the least bit hesitant in your heart" — *kichu nā saṅkoca kariha cite*. "Regarding me as your own" — *āpana jāniye*. "Gaurangachanda" — *gaurāṅga-cā̃da*. "In order … by day and by night" — *dibāniśi krama*: in the order of the periods of the day (cf. 17.4). "Dispel my longing" — *ghucāha byathā*. "Humility, modesty and so on" — *dainya binayādi*: the Ganga, as is fitting, first speaks of her own unworthiness. "The most charming pastimes of Narahari's Lord" — *narahari pahũ līlā sulalita*. Here a new kind of mood begins: the account of the ashtakaliya-lila of Gaura and Vishnupriya — Their pastimes by day and by night, beginning with nishanta (17.5–17.7) — in the words with which the padas describe the nishanta-lila of Radha and Krishna (*rasa-lampaṭa*, *rasika nāgara*, *nāgara-rāya*). Vishnupriya is Gaura's consort in Navadvipa, revered by the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (on the wedding of Gaura and Vishnupriya, CB Adi 15). Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, following the Chaitanya-bhagavata (Adi 15: in His lila the Lord did not jest with women and did not even look at them), rejected gaura-nagari-vada — the worship of Gaura as an enjoying lover (*nāgara*): Gaura is Krishna in the mood of Radha, tasting Her love, not the enjoyer. Cf. the main notes of kiranas 11 (note 2), 12 (note 10), 14 (note 2) and 16 (note 2). Further notes in this kirana refer to this one. Signature: "Narahari's Lord".

[^17-4]: The heading is "গঙ্গা প্রাহ—(বিভাষ)" ("the Ganga said").. "Playful" — *raṅgiṇi*. "The Moon of Nadiya" — *nadīyā-cānda*. "Shesha, Shiva or Vidhi" — *śeṣa śiba bihi*: Vidhi is Brahma (cf. 13.5). "All the wishes He fulfils in secret He reveals to no one except those who know [His] heart" — *gupate sādhaye yata manoratha marami bine nā kahaye kāre*: the subject is not named; it can also be understood: "all the wishes [the women of Nadiya] secretly cherish they reveal to no one except those who know the heart (*marami*)" — the understanding is uncertain. "In the fresh manner of a nagara" — *naba nāgarāli chānde*: *nāgarāli* — the ways of a nagara. "Worship [Him]" — *ārādhaya*. "And yet" — *tamu* (= *tabu*). "On some pretext" — *kona chale*. "She gives Him her life and youth" — *jībana yaubana sõpaye tāre*: translated exactly. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "The embodiment of love" — *pirīti murati*. "For love's sake, what will one not do?" — *pirite ke ki nā karite pāre*. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pahũ*).

[^17-5]: The headings are "অথাষ্টকালাঃ—" ("now — the eight periods [of the day]") and "(ভৈরব)".. The Ganga's speech continues (the address "O Yamuna"). Each printed line has two halves ("।" in the middle, "॥" at the end). The ashtakaliya-lila is the Lord's pastimes in the eight periods of the day: nishanta (the end of the night), pratah (morning), purvahna (forenoon), madhyahna (midday), aparahna (afternoon), sayahna (evening), pradosha (early night) and nakta (night). "The rasa of love, a treasury of rasa" — *piritera rasa rasera rāśi*. "Rama" — Lakshmi. "Secret heart" — *marama*. "Neither lives without the Other" — *duhũ binā dõhe nā jiye prāṇe*. "With one mouth" — *eka mukhe*. "The ways of [Their] pastimes" — *bilāsa-rīti*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari" — merged with the Ganga's speech.

[^17-6]: The headings are "তত্র প্রথমতো নিশান্তঃ—" ("of these, first — nishanta") and "(ভৈরব)". The stanza begins and ends with "॥৫॥". From this stanza the account (apparently still the Ganga's) is given in the third person, without addresses to the Yamuna. "Avid for rasa" — *rasa-lampaṭa*, literally "greedy for rasa": an epithet of Krishna in the padas. "The crown jewel of rasikas" — *rasika-śiromaṇi*. "Full of rasa" — *rasera bhare*. "Plays" — *bilasaye*. "How [wondrous] in that rest — body blended with body" — *kibā se śayane tanu tanu miśā*: translated exactly. "Lights up the three worlds" — *trijagata karaye ālā*. "Like a golden tree encircled by a golden creeper" — *hema-taru hema-latā-beṛā*; "a garland of champaka entwined with lightning" — *taṛite jaṛita campaka-mālā*: both bodies are golden. "A dear maidservant" — *priya-dāsī*. "Puts her face to the little window" — *mukha gabākṣe diyā*. "No one can keep composure" — *dhairaja nā yāya rākhā*. "How steeped in drowsiness" — *kibā alasa-mākhā*, literally "anointed with drowsiness". For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pahũ*).

[^17-7]: The heading is "(ভৈরব)".. Nishanta continues. "Rasika-nagara" — *rasika nāgara*. "The jewel of virtues" — *guṇamaṇi*. "His heart thrilled with ecstasy" — *hiyā pulakita*. "His moon-faced beloved" — *bidhu-badanī dhanī*. "He takes her in His arms" — *karaye kole*, literally "takes on His lap / into His arms". "Dear as life" — *prāṇa-priya*. "Arranging their attire" — *beśa biraciyā*. "Tambula" — betel (cf. 14.6). "Composure is not held" — *dhṛti nā bāndhe*. "Lip pressed to lip, slowly, playfully, They sink back again onto that bed" — *adhare adhara dhari dhīre puna aṅga gaṛāyaye raṅge se śeje*: translated exactly. "Narahari again tries to wake [Them]" — *narahari phiri jāgāite karu yatana*: the poet puts himself in the place of the maidservant who wakes the Divine Couple (cf. 17.5, lines 5–7); it can also be understood: "the maidservant again tries to wake [Them]; [so says] Narahari". "Even so" — *tamu* (= *tabu*). For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3.

[^17-8]: The headings are "অথ প্রাতঃ—" ("now — the morning", pratah) and "(ললিত)".. Raga lalita is a morning raga. "Cannot hold His composure" — *nā dhairaja bāndhe*. "The moon-faced one" — *śaśimukhī*: Vishnupriya. "In a sweet manner" — *madhura chānde*. "In the absorption of that rasa" — *se rasa ābeśe*. "How tenderly He treats her" — *kata nā ādari tāya*, literally "showing her so much affection". "Arranging His garment" — *sambari basana*. "The king of nagaras" — *nāgara-rāya*. "Troubled" — *biyākula*. "Embracing" — *āliṅgana kari*. "Out of the chamber" — *mandira bāhire*: *mandira* here means "chamber, house". "From the corner of His eye" — *ā̃khi-koṇe*. "The crown of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara*. "Entice the world" — *bhubana-lobhā*. The stanza ends with "॥৭॥"; 17.8 ("বিভাষ", "প্রভাত সময় গোরা…") — the next portion. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari's Lord" (*narahari-pahũ*).

[^17-9]: The heading is "(বিভাষ)".. The morning (pratah; cf. note 8) continues. "Full of rasa, floating in rasa" — *rasamaya rase bhāsi*. "Comes and sits down [in His place]; there…" — *āsi baisaye yathā … yogāya tathā*, literally "where He sits… there he offers". "His dear servant" — *priya-dāsa*. "Pitcher" — *jhāri*, a spouted water vessel. "His elders" — *guru-jane*. "The morning rites" — *prātaḥ-kriyā*. "Telling of former pastimes" — *puruba bilāsa kaiyā*: probably of the pastimes of Vraja; Gadadhara Pandita in Krishna's lila is Radha (*Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*). "Mukunda and Madhava" — kirtan singers (cf. 15.7). "And yet" — *tamu* (= *tabu*, as in 17.6). "The nagara of Gokula" — *gokula-nāgara*: Krishna. "The thief of the lives of the nagaris of Nadiya" — *nadīyā-nāgarī-parāṇa-corā*: translated exactly; these are the words of "someone" among those present. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari pahũ*).

[^17-10]: The heading is "(ললিত)". The stanza begins and ends with "॥৯॥". Raga lalita is a morning raga. Short odd lines and long even lines, as in print. "At play" — *raṅge*. "Way of prema" — *premera gati*. "When closeness with the body of Him who is dear as life was broken off" — *prāṇa-priya-aṅga saṅga-bhaṅge*: after Gaura rose and left the chamber (17.7); translated exactly. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "Bedchamber" — *śayana-gehā*. "With what fresh love" — *kata ki naba lehā* (*lehā* — "love"). "Sita" is Advaita's wife; "Malini" is Shrivasa's wife; both are Shachi's elder friends. "The lovely one" — *dhanī*: Vishnupriya. "Having allowed [her] to bathe" — *snāne anumati diyā*. "Gorachanda" — *gorā-cānda*. "Auspicious rites" — *maṅgala rīti*. "The servant Ghanashyama" — *dāsa ghanaśyāma*: Ghanashyama (Dasa) is another name of Narahari Chakravarti himself (see the notes of kirana 1); the poet places himself in the lila as a servant. This is the stanza's signature.

[^17-11]: The heading is "(বিভাষ)".. "Fragrant oil and turmeric [she wished] to apply" — *sugandhita taila haladi mākhiba(?)*: the reading is unclear — the last letter of the verb is indistinct; taken as Vishnupriya's words or intention. "Towel" — *pānitolā*. "As if streams of nectar were pouring down" — *sudhā-rāśi bariṣe janu*. "With wagtail eyes" — *khañjana-locanī*: lively, beautiful eyes. "Places them all in the room for the worship of the Deity" — *debapūjā ghare dhare saba*. "Shrivasa's wife" — *śrībāsa-gṛhiṇī*: Malini. "Narahari gives up his life for it" — *narahari-prāṇa nichaye tāhe*: *nichaye* — nirmanchhana, "offers himself up" (cf. note 2). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-12]: The heading is "(তোড়ী)".. "Advaita's wife" — *adbaita-banitā*. "Kitchen" — *randhana-mandira*. "O lady" — *ṭhākurāṇi*. "How many shall I name?" — *kata niba nāma*. "She is [Lakshmi herself]" — *lakhimī eho*. "Offerings" — *upahāra*: here probably the dishes they cooked. "Those of whom I have heard [that they were] in Nanda's house in the town of Vraja — they have appeared here" — *brajapure nanda-mandire ye śuni tā̃hārā ethāi prakaṭa haila*: Vishnupriya's friends are the same who served in Nanda's house in Vraja (by tradition Radha and Her friends cooked for Krishna in Yashoda's house); the understanding is uncertain. "Ai" — *āi*, "mother": Shachi. "Her daughter-in-law" — *badhū*: Vishnupriya. "The Moon of Nadiya, Narahari's life" — *narahari-prāṇa nadiyā-cānde*. Signature: "Narahari" (*narahari-prāṇa*).

[^17-13]: The heading is "অমর(?) তোড়ী". A raga "amara-todi" is not known to us; possibly a misprint, or "অপর" (*apara*, "another [todi]" — after the todi of 17.11) — the reading is unclear.. Each printed line has two halves ("।" in the middle, "॥" at the end). "Child" — *bāchā*: Shachi addresses Gadadhara affectionately. "That body has grown thin" — *o tanu khīṇa*. "I understood from the signs" — *pāilu cina*. "He will heed [your] words" — *bāṇī mānibe*. "Bathe together with everyone" — *snāna karaha sakala sahe*. "All are glad to bathe" — *sabhe sināite ulāsa bāse*. "It is proper to bathe at home" — *gṛhe sināite ucita haya*: probably rather than in the Ganga, so as not to delay the meal (cf. line 2). Narahari here is a participant in the lila, Gaura's servant. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-14]: The heading is "(তোড়ী)".. Two halves per line; in lines 2, 4, 6, 8 there is no "।" between the halves. "In seclusion" — *birale*. "His dear servant" — *priya dāsa*. "Fine garments" — *saruyā basana*. "Tying the ends of the thick locks" — *bāndhi agra-bhāga kuntala-bhārā*. "Like a stream of the Yamuna" — the dark hair. "Taking Narahari by the hand, the Lord" — *dhari pahũ naraharira kare*: it may also be understood as "the Lord of Narahari, taking [the servant] by the hand" — the understanding is uncertain. "To the room of the Deity" — *debera ghare*: Gaura worships the household Deity. "Cannot contain His heart" — *hiyā dharite nāraye*. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-15]: The heading is "তুরস্ক তোড়ী" (the conjunct is indistinct: "তুরষ্ক"?) — raga turushka-todi ("Turkish todi"). The stanza begins and ends with "॥১৪॥". "Lord Nitai" — *pahũ nitāi*: Nityananda. "Blue garments" — *nīla basana*: Nityananda (Balarama) wears blue. "Who attracts the world" — *bhubana-lobhā*. "Gadadhara, Gauri[dasa], Gadadhara [Dasa]" — *gadādhara gaurī dāsa gadādhara*: probably Gadadhara Pandita, Gauridasa Pandita and Gadadhara Dasa (cf. 17.15, lines 5–6, where "Gauridasa" and "Gadadhara Dasa" are named); "Gadadhara, Gauridasa, Gadadhara" is also possible — the division is unclear. "Yadu" — *yadu* (?): the reading is unclear — the letters are indistinct (possibly *baṛu*); the person is not identified. "As if moons were rising" — *cā̃dera udaya yena*. "Gadadhara, [dear to her] as life" — *prāṇa gadādhare*. "The dining room" — *bhojana-ghare*. "Gadai" — Gadadhara. "Washes everyone's feet" — *pāda pākhālaye*. Signature: "the servant Narahari" (*dāsa narahari*).

[^17-16]: The heading is "অমর পঞ্চম" (raga "amara-panchama"; cf. note 13 — possibly "অপর", "another").. "Playful quarrel" — *rasa-kalaha*: Advaita and Nityananda banter at the meal (cf. CC Madhya 3). "Shrinivasa" — *śrīnibāsa*: Shrivasa. "Gadadhara Dasa" — *dāsa-gadādhara*. "Dhananjaya" — Dhananjaya Pandita. "Shridhara-vatu" — *śrīdhara baṭu*: *baṭu* — "young brahmin, brahmachari"; whether the word refers to Shridhara or names a separate person is unclear. "Sadashiva" — Sadashiva Kaviraja (?). "Vasu" — *bāsu*: probably Vasu Ghosha (cf. 15.7). "Masters of the meal" — *bhojana-paṭu*, "skilled at eating" — said in jest. "Fill a plate" — *sājāyā thāli*. "Hiding behind Malini, unseen" — *mālinīra āṛe hai alakhita*. "The Lord of her life" — *nija parāṇa-nātha*: Gaura. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "Achamana" — rinsing the mouth after eating. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-17]: The heading is "(তোড়ী)". The stanza begins and ends with "॥১৬॥". Two halves per line. "Talks merrily of the meal" — *bhojana-kautuka kathā*. "Many-coloured seats" — *bicitra āsane*. "Having eaten their fill" — *paripūrṇa bhojana-prakāre*. "His own lodging" — *nija nija bāsā*. "Lay down on His bed" — *śeje aṅga gaṛāila*. "Fan [Him]" — *karaye bātāsa*. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-18]: The heading is "(ভূপালী)", raga bhupali.. Two halves per line. "Daughter-in-law" — *badhū*: Vishnupriya. "Taking Shachi with them" — *śacīre laiyā*. "Having rinsed her mouth" — *ācāiyā*. "Unable to restrain herself for affection" — *snehe thira nā bāndhe*. "In a circle" — *maṇḍali chānde*. "The nectar of her Beloved's lips" — *priya-adharāmṛta*: the remnants of Gaura's meal, sanctified by His lips (*adharāmṛta*); Vishnupriya shares them with her friends. "What will Narahari understand of this way?" — *ki bujhiba narahari e rīti*. "The morning pastimes" — *prātaḥkāla līlā*: with this stanza the pratah period ends. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-19]: The headings are "অথ পূর্বাহ্ণঃ—" ("now — the forenoon period", purvahna; cf. note 5) and "(দেশপাল)", raga deshapala (cf. 12.10). The stanza begins and ends with "॥১৮॥". "The drowsy lotuses of His eyes" — *alasala ā̃khi-kamala*. "Unseen" — *alakhita*. "She cannot meet [His] eyes" — *dite nāre ā̃khete ā̃khi*. "The Lord full of rasa grew restless" — *rasamaya pahũ byākula haite*. "The birds of His eyes, flying up and alighting, drink the sweet nectar of [her] face" — *ā̃khi-pākhī uṛi paṛi piye mukha amiyā miṭhi*: the figure of the chakora drinking moonlight. "Eyes upon eyes — what play, what a manner" — *nayāne nayāne bhaṅgi ki bhāti*. Translated exactly. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "May Narahari be offered up to this sport" — *narahari rahu nichani e raṅga*: *nichani* — nirmanchhana (cf. note 2). "Some" — *keho keho*: probably the maidservants. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-20]: The heading is "(মায়ূর)", raga mayura. The stanza begins after the end of 17.18. The forenoon (purvahna; cf. note 19) continues. Short and long lines alternate, as in print. "Ah" — *āhā mari*. "Manner" — *chānde*. "Looking at each other eye to eye" — in print *harāhari duhũ ākhye ākhi diyā*; *harāhari* is probably *herāheri* ("looking at each other"), *ākhye* = *ā̃khe*: the reading is unclear. "The Two" — Gaura and Vishnupriya (continuing 17.18). "Tambula" — betel (cf. 17.6). "Your Beloved, dear as life" — *tuyā prāṇa-priyā*. "Do as is fitting" — *karo ye ucita haya*. "The Moon of Nadiya" — in print it resembles *natmābidhu* / *nagyāsidhu*; probably *nadyā-bidhu* (cf. *nadyā-nibāsī*, 17.1): the reading is unclear. "Little box" — *sampuṭa*, a betel casket. "Rejoices in body, covered with thrills of ecstasy" — *ulasita tanu jhā̃pila pulakābali*. "With the remainder" — *abaśeṣa saha*: the rest of the tambula, sanctified by Gaura's lips (cf. *adharāmṛta*, note 18). "Saying so many things" — *kata nā kaiyā*. "The rarest of things" — *se ati dulaha*: *dulaha* = *durlabha*, "hard to obtain". "Gave to the servant Narahari" — *dila narahari dāse*: the poet is a participant in the lila, a servant (cf. 17.13–17.15). For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "the servant Narahari".

[^17-21]: The heading is "(ধানশী)", raga dhanashi. The stanza begins and ends with "॥২০॥". Each printed line has two halves. "With a friend" — *sakhīra saṅge*. "The fullness of thrills" — *pulaka bhare*. "What the Lord will eat at midday" — *pahũ madhyāhne bhuñjiba yāhā*. "A most secluded place" — *atibirale*. "Putting her eyes to a lovely little window" — *cāru gabākṣe nayāna diyā* (cf. 17.5). "Flower garden" — *puṣpa kānana*: probably the garden by the house (cf. 17.25, "the garden near the house"). "How great the eagerness" — *uchāha bahu*. "Ah" — *mari*. "A love unequalled in the world" — *jagate atula prīta*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-22]: The heading is "(কামোদ)", raga kamoda.. Two halves in each line. "Charming" — *binodiyā*. "Moment by moment" — *tile tile*. "Offers up His life to Vishnupriya's qualities" — *nichaye parāṇa gorā biṣṇupriyā-guṇe*: *nichaye* — nirmanchhana, "offers up" (cf. note 2). "Having become steady" — *thira haiyā*. "The outer house" — *bāhira bhabane*: the part of the house where guests are received. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-23]: The heading is "(দেশপাল)", raga deshapala (cf. note 19).. The language of the stanza is Brajabuli. "Graceful, loving" — *sughara sanehi* (*sughaṛa*, "graceful, skilful"; *sanehī*, "loving"). "[His] deeds are the great depth of the heart" — *carita ati hiya thāha*: *thāha* (Hindi *thāh*) means "bottom, depth"; possibly "[His] deeds [are grasped only in] the depth of the heart" or "the heart finds no bottom [to them]" — the understanding is uncertain. "Supreme joy" — *parama uchāha*. "The Lord of their life" — *prāṇa-pahũ*. "In a radiant throng" — in print *chabipañje*; probably *chabi-puñje*, "in a throng of beauty": the reading is unclear. "The Subduer of Manmatha" — *manoja-damana*: Manmatha (Kamadeva) is the god of love; Gaura conquers him by His beauty (cf. 17.25, 17.29). "Youth" — *kiśora*. "Sways with bhava" — *bhābe ṭalamala*. "Lotus eyes" — *jalaja-diṭhi*. "Taking Gadadhara by the hand" — *gahi gadādhara-kara*: Gadadhara Pandita is Radha in Krishna's lila (*Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*; cf. note 9). "Reveals the excellent rasa of Vraja" — *bara-barajarasa-parakāśa*. "The forenoon" — *pūrbāhṇa samaya*: with this stanza the purvahna ends. Signature: "the servant Narahari" (*dāsa narahari*).

[^17-24]: The headings are "অথ মধ্যাহ্নঃ—" ("now — midday", madhyahna; cf. note 5) and "(শ্রীরাগ)", raga shri. The stanza begins and ends with "॥২৩॥". "The Moon Gaura" — *gauraśaśī*. "Flower garden" — *puṣpabāṭi*. "Place" — *thala* (= *sthala*). "Sharis, shukas" — female and male parrots, the singing birds of the lila. "Flocks of cuckoos" — *piku-kula* (*pika*, the Indian cuckoo). "As if instruments were sweetly playing" — *yena bāje yantra madhura bhā̃ti*. "Peacocks, spreading their tails" — *śikhi pākhī pākhā pasāri*, literally "spreading their wings". "Varied plumage" — *nānā pakṣa-śobhā*. "Encircled by rows of creepers" — *latāli-balita*. "With what delight" — *ki raṅge*. Signature: "the Lord of Narahari" (*narahari pahũ*).

[^17-25]: The heading is "(সুহই)", raga suhai.. Two halves in each line. "In a kunja of Vrindavana" — *bṛndābana-kuñje*: Gaura in the mood of Vraja. "Very restless" — *cañcala ati*. "At the little window" — *gabākṣa-dbāre*. "With an unseen glance saw Him" — *alakhita diṭhe dekhila tāre*: the subject is not named; understood as Vishnupriya (cf. 17.18, 17.20), but it can also be understood as "[He] with an unnoticed glance saw her"; cf. line 5 — the Two see each Other. "New love" — *naba leha* (*leha* = *sneha*). "Without blinking she looks" — *animikha ā̃khe dekhaye seha*: *seha* is "she" or "He" — the understanding is uncertain. Translated exactly. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-26]: The heading is "(ধানশী)", raga dhanashi.. The language is Brajabuli. "The youth of Vraja" — *baraja-kiśora*. "Who breaks the pride of Rati's husband" — *rati-pati-garaba-bhañjana*: Rati's husband is Kamadeva (cf. note 23). "Lord" — *nāha* (= *nātha*). "In the garden of lovely flowers near the house" — *bhabana-samīpa sukusuma-kānana-māha*. "Some come, [others] go" — *kou āyata yāta*. "Nitai and Advaita" — *nitāi … adbaita*. "No one's eyes blink" — *kāhu nayana nā nimikha sañcaru*: *sañcaru* — the reading is unclear (the letters are indistinct); understood as "no blink arises". "He begins to speak of former [pastimes]" — *racai puruba prasaṅga*: probably of the pastimes of Vraja (cf. note 9); the subject is not named — Gaura (or the assembly). "Overflowed" — *umaṛi*. "That joy" — *uchāha*. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-27]: The heading is "(সারঙ্গ)", raga saranga.. Two halves in each line; after the first half of line 7 the print has "॥" instead of "।". "The mother of the world" — *jagata-jananī*. "Daughter-in-law" — *badhū*: Vishnupriya. "Prepare and give all the food" — *deha saba sāmagrī sājāyā*: *sāmagrī* — provisions, food. "Nimai and Nitai" — *nimāi nitāi*: Gaura and Nityananda. "Together with everyone" — *sabhā laiyā*. "Now it is already midday" — *ebe haila duprahara*, literally "now it is the second prahara". "The lovely one" — *dhanī*: Vishnupriya. "Mother-in-law" — *śāśuṛī*: Shachi. "Ishana" — *īśāne*: Ishana, Shachi's servant (cf. kirana 4). "Sent off" — to the garden where Gaura is with His associates (line 1). "Kshira" — *kṣīra*, thickened milk; "butter" — *nanī*, fresh butter; "cream" — *sara*, the skin of boiled milk. "Nitaichanda" — *nitāi cā̃da*. "How they long" — *kata nā sādha*. "At the midday hour — most charming pastimes" — *madhyāhna kālete ati lalita bilāsa*: with this stanza the madhyahna ends. Signature: "the servant Narahari" (*narahari dāsa*).

[^17-28]: The headings are "অথ অপরাহ্ণঃ—" ("now — the afternoon period", aparahna) and "(ভাটিয়ালী)", raga bhatiyali.. Two halves in each line. "The secret heart of Gora" — *gorā-marama*. "Half a moment" — *tila ādha*. "He sees Vishnupriya at the little window; with an unseen glance she saw Him" — *dekhe biṣṇupriyā gabākṣa-dbāre alakhita diṭhe dekhila tāre*: the subjects are not named; it can also be understood as "Vishnupriya looks from the little window; with an unseen glance she saw Him" or "…He saw her unnoticed" (cf. note 25) — the understanding is uncertain. "With the corner of her eyes" — in print *ā̃khora koṇe* (= *ā̃khira*). "I do not know what she told Him" — *nā jāniye kibā kahila tāne*: the speaker is not named; probably Vishnupriya — with her glance. "Jewel among women" — *ramaṇī-maṇi*: Vishnupriya. "With the rasikas" — *rasika saṅge*: with His associates. "Slow gait" — *manthara gati*. "Understands nothing" — *kichu nā bujhe*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-29]: The heading is "(পৌরবী)", raga paurabi (*paurabī*; apparently a variety of raga purvi, an evening raga).. "Suradhuni" — the Ganga (cf. note 3). "Does not come to the lips to be told" — *kahite nā āise mukhe*. "From one another" — *āne āne*. "The celestial women with their companions" — *gaṇa saha suranārī*. "As if in play" — *kautuka-cchale*, literally "on the pretext of play". "That form" — in print *onā rūpa*: *onā* is probably "that, His" (cf. Hindi *un*): the reading is unclear. "Which enchants the minds of the three worlds" — *tribhubana mana-mohaniyā*. "Float in the tears of their eyes" — *bhāse ā̃khera jale*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3 (the celestial women, seeing Gaura's beauty, are filled with prema; cf. the main notes of kiranas 11–16). "The daughter of Jahnu" — *jahnu-kanyā*: the Ganga. "Surged up" — *uthalala*: rose in waves. "At the close of the day" — *dibābasāne*: with this stanza the aparahna ends. Signature: "Narahari" (*narahari prāṇanāthera*, "of the Lord of Narahari's life").

[^17-30]: The headings are "অথ সায়াহ্নঃ—" ("now — evening", sayahna) and "(গৌরী)", raga gauri. The stanza begins and ends with "॥২৯॥" — translated in full. Short and long lines alternate. "The crown of rasikas" — *rasika-śekhara*. "Jahnavi" — the Ganga, "daughter of Jahnu". "Touches the cool water" — *paraśi śītala nīre*. "Hari takes away the pride of the elephant" — *kuñjara-garaba hare hari*: Gaura's gait is lovelier than an elephant's. "Manmatha keeps no composure" — *manamatha nā dharaye dhṛti* (cf. note 23). "Glancing unseen on all sides" — *alakhita cāri pāśe nirakhite*. "The Beloved of His life" — *prāṇera piyā*: Vishnupriya. "He holds firm, [yet] composure cannot be kept" — *dhṛti dharu, nā dhairaja bā̃dhe*. "Thirsty chakora birds" — *tṛṣita cakora pākhī*: the chakora drinks moonlight (cf. note 19). "She cannot hold [herself in her] body" — *dharite nāraye dehā*: she cannot bear [the separation]; translated exactly. "Unseen, meeting [His] eyes with her eyes" — *alakhita ā̃khe ā̃khi diyā*. "In incomparable, dense bliss" — *anupama ghana ānande*. "Does not grasp the secret" — *nā marama bujhe*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari". The next stanza is 17.30, continuing the sayahna.

[^17-31]: The heading is "(গৌরী)", raga gauri (cf. 17.29).; lines 1–4 of are the end of 17.29 (translated in the previous portion). The evening (sayahna; cf. note 30) continues. "The evening rites" — *sandhyākālera kriyā*. "Having carefully served Vishnu" — *sebi biṣṇu yatane*: the household Deity (cf. 17.13); Shachi's prayer is addressed to Him. "By Your own grace" — *āpana guṇe*, lit. "by Your own qualities". "Boyish ways" — *bālaka rīti*: Nityananda, like a child, obeys no one (cf. CB, Madhya 3–4). "No misfortune" — in print *bibini*; probably *bighini* (= *bighna*, "obstacle, misfortune"): the reading is unclear. "Standing at a distance" — *dūre rahi*. "Embraces" — *karaye kole*, "takes onto her lap, holds close". "May Narahari be offered up" — *narahari rahu nichani* (nirmanchhana; cf. note 2). "Loveliness of love" — *prema paripāṭi*. "At the evening hour" — *sāyāhnakāle*: this stanza ends the sayahna. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-32]: The headings are "অথ প্রদোষঃ—" ("now — pradosha", the early night; cf. note 5) and the raga "আম্রপঞ্চম" (?): the reading is unclear — the second letter in print is a conjunct with ra-phala and looks like *āmra-pañcama*; possibly the same raga as "অমর পঞ্চম" before 17.15 (note 16). The stanza begins and ends with "॥৩১॥". "In solitude" — *nirajane*. "Watches the road [by which] His dear associates [will come]" — *nirikhaye priya parikara-patha-pāne*. "Incomparable are the ways of the Two" — *atula dõhāra rīti*. "The eyes do not blink" — *nimikha nā bahe ā̃khe*. "The former tales" — *puruba kāhinī*: probably of the pastimes of Vraja (cf. notes 9, 26). "Of Narahari's Lord" — *narahari pahũ*: the signature merged with the line; it may also be understood as "Lord Narahari tells…" — the understanding is uncertain. Signature: "Narahari's Lord".

[^17-33]: The heading is "(ধানশী)", raga dhanashi. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৩২॥". Two halves in each line. "That all had come" — *gamana sabhāra*: the associates had come to Shachi's house (17.31). "Still" — *tamu* (= *tabu*, cf. note 9). "[Shachi] bows at [her] feet" — *praṇame padatale*: the subject is not named; probably Shachi bows to Sita, Advaita's wife; it may also be understood the other way round — the understanding is uncertain. "Went forward" — *āgusāri*. "Embrace" — *kare kore*. "Come and see" — in print *dekhasiyā*; probably *dekha āsiyā*: the reading is unclear. "Come to the meal with everyone" — *bhojane āisaha sabhā laiyā*: apparently addressed to Gaura; Gadadhara passes the invitation on to all (cf. 17.14). "Sita Devi together with Malini serves" — *sītādebī mālinī sahite kari paribeśana*: it may also be understood as "[Shachi] together with Sita Devi and Malini serves" — the understanding is uncertain (likewise line 21). "Achamana" — rinsing the mouth after eating (cf. note 16). "Pleasing to the heart" — *manohita*. "Her friends" — *saiyere* (*saī*, "friend"). "The Two with their [companions]" — *dõhe gaṇa saha*: Sita and Malini. "Malini fed her daughter-in-law Vishnupriya" — cf. 17.17. "With stricken hearts" — *kātara parāṇe*. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-34]: The heading is "(সুহই)", raga suhai (cf. note 25).. Two halves in each line. "Nothing like it is seen anywhere" — *hena nā dekhi yene*: *yene* probably means "anywhere"; the reading is unclear. "Drowsiness" — *alasa*. "Without lying down" — *śayana bine*. "Discomfort will grow in the body" — *pīṛā bāṛiba dehe*. "Nitai Thakura" — *nitāi ṭhākure*: Nityananda lived in Shrivasa's house (CB, Madhya 4). "They cannot hold on to life" — *prāṇa dharite nāre*: of the pain of separation; translated exactly. "So wretched a night" — *pāpiyā rāti*, lit. "sinful night". "What became of Him" — *ye haila*, lit. "what happened": Gaura's state on parting from His associates; the understanding is uncertain. "Charming way" — *lalita rīti*. "At the hour of pradosha" — *pradoṣa kāle*: this stanza ends the pradosha. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-35]: The headings are "অথ নিশা—" ("now — night", nisha: the eighth period; in note 5 it is called "nakta") and "(কানড়া)", raga kanada, a night raga. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৩৪॥". "His composure gone" — *dhṛti gata*. "Fixed His eyes on the path by which Vishnupriya would come" — *biṣṇupriyā gati-pathe nayāna diyā*. "Young jewel among women" — *naba ramaṇī-maṇi*: Vishnupriya. "Adorns herself" — *biracaye beśa*. "Anointed with kunkuma" — *kuṅkumete māji*. "Sindur" — *sindūra*, red powder, the mark of a married woman. "Designs in sandal and the like" — *citra candanādi*. "Musk" — *mṛgamada*. "A blue sari" — in print it looks like *nīpa*; probably *nīla*, "blue": the reading is unclear (in the padas Radha wears blue). "Overflows" — in print it looks like *unaṛaye*; probably *umaṛaye*, "overflows, surges" (cf. *umaṛi*, note 26): the reading is unclear. "Love" — *lehā* (= *sneha*). "To the Lord, the life of Narahari" — *narahari prāṇa pahũ*. "Like lightning" — *taṛita*: Vishnupriya's golden body. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-36]: The heading is "(ধানশী)", raga dhanashi.. Short and long lines alternate, as in print. "By it the pride of many Madanas is destroyed" — *tāhe kata madana-garaba nāśe*: Madana (Kamadeva) is the god of love (cf. notes 23, 26, 30). "Veil" — *ghughaṭe*. "Tenderly leads [her]" — *ādari āne*. "Bed of flowers" — *kusuma śeje*. "The fair one" — *dhanī*: Vishnupriya. "Eye to eye" — in print *ā̃kho ā̃khi* (= *ā̃khe*). "Greedy bee" — *lubadha bhramara*. "Touching the pair of golden lotuses of [her] breasts, He trembles like lightning" — *kucayuga-hema-kamala paraśi kā̃paye bijuri janu*: the subject is not named and is understood as Gaura (continuing lines 11–13); "like lightning" may also refer to Vishnupriya — the understanding is uncertain. The stanza describes the night pastimes in the words of the padas on the night pastimes of Radha and Krishna; translated exactly, without softening. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "Narahari offers his life to the Lord" — *narahari pahũ parāṇa nichai*: it may also be understood as "Lord Narahari…"; *nichai* — nirmanchhana (cf. note 2). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-37]: The heading is "(ধানশী)", raga dhanashi. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৩৬॥". Two halves in each line; the language is Brajabuli. "Their bodies were moist with sweat" — *ghāme titila gā'*. "Fan each Other with the ends of Their cloth" — *duhu dõhe karu ā̃care bā'* (*bā'* = *bāo*, "breeze"). "With languid bodies" — *alasala tanu*. "Lay down" — *śutala*. "The two became one body" — *duhũ jana bhela ekai dehā*: translated exactly (a phrase from the padas on Radha and Krishna). "New love" — *naba nehā*. "Lights up the chamber" — *ghara karaye ālo*. "May Narahari be offered up to the Two" — *narahari duhũ nichani rahu*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-38]: The headings are "ইত্যাষ্টকালঃ।" (so in print; = *ityaṣṭakālaḥ*, "thus the eight periods") — the end of the Ganga's account of the ashtakaliya-lila begun with the heading "অথাষ্টকালাঃ—" (note 5) — and "(বিভাষ)", raga vibhasha.. Lines 1–2 are the refrain (*dhru*). These are again the author's words — the frame of the conversation of the Ganga and the Yamuna (cf. 17.1–17.3). "Can he, for happiness, keep hold of [himself in his] body" — *se ki sukhe dharite pāraye aṅga*. "The Suradhuni" — the Ganga (cf. note 3). "The Lord of Vishnupriya's life" — *biṣṇupriyā-prāṇapati*: Gaura. "Overpowered" — *abaśa*. "Moon of Nadiya" — *nadyāra cā̃da*. "The loving young women of Vraja" — *premabatī braja-yubatī-jana*: the gopis. "The snare of their hearts" — *manera phānda*. "Subject to their prema and rasa" — *tā'sabhāra prema rase baśa*. "Who else will know that He is that very One — this thief of the lives of the nagaris of Nadiya" — *āne ki jāniba sei ei nadyā-nāgarī-parāṇa-corā*: Krishna of Vraja and Gaura of Nadiya are one; translated exactly. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. "The crown of nagaras" — *nāgara-śekhara*: Krishna. "Having become hidden" — *gupata haiyā*: Krishna hidden in the form of Gaura (cf. *channaḥ kalau*, "hidden in the Kali age", SB 7.9.38). Where the Yamuna's speech (lines 7–11) ends is not marked in print. "Ah, ah!" — *āhā mari mari*: an exclamation of wonder (cf. note 20). "Takes upon himself the misfortunes of the Lord's pastimes" — *pahũ līlāra bālāi laiyā*: *bālāi laoyā*, "to take the misfortune [of a beloved one] upon oneself", is an expression of tender love (cf. *nichani*). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-39]: The heading is "(ললিত বিভাষ)", raga lalita-vibhasha.. Two halves in each line. "The Jahnavi" — the Ganga, "daughter of Jahnu". "Most experienced" — *parama prabīṇā* (cf. *supravīṇā*, 17.2). "I have entrusted my life to you" — *prāṇa sõpila tohe*. "Of Gauranga" — *gaurāṅgera*. "Regarding me as your own" — *āpana jāni* (cf. 17.2). "With sweet speech" — *subhāṣe*. "Makes a request" — *nibedaye kichu*, lit. "submits something": in the next stanza the Ganga asks the Yamuna to tell of the former (Vraja) pastimes of Gaurangachanda. The stanza has no signature of Narahari.

[^17-40]: The headings are "গঙ্গা আহ—" ("the Ganga said") and "(বিভাষ)", raga vibhasha.. The answer to the Ganga's request in 17.38: now the Ganga (the river of Navadvipa, witness of Gaura's pastimes) asks the Yamuna (the river of Vraja) to tell of the former pastimes of Gaura in Vraja, that is, the pastimes of Krishna: Gaura and Krishna are one (cf. 17.37, note 38). "I entreat you" — *nibedi*. "Longing" — *bethā* (= *byathā*, cf. note 3). "Gaurangachanda" — *gaurāṅga-cā̃da*. "The former pastimes, full of rasa" — *pūraba bilāsa-rasera kathā*. "I see no others like them" — *nā dekhi āne*. "Above all" — *adhikā*. "Shyama" — Krishna, "the dark one". "He loves many" — *bahujana sane leha*. "In the order [of the periods]" — *krama-baśa* (cf. *dibāniśi krama*, 17.2, note 3): having told of Gaura's ashtakaliya-lila, the Ganga asks about the ashtakaliya-lila of Vraja. "The witness of my heart" — *marama sākhī*; it can also be understood as "the friend of my heart" (*sakhī*) — the understanding is uncertain. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-41]: The headings are "শ্রীযমুনা গঙ্গাং প্রত্যাহ—" ("Sri Yamuna answers the Ganga"; Sanskrit) and "(বিভাষ)", raga vibhasha.. "Charming and wise" — *cāru caturā*. "I shall tell" — printed *diye ye* (= *dii ye*?). "Vrishabhanu" — Radha's father, king of the cowherds. "Kirtida" — printed *kṛtikā* (marked with an editor's footnote giving the correction "কীর্তিদা"); Radha's mother is known as Kirtida. "Jatila", "Kutila", "Abhimanyu" — Radha's mother-in-law, sister-in-law and "husband"; in the understanding of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Rupa Gosvami, Ujjvala-nilamani; Jiva Gosvami), the marriage of the gopis to the gopas is an arrangement of yogamaya for the sake of parakiya-rasa: the gopis' husbands were not in fact united with them (cf. SB 10.33.37: the people of Vraja, bewildered by Krishna's maya, thought their wives were beside them). "Chitra" — printed *sucitrā* ("lovely Chitra"). Lalita, Vishakha, Chitra, Champakalata, Rangadevi, Sudevi, Tungavidya, Indulekha are Radha's eight principal sakhis. Rupa-manjari and Rati-manjari are the chief manjaris, Radha's maidservants. "A stream of nectar is far [behind]" — *sudhādhārā dūre yāra rūpa heri*: before Radha's beauty a stream of nectar is nothing; "Her" may also refer to the maidservants — the understanding is uncertain. "Uma" — Parvati; "Rati" — the consort of Kamadeva. "Playful" — *raṅgiṇī*. "Knowing rasa" — *rasikiṇī*. "Performing the abhisheka, made Queen of Vrindavana" — *bṛndābana-rāṇī kari abhiṣeka karilā*. "The daughter of Yashoda" — *yaśodānandinī* (marked with an editor's footnote explaining "একানংসা", Ekanamsha): Yogamaya, born to Yashoda at the same time as Krishna (SB 10.4). "The daughter of the ocean" — *sindhusutā*, Lakshmi. "Sold at Her feet" — *bikāila yāra pāya*. "To you" — *tuhe*. "Above all is Radha" — *adhika rādhā*: the answer to the question of 17.39 (line 4). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-42]: The heading is "(বিভাষ)", raga vibhasha.. The Yamuna's speech continues. "Puts to shame the husband of Rati" — *ratipati-nindi*: Kamadeva. "Love embodied" — *pirīti murutikhāni*. "Having gathered [in a heap] lovely garments" — *cāru cīra-caya kari*: probably the stealing of the gopis' garments (SB 10.22); after the line the edition has "(?)" — the editor's doubt about the reading. "Trees and creepers weep" — *tarulatā jhure*. "He carries [her] on His shoulders" — *bahe kāndhe kari*: cf. SB 10.30.37–38 (the proud gopi asks Krishna to carry her). "With what care" — *kata yatana pāyā*: the understanding is uncertain. "Scolding words" — *bhartsana bāṇī*. "Has sunk [in love]" — *majilena*. "Casting off" — *diyā tilāñjali*, lit. "offering water with sesame" (as to the departed), i.e. giving up for ever. "Disregarding Her husband" — *pati upekhiyā*: the parakiya-rasa of Vraja (see note 41). "She dwells in the forest" — *basati bane*. "People's slander" — *loka paribāda*. "Without [Him] for half a moment" — *tila ādha bine*. "The Lord of Her life is [Her] all" — *prāṇanātha sarabasa*. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-43]: The headings are "পুনরাহ—" ("again she said") and "(বিভাষ)", raga vibhasha. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৪২॥". The speaker is not named; from the sense, the Yamuna, answering the Ganga's question "for which of them does He hold the greater love" (17.39, line 6). After the stanza number the edition has the mark of an editor's footnote referring to 17.42–17.43: these two padas are also quoted in the Bhakti-ratnakara (taranga 5). "I am sold to your virtues" — *bikāinu mui tomāra guṇe*. "Chandravali" — *candrābali*, leader of the group of gopis rivalling Radha (cf. Ujjvala-nilamani). "Rama" — Lakshmi (cf. 17.4). "In the bloom of youth" — *nabīna yaubanī*. "Kunja-temple" — *nikuñja mandire*. "Plays merrily" — *raṅge bilase*. "Is aware of nothing" — *kichu nā sphure*. "The Dark One" — *kālā*, Krishna. "Kanu" — Krishna. "An offering [for the sake of] such love" — *hena premera nichani* (nirmanchhana; cf. note 2). "Who can find their bottom" — *kebā bā̃dhe thehā*: *thehā* — "bottom, limit" (cf. *thāha*, note 23). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-44]: The headings are "পুনরাহ—" ("again she said") and "(তোড়ী)", raga todi.. On the mark of the editor's footnote after the stanza see note 43. "The soul is one, the bodies different" — *prāṇa eka tanu bhina bhina*: cf. CC Adi 1.5 and 4.55–56 (Radha and Krishna are one soul that has taken two bodies for the sake of pastimes); in Gaura the Two are united again (CC Adi 1.5) — hence this answer belongs to the "former pastimes of Gaurangachanda" (17.39). "With what joy" — *kata ānanda māni*. "Colour" — *baraṇa*. "Dark and golden" — *śyāma gaura*: Krishna's dark colour and Radha's golden colour. "Showing the world its utmost" — *tāhāra abadhi dekhāyā jagate* (*dekhāyā* = *dekhāiyā*). "Captivating the eyes" — *nayana lobhā*. "The Dark One" — *kāliyā*, Krishna (not the serpent Kaliya). "Playful Radha" — *raṅgiṇī rādhe*. "Impatient" — *ātura haiyā*. "Eagerly takes [Her] into His embrace" — *kore karaye sādhe*. "Adversaries" — *bipakṣa loka*: those of the rival group (cf. note 43) or the elders. "For a cloud with lightning" — *ghana taṛitādi bhrame*: dark Krishna is the cloud, golden Radha the lightning. "That skilled poet, the Creator" — *sukabi bihi*: Vidhi (Brahma). "Composes" — printed like *raciba* ("I shall compose"); from the sense probably *racila* ("composed"): the reading is unclear. "Fashioned of Rai's prema" — *rāi-preme gaṛā*. "Consists of Kanu's prema" — *kānu-premamaya*. "The equal of a beast" — *paśu-samāna*. For the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas see note 3. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-45]: The heading is "মাত্রাবৃত্তে সুরধনী" — the metre "suradhuni" (*suradhanī*, matra-vritta); printed without the word "chhandah". The name of the metre coincides with the name of the Ganga, whom the Yamuna addresses in this stanza — probably a deliberate play by the author.. Two halves to a line. "The beginning of that account" — *tāra ādi carita*: Radha's family, friends and maidservants, Her love for Krishna (17.40–17.43). "The account of days and nights" — *dina rajanī kathā*: the Yamuna passes to the pastimes of Radha and Krishna (the answer to the question of 17.39, line 7). "Met" — *mililā*. The stanza has no signature of Narahari.

[^17-46]: The headings are "কস্মিন্ দিবসে—" ("on a certain day"; Sanskrit) and "(পৌরবী)", raga paurabi (cf. 17.28). The stanza begins and ends with "॥৪৫॥". The story: Krishna, disguised as a flower-seller (*mālinī*), comes to Radha. "Who steals the composure of Vraja" — *barajadhṛti-hara*. "The beloved" — *piya* (= *priya*); it can also be understood as "drinking" — the understanding is uncertain. "Having prepared flowers" — *kusuma sajja kari*. "Yavatapura" — *yābaṭapura*, Yavata, the village of Radha's father-in-law. "Looking at Himself" — *āpuni āpanāre heriyā*: admiring Himself in a woman's guise. "Come and take" — *āisa leo giyā*. "Kokila" — the Indian cuckoo. "Vinodini" — "the Playful One", Radha. "Hey, come here" — *hera āiso*. "Hurries [to her]" — printed *cale dhāyā*; possibly *ḍhale* ("leaning"): the reading is unclear. "The city of the gods" — *surapura*. "Come, let me see" — *dekhi ohe*. "Sorely afflicted by Madana" — *madanabhare ati pīṛita*: Madana is the god of love; here, as in the padas, a figure for Radha's prema (below — "the play of Ananga"). "Only the one dear to me as life strings [flowers] like this" — *erūpa gā̃the mora prāṇapiyā*: Radha recognises Krishna's hand. "I feel like giving you my life" — *mane kariye prāṇa diye tore*. "Gajamoti pearl" — *gajamoti*, "elephant pearl", said to form in an elephant's head; probably a pearl in Radha's ornament. "As you yourself say" — *nija bale*: the understanding is uncertain. "On the lovely hills of [Her] breasts" — *rucira kucagiri upare*. "Trembles all over" — *tharahari kā̃pe*. "The play of Ananga" — *anaṅga-raṅge*: Ananga ("the Bodiless") is Kamadeva; the language of the padas on Radha and Krishna; translated exactly, without softening. In the understanding of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, the love of the gopis that is called "kama" is pure prema: there is in it no wish for one's own enjoyment, only the wish to please Krishna (CC Adi 4.164–171; Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu 1.2.285, quoted in CC Madhya 8.215–216); Krishna is the transcendental Madana (CC Madhya 8.138). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-47]: The headings are "কস্মিংশ্চ—" ("and on another [day]"; Sanskrit) and "(পৌরবী)", raga paurabi. The stanza begins, continues and ends with "॥৪৬॥"; translated in full. Each printed line consists of two halves and a third, indented part; here, two lines. The story: Krishna, disguised as a girl skilled in adornment, comes to Radha and adorns Her. "The moon of Vraja" — *barajaśaśī*. "Surpassing a mass of rain clouds" — *jini sajala jaladaghaṭā*. "The beauty" — *dhanī*: both the disguised Krishna and Radha are so called (by context). "Knowing the secret" — *marama jāni*: the friend guesses who has come. "Brings [her] in with honour" — *supraveśe milaye*: the understanding is uncertain. "Never" — *kakhahu*. "Since you have asked" — printed as an unclear conjunct (like *bamni*), probably *yadi*: the reading is unclear. "Expert" — *prabīṇā*, "experienced, skilled". "The wife of Abhimanyu" — Radha (cf. note 41). "Rama" — Lakshmi. "Heroines" — *nāyikā*. "The most graceful" — printed like *sukharabara*; probably *sugharabara* (*sughaṛa*, cf. note 23): the reading is unclear. "The fragrant [guest]" — *subāse*; possibly *subeśe*, "finely dressed": the reading is unclear. "Shaming a serpent" — *phaṇi nirasi*. "Kajal" — collyrium for the eyes. "Beshar" — *beśara*, a nose pendant. "Madana himself will faint" — *madana… muruche*. "Forgetting to blink" — *dūre nimikha*, lit. "blinking is far away". "Bodice" — *kā̃culi*. "The Upholder of the world" — *bhubana-dhara*: Krishna; a play on *dhṛti* ("composure") and *dharite* ("to hold"). "Putting a finger to her chin" — a gesture of astonishment. "Luckily" — *bhāga ye* (= *bhāgye*). "O restless one" — *ohe capala*: Radha has recognised Krishna. "The noose of [your] arms" — *bhuja-pāśe*. "That Ghanashyama" — *se ghanaśyāma*: Krishna, "dark as a cloud", and at the same time the author's signature — Ghanashyama Dasa (Narahari; cf. 1.2). The language of the padas on Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly. Signature: "Ghanashyama".

[^17-48]: The headings are "কস্মিংশ্চ —" ("and on another [day]"; Sanskrit, cf. note 47) and "(তোড়ী)", raga todi. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৪৭॥". The Yamuna continues telling the Ganga of the "former pastimes of Gaurangachanda" — the pastimes of Radha and Krishna in Vraja (17.39, 17.44); the story: Krishna, hiding in the waters of the Yamuna, floats up to Radha in the guise of a lotus. "The Shyama-moon" — *śyāma śaśī*. "Avid for rasa" — *rasa-lampaṭa* (cf. note 6). "Yavata" — *yābaṭa*, the village of Radha's father-in-law (cf. note 46). "Under many pretexts" — *kari kata chala*. "The king's daughter" — *rājanandinī*: Radha, daughter of Vrishabhanu (17.40). "A parrot" — *śuka pākhī*. "Under the guise of smiles [She] rains down nectar" — *hāsimiṣe bariṣe amiyā*. "Kala" — *kālā*, "the Dark One": Krishna. "Makes the chakoras of His eyes drink it" — *piyāya he netracakore*: the chakora is a bird believed to feed on moonlight; *he* is a particle. "Unseen" — *alakhita*. "Upstream" — *ujāna*. "Ghat" — *ghāṭa*, steps leading down to the water for bathing. "The mass of happiness" — *sukha-rāśi*: Krishna. "The playful one" — *raṅgiṇī*: Radha. "Has lit up" — in print *karāche* (= *kareche*). "Kalindi" — *kālindī*, the Yamuna ("daughter of Mount Kalinda"): Radha thanks the narrator herself, the Yamuna. "With her wave-hands" — *taraṅga-kare*. "Leaving the midst of Her friends" — *āli-madhya teji*. "Pressing face to [Her] face" — *mukhe mukha jhā̃pi*, literally "covering face with face"; whose face covers whose is not said — the understanding is uncertain. "Shyamaraya" — *śyāmarāye* (agentive form). "Taking [Her] in His arms" — *kariyā kole*. "As if lightning were swinging upon a cloud" — golden Radha in the arms of dark Krishna (cf. note 44). "With playful gestures" — *bhaṅgi kari*. "Again and again" — *ghana*. "Ghanashyama" — Krishna and at the same time the author's signature (cf. note 47). The language of the padas on Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly; cf. also note 3. Signature: "Ghanashyama".

[^17-49]: The headings are "অথ রাত্রৌ—" ("now — at night"; Sanskrit) and "(গৌরী)", raga gauri (cf. 17.30). The stanza begins and ends with "॥৪৮॥". Each printed line has two halves and an indented third part; here, two lines (cf. note 47). The story: Krishna, disguised as a young bride, comes with Kundalata to Radha and presses Her feet. "Giver of the highest happiness" — *barasukhakārī*; possibly *baraja-sukhakārī*, "giver of happiness to Vraja": the reading is unclear. "Having devised a plan" — *yugati bicāri* (*yugati* = *yukti*). "Kundalata" — a friend of Radha and Krishna, the wife of Subhadra, Krishna's cousin; at Yashoda's word she comes to Yavata for Radha. "The Beautiful One" — *sundara* (masculine): the disguised Krishna. "A plate" — *thāri* (= *thāli*). "Provisions" — *sāmagrī*. "Abhimanyu's mother" — Jatila, Radha's mother-in-law (in print *abhimantera*); on the marriage of the gopis as an arrangement of yogamaya, see note 41. "Yashomati's message" — *yaśomati-bāṇī*: Yashomati is Yashoda. "Abides" — *bilase*. "The one full of rasa" — *rasabatī*: Radha (cf. 17.45). "News of Her Beloved, dear as life" — *prāṇapiyā-kathā*: of Krishna. "At that [companion]" — *tā pāne*. "Merry [girl]" — *raṅgiṇī*. "She lives in our neighbourhood" — *paṛasa-nibāsī*. "This young bride" — *nabīnā badhū*. "Is very dear [to me]; later I will introduce [her to you]" — *adhika sādhera pāche paricaya diba ye āmi*: it may also be understood as "it is her great wish that I introduce [her to you]" — the understanding is uncertain. "The Creator" — *bihi*. "She always keeps very much hidden" — *thāke ati gopane*: a young bride does not show herself to strangers (and a hint at the secret of the disguise). "Skill" — *cāturi*. "By her touch the pains of the body and the like are dispelled" — *paraśite nāśe dehādira bethā*: the guest is skilled in massage. "Soft hands" — *mṛdu kara*. "The dark young bride" — *śyāmā-badhū*: *śyāmā* means both "young woman" and "dark" — a hint at Shyama. "With a playful gesture" — *bhaṅgi kari*. "Press Her feet" — *caraṇa cāpiyā*. "Sprinkle [Her] with streams of nectar" — *siñcaha amiyā rāśi*. "The face anointed with nectar" — *sudhāmākhā mukha*. "The couch" — *pālaṅka*. "Smiling… touches the soft feet" — *hāsi mṛdu caraṇa paraśe*: *mṛdu* may also go with "smiling" ("smiling softly") — the understanding is uncertain. "Like a golden creeper troubled by the wind, like lightning" — *bātātura hemalatā, taṛita janu*. "Displaying incomparable skill" — *anupama guṇa prakāśi*. "Captivate each other's hearts" — *duhu duhu manolobhā*. "There is no pause" — *bhaṅga nahe*. "Thrills of ecstasy" — *pulaka*. "Keep their faces covered with the ends of their cloth" — *basane jhā̃pi rahe mukha*. "Winking at one another" — *ṭhārāṭhāri kari*. "Improper" — *biparīta*, literally "contrary". "Embracing a young bride — [that is] your beloved's share; I do not know what he will think of it" — *badhū-āliṅgane badhũyāra bhāge, nā jāniye o ki kahibeka mane*: *badhũyā* is "the beloved", Krishna; the reading *bhāge* and the understanding are uncertain — Kundalata jokingly reproaches Radha as if She had given the "young bride" what belongs to Krishna. "Bidding farewell to shame for good" — *lāje tilāñjali diyā*, literally "giving shame the last libation of water and sesame". "Dressing [him] up like this" — *eirūpa beśa banāiyā*. "Your own devara" — *nija debare*: the husband's younger brother; Krishna is the cousin of Kundalata's husband, that is, her devara; Radha, having seen through the disguise, answers a joke with a joke. "Play" — *bilasa*. "Whatever it may be" — *ye se hau*. "Ghanashyama" — Krishna and the author's signature (cf. note 47). The language of the padas on Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly, without softening. Signature: "Ghanashyama".

[^17-50]: The headings are "কস্মিংশ্চ—" ("and on another [day]") and "(গৌরী)", raga gauri. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৪৯॥"; translated in full. The story: Krishna, disguised as a brahmana, comes to Jatila and, with her permission, to Radha. "Overwhelmed [with longing]" — *garagara*. "Seeing the moonrise" — *candrodaya heri*: the "brahmana" has supposedly fasted until moonrise, worshipping the moon (line 5). "The excellent twice-born" — *dbijabara*. "Let me hear your words" — *śuni tuyā bāṇī*: it may also be understood as "hearing your words" — the understanding is uncertain. "A cowherd's house" — *gopera gṛhe*. "The king of the twice-born" — *dbijarāja*: the word also means "the moon" — a play on words (cf. lines 2, 16). "Your daughter-in-law" — *tuyā badhūkhāni*: Radha. "Devoted to her husband" — *patibratā*: so Jatila thinks; on Radha's marriage to Abhimanyu as an arrangement of yogamaya, see note 41. "Deeming it a pious deed" — *sukṛti māni*. "Cows and wealth" — the print is faint, apparently *dhenudhana*: the reading is unclear. "By the boon" — *bare*. "Curd, milk, ghee" — *dadhi dugdha ghṛta*. "Again and again" — *beri beri*. "In various ways" — *nānā chānde*. "How could I neglect [it]?" — *upekhi kirūpe*. "My hunger and the sorrow of my heart are gone" — *bhukha gelo manodukha*. "Vishakhika" — *biśākhikā*, an affectionate form of Vishakha's name. "An unearthly pastime of mother-in-law and daughter-in-law" — *alaukika śāśu-bauyera līlā*: Vishakha jokes that the mother-in-law herself sent the "brahmana" to her daughter-in-law. "The moon-faced One" — *śaśīmukhī*: Radha. "The beloved Ghanashyama" — *ghanaśyāma piya*: Krishna; the name is also the author's signature (cf. note 47). "Trembles all over" — *kā̃paye tharahari*. The language of the padas on Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly. Signature: "Ghanashyama".

[^17-51]: The heading is "রাগ ললিত", raga lalita (cf. 17.7, 17.9). The stanza begins after the end of 17.49 and ends with "॥৫০". Two halves per printed line; here, one line. The Yamuna speaks: the conclusion of her account of the five pastimes in Yavata (17.45–17.49) and a transition to the pastimes "by day and by night" (the answer to the Ganga's question in 17.39, line 7; cf. 17.44, note 45). "Something [of this] I have told" — *kichu kahilu*. "Who enchants Shyama" — *śyāmamohinī*. "By day and by night, separately" — *dina rajanī pṛthak mate*. "Suradhuni" — the Ganga (cf. 17.44). Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-52]: The headings are "কস্মিন্ দিবসে—" ("on a certain day"; Sanskrit) and "তোড়ী", raga todi.; ends with "॥৫১॥". The stanza is a long "dandaka" printed as continuous text; here it is given by cola, separated in print by "।" and "॥". The language is Brajabuli. The story: Radha disguised as Subala — Subala, Krishna's friend who resembles Radha, is a well-known theme of the padas ("Subala-milana"); the word "again" (*puni*, line 8) shows that Radha had disguised herself so before. "Announcing the beauty's coming, she quickly returns to [Her] home" — *dhanīgamana bhaṇi jhaṭita āyai nilaye*: the understanding is uncertain. "Within a mountain cave" — *girikuhara madhya*. "The Restless-eyed One" — *capalanayanī*, Radha. "The best of mountains" — *giribara*: probably Govardhana. "So that the deception holds" — *ki kaba janu bharama rahaye*, literally "what to say? — so that the illusion remains": the understanding is uncertain. "Full of rasa" — printed *rasili* (= *rasīlī*?): the reading is unclear. "With radiance" — *mahasā*. "With frequent glances (?), waits in hope" — *joi maga saghana dṛśa(?) āśa karaye*: the word after *saghana* is unclear in print (like *ḍhuśa* / *dṛśa*); after *karaye* the edition has "?", probably the editor's doubt; "watches the path" — printed like *magha*, read as *maga* ("path"). "To 'Subala'" — to Radha in Subala's guise; Krishna takes her for Subala. "Is all well" — faint in print, probably *kuśala*. "Was coming here" — *ita gamana karaye*: the verb is unclear in print. "Her sister-in-law" — *nanadī*, Kutila (cf. note 41). "I came" — *hāma āyalanu*: Radha speaks as Subala. "Exhausted" — printed like *kṛśa*: the reading is unclear. "Reproaching the Creator" — *bihika dei gāri*. "They rain rasa from their eyes" — *rasa bariṣe nayane*: they laugh till they weep. "Ghanashyama" — Krishna; also the author's signature (cf. note 47). "How have you become so clever?" — *kaiche tuhu sughara*: the friends tease Krishna for not recognising Radha. "Rasavati's good friend" — *rasabatī-susakhika*: "Subala"; *Rasabatī*, "She who is full of rasa", is Radha. "Cleverness" — *sugharāi*. "The jewel of the clever" — *caturamaṇi*, Krishna. "The Fair One" — *gorī*, Radha. "The play of Madana" — *madanaraṅga*: the language of the padas; on the kama of the gopis as pure prema, see note 46. "The incomparable union" — *atula lagana*. Translated exactly, without softening. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-53]: The headings are "কস্মিংশ্চ—" ("and on another [day]") and "মায়ূর", raga mayura (cf. 17.19).; ends with "॥৫২॥". Each printed line consists of two halves and a third, indented part; here, two lines. The story: Radha comes to Krishna as "her own messenger", in the guise of a messenger. "On the pretext of worshipping Gauri" — *gaurīpūjanachale*: Radha's usual pretext for leaving home (cf. the worship of Surya). "Conquering the people of the world" — *bhubanajana-jai*. "Swaying at every [step]" — *nai nai* ("bending, bending"). "As if dancing" — *janu nṛtyata bhale*. "Grama, murchhana, tana, shruti, yati" — terms of Indian music: scale, mode, run, microtone, caesura. "Kinnaris" — celestial singers. "Conch-shell [bangles]" — *śaṅkha*; the word before it is *kāhu* (so printed): the reading and understanding are unclear. "Jingle" — the onomatopoeias *jhanananajhanana*, *riṇi*, *jhinini*, *runu runuruna* are given in transliteration. "Husbands of Rati" — *ratipati*, Kamadeva. "Her own messenger" — *nija dūtī*: Radha plays the part of her own messenger. "Dhira-samira" — *dhīrasamīra*, a well-known kunja on the bank of the Yamuna in Vrindavana. "Sari" — *sārī*, a female myna; in the padas, a messenger of Radha and Krishna. "For news of where you are" — *thiti-sambāda-hetu*. "Of playful glances" — *dṛga-bhaṅginī*: it may be an address to the messenger or "[her] whose glances are playful" — the understanding is uncertain. "Who delight people" — *janarañjinī*. "Graceful" — *sughara*. "Shines" — *lasai*. "'How long [will you keep hiding]?' [He] says" — *rahata kata kahu*: the understanding is uncertain. "The friend with the friends" — *sakhī sakhika sañe*: Radha as "messenger", with the friends. "The Lord" — *nāha*, Krishna. "Vrinda" — Vrinda-devi, the goddess of the Vrindavana forest, who arranges the meetings of Radha and Krishna. "A lotus with a bee" — *madhupa saha susaroja*. "The tree entwines the creeper" — usually the creeper (Radha) entwines the tree (Krishna). "Holder of the mountain" — *dharādhara-dhara*: Krishna, who lifted Govardhana (*Bhag.* 10.25). The language of the padas about Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-54]: The headings are "কস্মিংশ্চ—" ("and on another [day]") and "দেশপাল", raga deshapala (cf. 17.22). The stanza begins and ends with "॥৫৩॥"; the lines are printed in pairs with gaps, but the stanza has one number. The story: Radha sits veiled in a kunja, and Vrinda passes her off to Krishna as the goddess of the nikunja. "Who is this" — *kebā*. "Uma, Rama, Rati" — Parvati, Lakshmi, the consort of Kamadeva (cf. 17.40). "This is she, the goddess of this nikunja" — faint in print, like *kahei hõi ei nikuñja-debī*; read as *kahe iṅhoi*: the reading is unclear. "Vidagdha" — "the Expert One", Krishna. "The darling of my heart" — *hiyāra putali*, literally "the doll of my heart". "Yugas" — ages. "Of a noble family" — *kulabatī*; on Radha's marriage as an arrangement of yogamaya, see note 41. "Most secret" — *atigūṛha*. "Dark One" — *kāliyā*, Krishna. "With every limb… every limb" — *prati aṅga diyā pūjo prati aṅga*. "The Moon-faced One" — *śaśimukhī*, Radha. "Under the veil" — *ghughaṭe*. "And again gives [Herself] away with a slight smile" — *se prakāśaye puna īṣata hāsi*: it may also mean "[but] she (Vrinda), smiling slightly, again gives [her] away" — the understanding is uncertain. "You do everything the other way round, you who are greedy for love-play" — *biparīta ratilampaṭa tumi*: Krishna was to worship the "goddess", but took her in His arms; *lampaṭa*, "greedy", cf. *rasa-lampaṭa* (17.47). "You see no fault in this" — *ithe doṣa nā māno*; the reply — *yābe doṣa tuyā paraśa pāyā*: the speaker is not named; by sense, Krishna, jesting with Lalita. "Narahari [and] the companions" — *narahari sahacarī*: the author as if among the friends. The language of the padas about Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-55]: The headings are "কস্মিংশ্চ—" ("and on another [day]") and "মায়ূর", raga mayura. The stanza begins and ends with "॥৫৪॥"; translated in full. The story: Radha comes to Krishna in the guise of Chandravali's messenger (on Chandravali, see note 43). "A little way off" — *kathodūre*. "Overwhelmed [with longing]" — *garagara*. "Kala" — Krishna. "Troubled" — *bimariṣa* (= *vimarṣa*). "Grave trouble" — Krishna fears that Chandravali's messenger will lead Him away from meeting Radha. "Seizes [His] feet" — *pāya dharaye*. "Overjoyed" — *parama paraśana* (= *prasanna*?). "Of mind (?)" — printed *meno*; probably *mano* (rhyming with *yeno*): the reading is unclear. "As if a serpent had bitten Him" — *sā̃pinī daṃśaye yeno*: such is the effect of Radha's touch on Krishna. "The jewel of the clever" — *sucatura-maṇi*. "Irresistibly" — *anibāra*. "Mallika creepers" — *malliballi*, jasmine. "Knitting her brows" — *bhrūbhaṅgi kari*. "Greedy for love-play" — *ratilampaṭa* (cf. note 54). Lalita's anger is in jest: she knows the "messenger" is Radha. "Ah, ah!" — *āhā mari*. "His hand like a tender shoot" — *kara-pallava*. "Fans with a chamara" — a whisk of yak tail; Narahari sees himself as a maidservant (manjari) in this pastime. The language of the padas about Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly, without softening. Signature: "Narahari".

[^17-56]: Marking — "ধানশী", raga dhanashi (cf. 17.20); printed without the word "raga". At the top of is the end of 17.54, translated in the previous portion. Two halves per line; here one line. The first line is the author's words; then Yamuna speaks. "Rai and Kanu" — *rāi kānu*: Radha and Krishna. "Suradhuni" — *suradhanī*, the Ganga (cf. note 45). "What joy was in her heart!" — *ki manera ullāse*. "Such are [Their] ways" — *enā rīta*: printed *enā*, probably = *ena* ("such"); reading unclear. "In the order of day and night" — *dibāniśi krame*: Yamuna begins her account of the ashtakaliya-lila of Radha and Krishna in Vraja — a parallel to Ganga's account of Gaura's eight periods of the day (17.4–17.38). 17.55 is the last number in the continuous count of the kirana before the internal numbering of the periods (see note 57).

[^17-57]: Markings — "প্রথমতো নিশান্তঃ—" ("first — nishanta"; Sanskrit; cf. the marking before 17.5, note 6) and "ভৈরব", raga bhairava (cf. 17.5). **Numbering.** Here the edition starts counting afresh: the nishanta stanzas are marked "॥১॥"–"॥৫॥", and after the fifth stands "॥৫॥৬০", that is, the 60th stanza of the kirana (55 + 5). There is no colophon of kirana 17 before this and no heading of a new kirana: it is an internal count of the period. The stanzas are therefore numbered by the kirana count, which the edition itself confirms: 17.56–17.60 = nishanta 1–5; 17.61–17.62 = morning 1–2 (see notes 61–63). This stanza is "॥১॥" in the edition. The plot of the Vraja nishanta-lila is close to Krishnadasa Kaviraja's *Govinda-lilamrita* (sarga 1): Vrinda wakes the Couple with the voices of birds, the warnings, Kakkhati, the exchange of garments, Mukhara and Jatila. "The nagara of the new nikunja" — *naba-nikuñja-nāgara*. "Breaker of [the gopis'] composure" — *dhṛti-bhañjana-kārī*. "Rasavati" — *rasabatī*, "She who is full of rasa": Radha (cf. note 52). "Intoxicated in the battle of rasa" — *rasa-samarete māti*: a figure from the language of the padas; on the kama of the gopis as pure prema see note 46; translated exactly. The parallel is the nishanta of Gaura and Vishnupriya (17.5–17.7); on it see note 3. "A dense mass of cloud entwined with lightning" — dark Krishna and golden Radha (cf. note 48). "Vrinda" — Vrinda-devi, goddess of the forest, arranger of the pastimes. "Display their skills" — *prakāśaye guṇa*. "Sharis, shukas" — *śārī śuka*: female and male parrot (in the pastimes, the birds of Radha and Krishna). "Kokilas" — *piku* (= *pika*). "On earth" — *mahimājhe* (= *mahī-mājhe*). "Drinking that honey" — *se madhu-pāne*. "In a happy rush of joy" — *subhaga-rabhase*. "Pressing face to lovely face" — *sucāru badane badana diyā*. "Reared by the Two" — *dõhākāra prati-pālita*. "As if steeped in nectar" — *janu amiyā mākhi*: the birds' speech. "The Holder of the mountain" — *dharādhara-dhāri*: Krishna, who lifted Govardhana. "Dhani" — *dhanī*, Radha. "Praises the shuka" — *śuke praśaṃsaye*. "Of the qualities of the Beloved" — *prāṇapiyā-guṇa*: apparently spoken by the shari. "The dear Ghanashyama" — *ghanaśyāma piya*: Krishna; the name is also the author's signature (cf. note 47).

[^17-58]: No raga marking. "॥২॥" in the edition. The bees speak — first to Krishna, then to the sakhis; "I know" (line 7) is the speech of one of them. "The bee and his mate" — *bhramarā bhramarī*.
