"Under the guise of… humming" — *guñjāra chale*. "Shyama-moon" — *śyāmaśaśi*. "At daybreak" — *bihānabele*. "There will be trouble" — *pramāda habe*. "Stain of disgrace" — *kalaṅka*. "Women of good families" — *kulabatī*. "Bring blame upon yourselves" — *doṣabhāgī haicha*. "Leaving love for her own husband, she has clung to another" — *chāṛi nija pati prīti para-sane paraśala*, literally "has touched with another": the bees speak the language of worldly gossip; Radha's marriage to Abhimanyu is an arrangement of yogamaya (see note 41). "The Dark One" — *kāliyā*: Krishna. "Shameless" — *ḍhiṭha*. "Victorious in Vraja" — *braje bijayī*. "Shame before [her] family" — *kula-lāja*. "Such are the qualities of Nanda's [son]" — *hena nandera guṇe*, literally "such are Nanda's qualities": by sense, Krishna's; understanding uncertain. "The cruel sister-in-law, [her] enemy" — *ari nanadī dāruṇa*: Kutila, Abhimanyu's sister (cf. note 52). "May no longer keep Radhika at home" — *pāche nā rākhe ghare*. "Fate has made [her] a forest-dweller" — *bihi banabāsī kaila*: the connection with the line and the understanding are uncertain. Signature: "Ghanashyama".

[^17-59]: No raga marking. The stanza begins and ends; "॥৩॥" in the edition. "With the corner of Her eye" — *ā̃khikoṇe*. "The Holder of the mountain" — *dharādhara-dhārī* (cf. note 57). "Pained that the touching of [Their] bodies is broken off" — *aṅga-saṅga-bhaṅge kātara*. "Sprout-like hand" — *kara-pallava*. "On His left thigh" — *nija bāma uru-pari* (cf. 17.54). "Loosened fine hair and dress" — *śithila sukeśa-beśa*. "Counting [it] great good fortune" — *kata sukṛti māni*. "The jewel of the clever" — *catura maṇi*: Krishna. "A plait that surpasses a serpent" — *phaṇi-jini beṇī*. "A dot of sindura in the parting" — *sĩthe sindura-bindu*. "Nose-ring" — *besara*. "Kunkuma" — *kuṅkuma*, saffron paste. "Musk" — *mṛgamada*. "The bodice on [Her] breast" — *kañcukī kuca-yuge race*: translated exactly. "Betel" — *tāmbūla*. "Placing mouth to mouth, taking [it]" — *mukhe mukha diyā laiyā*: Krishna takes the betel from Radha's mouth; understanding uncertain. "Radhe" — *rādhe*, vocative. "The edge of Her cloth" — *paṭāñcala*. "The dear Ghanashyama… embraces [Her] closely" — *ghana śyāma-piya … ghana āliṅgaye*: wordplay (*ghana* — "cloud" and "closely"), Ghanashyama's signature. The language of the padas on Radha and Krishna (see note 46); translated exactly, without softening.

[^17-60]: No raga marking. The stanza begins and ends; "॥৪॥" in the edition. "The Nectar-faced One" — *sudhāmukhī*: Radha. "Words of reproach" — *bhartsana bāṇī*. "The moon of [His] face is like the moon [with its mark]" — *mukhaśaśī śaśi-sama dekhi*: the kunkuma, musk and sindura from Radha's face have left their marks on Krishna's face (line 4; cf. 17.58) — like the mark on the moon; understanding uncertain. "Mirror" — *darapaṇa*. "Wipes away" — *põche*. "Countless moons" — printed *arbuda sarbabipati*; probably *śarbarīpati*, "lord of the night", the moon; *arbuda* — "a hundred million": reading unclear. "To the window" — *gabākṣe*. "Perfection of prema" — *prema-paripāṭi*. "Kakkhati" — *sukakkhaṭi* (*su-* — "good, worthy"): Jatila's old she-monkey; her cry "Jatila!" at dawn is the sign that it is time to part. "Under the guise of a cry" — *śabda-chale*. "They deliberate" — Radha and Krishna (or everyone; understanding uncertain). "Says by a sign" — *bhaṅgite kahe*. "Having exchanged garments" — *bastra-paribarta*: in Their haste Radha and Krishna put on Each Other's garments (cf. 17.61, 17.62). "Leave the bed" — printed *pari-baṅka* (or *-bakṣa*); probably *paryaṅka*, "bed": reading unclear. "Set out" — *gatipara*. "In the panchama note" — *pañcama gīti*: the fifth note, the voice of the kokila. "Vanjula" — *bañjula*, a tree (vetasa / ashoka). "Khanjanas" — *khañjana*, wagtails — a simile for restless eyes. "Lovely" — printed apparently *nañju*, read *mañju*. "In the play of Ananga" — *kuraṅga-anaṅga-raṅge*: Ananga is Kamadeva; said of the deer. Signature: "Ghanashyama".

[^17-61]: No raga marking. "॥৫॥৬০" in the edition — the fifth stanza of nishanta and the 60th stanza of the kirana (see note 57). "The sign from the corner of the Nectar-faced One's eye" — *sudhāmukhī ā̃khi-koṇe bhaṅgi*. "Hurry one another to go" — *paraspara gamana tvarā*. "Rupa, Rati, Rasa, Kasturi, Lavanga, Manjulali — the manjaris" — *rūpa-rati-rasa-kasturi-labaṅga-mañjulāli mañjarikādi*: Rupa-manjari, Rati-manjari, Rasa-manjari, Kasturi-manjari, Lavanga-manjari, Manjulali — Radha's young maidservants. "Water-jug" — printed *jharjharī*; probably *jhārī*, a water-jug: reading unclear. "The broken necklaces" — *chinna-hāra*. "The golden cage" — *svarṇa-piñjara*: the cage with the shari and the shuka (17.56). "Each on His and Her own way" — *nija nija gati pathe*. "From [Their] lotus eyes" — *bārijalocane*. "Touching the chin with a lotus hand, kissing" — *pāṇipadme paraśi cibuka cumbana kari*: who touches and kisses is not said; by sense, Krishna; understanding uncertain. "A companion" — *sahacarī*. "Meet… in dreams" — *svapane mile*. "At the time of nishanta" — *niśāntakāle*: the end of nishanta. Signature: "Ghanashyama". The language of the padas (see note 46); translated exactly.

[^17-62]: Marking — "বিভাষ", raga vibhasha (cf. 17.1). "॥১॥" in the edition — the first stanza of the morning (by the kirana count, 17.61; see note 57). The first word of the stanza — "প্রাতে" ("in the morning") — is set in bold and larger type: it is at the same time the marking of the period (cf. "first — nishanta" before 17.56); translated as part of the line. "Paurnamasi" — *pūrṇamāsī* / *paurṇamāsī*, the venerable ascetic of Vraja, arranger of the meetings of Radha and Krishna; in line 8 she is called "Bhagavati", in 17.62 "the Devi". "Yashomati" — Yashoda. "Kanu, [her] life" — *prāṇa kānu*. "The queen" — *rāṇī*: Yashoda. "On the moon-like bed" — *śaśikalpa-talpa* (*talpa* unclear in print). "Feigning sleep, stretches His body a little" — *kapaṭe nidrāte kiñcit moṛaye tanu*. "An emerald-dark creeper, the colour of dense collyrium" — *marakatatama ghanāñjana latā*: understanding uncertain. "The blue cloth" — *nīla basana*: Radha's cloth, put on by Krishna in the exchange (17.59). "The feet of both" — *duhũ pada*: of Yashoda and Paurnamasi. "To where the white cows are" — *yathā dhabalī-baṭā*: *dhabalī* — "white cow"; *baṭā* (= *bāṭā*, "pen"?) — understanding uncertain. "The knower of fun" — *kautuka-kobida*. "Subala" — Krishna's friend (cf. note 52). "The milking pail and the hobbling rope" — *dohanī chādana ḍora*: the rope with which a cow's legs are hobbled for milking. "The dear Ghanashyama" — *ghana-śyāmapiya* (the first letter in print also resembles "ব"): Krishna, and the author's signature.

[^17-63]: Marking — "বিভাষ", raga vibhasha. The stanza begins and ends; "॥২॥" in the edition (by the kirana count, 17.62; see note 57). "The moon-faced [son] of Vraja" — *braja-śaśimukha*. "Treasure of rasa" — *rasa-rāśi*. "The queen of Vraja" — *brajeśvarī*: Yashoda. "When night turns to dawn" — *rāti-prabhātakāle*. "Mukhara" — an elderly gopi, Radha's grandmother; "granddaughter" — *nātinī*. "Simple-minded" — *saralā*. "Mother of Abhimanyu" — Jatila, Radha's mother-in-law; on Radha's marriage as an arrangement of yogamaya see note 41. "[Jatila]… tells Mukhara" — the subject is not named; understanding uncertain. "To worship the sun" — *pūjite rabi*: the pretext under which Radha leaves home during the day. "The Devi" — Paurnamasi. "So that misfortune may be removed from the cows and wealth" — *dhenudhana yāte aśubha hare* (cf. 17.49). "Bedchamber" — *śayana-bhabana*. "The worship of Bhanu" — *bhānupūjā*: Bhanu is the Sun (Surya). "Yellow cloth" — *pītabasana*: Krishna's cloth, put on by Radha in the exchange (17.59). "The glow of the red rays [of dawn]" — *aruṇa kiraṇa-chale*. "A sakhi" — *sakhī*. "Showing her merriment" — *prakāśi raṅga*. "The bed" — *śeja*. "Narahari remains, offering himself [as a ransom]" — *narahari nichani raha*: *nichani* — the custom of waving something around the beloved and giving it away as a token of love, "to give oneself for [Them]"; signature: "Narahari". Below the stanza is the sign "*" and the editor's footnote: "আগরতলা পুঁথিতে এই পর্য্যন্ত পাওয়া গিয়াছে" ("in the Agartala manuscript [the text] was found up to this point"); it is not translated as text of the poem but rendered by the translator's italic line. Then a short rule; there is no colophon of kirana 17: the manuscript breaks off at the morning (pratah) of Yamuna's account.
